# Codeqlick — Architecture

> *Redefining information technology in society*

This document is the source of truth for how the Codeqlick platform is built. Both apps follow it; change it in the same pull request as any change that breaks it.

## 1. Overview

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  B["Browser"] -->|HTTPS| N["Nginx<br>codeqlick.com"]
  N --> W["codeqlick-frontend<br>Next.js 16 (SSR + BFF)"]
  W -->|"REST /v1 + Bearer token<br>(server-to-server)"| A["codeqlick-backend<br>NestJS 12 API"]
  A --> P[("PostgreSQL 18")]
  A --> M["SMTP (OVH)"]
  N2["Nginx<br>api.codeqlick.com"] --> A
  W -->|"/api/stats"| U["Umami<br>(analytics)"]
  N3["Nginx<br>client sites"] --> S["Static files<br>/srv/codeqlick/sites"]
  S -.->|"contact form"| N2
```

Two independent deployables in one repository:

| Directory | What it is | Runs on |
|---|---|---|
| `codeqlick-frontend/` | Public website (EN/ES), blog, portfolio, contact form, client portal and admin panel | Node 24, port 3000 |
| `codeqlick-sites/` | The basic-website template (`web-basica`) and the service that builds managed client sites from it | Node 24, internal port 4100 |
| `codeqlick-backend/` | REST API: auth, leads, blog, portfolio, client projects (updates, files, tasks, hours, briefs, reviews, access, monitoring), proposals, support, bookings, settings, users | Node 24, port 4000 |
| `codeqlick-cli/` | The `codeqlick` command of Codeqlick Cloud (section 4.13): sign-in, projects, deployments, domains; published to npm as `@codeqlick/cli` | Node 24 on developers' machines and CI |

Client websites hosted by Codeqlick are static sites next to them (section 8.2).

Cloud's public documentation (section 4.13) is a third static site, `codeqlick-docs/` (Astro, no server of its own): it is built and hosted by Cloud itself at docs.codeqlick.com.

Developers' static sites on Codeqlick Cloud are served the same way, from `/srv/codeqlick/cloud`, under their own domain (section 8.3).

They share **no code**. The contract between them is the HTTP API (section 5), documented live with OpenAPI at `/docs` in non-production environments.

## 2. Principles

1. **The backend owns data and rules.** The frontend never talks to the database and never re-implements validation it can get from the API (it validates for UX, the API validates for truth).
2. **Backend-for-frontend (BFF).** The browser only talks to `codeqlick.com`. Next.js server components, server actions and the proxy call the API server-to-server. Tokens never reach browser JavaScript.
3. **Secure by default.** Every API route requires authentication unless explicitly marked `@Public()`. Admin routes additionally require the `ADMIN` role.
4. **Validate at the edge.** Every request body, query and param is parsed with a Zod schema (NestJS 12 Standard Schema validation). Environment variables are validated at boot; the app refuses to start with bad config.
5. **Modules by domain, layers inside.** Each backend module owns its controller (HTTP), service (use cases), repository (persistence) and schemas.
6. **Exact dependency versions.** No `^` or `~` in any `package.json`. Upgrades are deliberate pull requests.
7. **Boring infrastructure.** PostgreSQL, Docker Compose, Nginx. No managed services required to run it.

## 3. Tech stack

| Concern | Choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Runtime | Node.js 24 LTS | Both apps, Docker `node:24-alpine` |
| Language | TypeScript 7.0 (API and website); 6.0 in the site template | TS 7.0 has only the `tsc` executable: the API compiles and watches with it; `astro check` still needs 6.0 (decision 19) |
| Backend framework | NestJS 12 (ESM) | `"type": "module"`, NodeNext resolution |
| Validation | Zod 4 via Standard Schema | `@Body({ schema })` + `StandardSchemaValidationPipe` |
| ORM | Prisma 7 + `@prisma/adapter-pg` | Generated client in `src/generated/prisma` |
| Database | PostgreSQL 18 | |
| Auth | JWT access token (15 min) + rotating refresh token (30 days) | Passwords hashed with Argon2id |
| API docs | `@nestjs/swagger` 12 | Zod → OpenAPI via `z.toJSONSchema` |
| Logging | `nestjs-pino` | JSON logs in production, pretty in development, request IDs |
| Rate limiting | `@nestjs/throttler` | 100 requests a minute per IP for everything; every public route that changes something (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE without a session) has its own stricter `@Throttle`, enforced by a unit test (`common/throttle-coverage.spec.ts`, decision 109) |
| Health | `@nestjs/terminus` | `GET /v1/health` checks the database |
| Email | Nodemailer (SMTP) | Mailpit in development |
| Backend tests | Vitest + `unplugin-swc` + Supertest | |
| Frontend tests | Vitest (node) | Unit tests next to the code (`src/**/*.test.ts(x)`): helpers, form schemas and plain components rendered with `react-dom/server`. Pages and Server Actions are checked in a browser |
| Frontend framework | Next.js 16 (App Router) + React 19 | `proxy.ts` (formerly middleware) |
| i18n | `next-intl` 4 | `/en/...` and `/es/...`, English default |
| Styling | CSS Modules + design tokens | Brand tokens in `src/styles/tokens.css` |
| Fonts | `next/font/local` | Latin subset of each variable font (OFL, from Fontsource) in `src/assets/fonts`, served from our domain; neither builds nor visitors reach Google |
| Lint / format | oxlint + Prettier | Both apps |
| Browser tests | Playwright (Chromium) | `codeqlick-frontend/e2e`: public pages, the contact form, and the portal flow (client comments and approves, the team answers), against the whole stack. The E2E workflow builds it from the commit; locally they run against the Docker dev stack (`npm run test:e2e`) |
| CI | GitHub Actions | Lint, typecheck, test, build per app; E2E browser tests; deploy to the VPS once all of a commit's CI has passed |

## 4. Backend (`codeqlick-backend`)

### 4.1 Folder structure

```
src/
  main.ts                    Bootstrap: app.setup.ts (helmet, CORS, global pipe/filter, shutdown hooks), Swagger outside production
  app.module.ts              Wires all modules, global guards (throttler, JWT auth, roles)
  config/
    env.schema.ts            Zod schema for process.env
    app-config.service.ts    Typed accessors over ConfigService
  common/
    decorators/              @Public(), @Roles(), @CurrentUser()
    guards/                  JwtAuthGuard (global), RolesGuard (global)
    filters/                 ProblemDetailsFilter (RFC 9457 error bodies)
    schemas/                 Shared Zod schemas: pagination, locale, id params, slug
    types/                   AuthUser, Paginated<T>
  prisma/                    PrismaModule + PrismaService (adapter-pg, connect/disconnect)
  generated/prisma/          Prisma client output (git-ignored, created by `prisma generate`)
  modules/
    health/
    auth/                    login, refresh (rotation + reuse detection), logout, me, two-step verification, sessions
    accounts/                Client companies: `accountOf()` and the people of an account
    activity/                The activity log (who changed what)
    users/                   Admin management of admin/client accounts
    mail/                    MailService abstraction over Nodemailer; email-layout.ts renders every email as plain text plus branded HTML
    leads/                   Public contact form + admin pipeline
    posts/                   Blog (EN/ES translations)
    newsletter/              Blog newsletter: double opt-in sign-up, one-click unsubscribe, each new post emailed once (section 4.14)
    portfolio/               Case studies (EN/ES translations)
    link-checks/             Broken links in published posts and case studies, checked weekly (section 4.6; the last run's time is a `settings` row)
    projects/                Client projects + progress updates (admin writes, client reads own)
    tasks/                   Project tasks: from support requests or planned; the client sees titles and statuses; the release key reads and updates them
    time/                    Time tracking per project and maintenance plans (monthly hour allowances) (admin logs it; the client sees it when the project shows it)
    bookings/                Calls booked from the website (free slots in Madrid time, iCalendar invitations)
    citas/                   Citas: people's booking pages and their appointments (section 4.9)
    swipe/                   Swipe 18+: adults' intimate photos rated by swiping, matches and their chat, moderation and removal requests (section 4.10)
    cruising/                Cruising 18+: worldwide directory of cruising spots on a map with member reviews, then a members' social network; moderation and removal requests (section 4.15)
    calculator/              Cost calculator prices (a `settings` row)
    reviews/                 Client reviews: asked for on launch, published on the site with consent
    credentials/             Access clients share for their projects (encrypted; the team reveals, logged)
    monitoring/              Uptime monitoring of hosted client sites (checks every 5 minutes, alerts, status in the portal)
    errors/                  Unexpected errors of the API and the website, grouped, with an email per new one (section 4.11)
    site-audits/             Free website check: a visitor's site analysed (speed, mobile, Google, security) and the report emailed (section 4.12)
    cloud/                   Codeqlick Cloud (section 4.13): teams, members, API tokens and CLI sign-in, projects, static deployments, custom domains, events, abuse reports
    site-forms/              Contact forms of hosted static client sites, relayed to the client's inbox
    domains/                 Client domains: availability, OVH (DNS and expiry) and renewal reminders
    managed-sites/           Managed basic websites: content, revisions, builds through site-builder
    briefs/                  The project brief questionnaire (portal form, admin view, release-key read)
    proposals/               Proposals and quotes: drafted and sent from the admin panel, accepted or declined in the portal
    support/                 Support requests: clients open and answer them in the portal, the team in the admin panel
    media/                   Image uploads (admin) stored on disk and served publicly
    saved-replies/           Reusable texts for lead replies and project comments
    stats/                   Admin dashboard numbers, the platform overview (section 4.17) and the public site's visits (Umami)
    search/                  Admin search across leads, users, projects, requests, proposals, posts and case studies (section 4.14)
prisma/
  schema.prisma
  migrations/
  seed.ts                    Creates the first admin from env vars
prisma.config.ts
test/                        e2e tests (Supertest)
```

Inside each module:

```
modules/posts/
  posts.module.ts
  posts.controller.ts          Public routes
  admin-posts.controller.ts    Admin routes (@Roles('ADMIN'))
  posts.service.ts             Use cases; throws Nest HTTP exceptions
  posts.repository.ts          The only file that touches Prisma for this domain
  posts.schemas.ts             Zod request schemas + inferred types
  posts.service.spec.ts        Unit tests with a mocked repository
```

Dependency direction: **controller → service → repository → PrismaService**. Never skip a layer, never go backwards. The `media` module has no database table: its `media.storage.ts` plays the repository's role over the file system, and `media.repository.ts` only reads which posts and case studies use an image. Likewise `search.repository.ts` (section 4.14) only reads other domains' tables, never writes them.

### 4.2 Data model

```mermaid
erDiagram
  User ||--o{ Session : has
  User ||--o{ PasswordResetToken : has
  User ||--o{ EmailChangeToken : has
  User ||--o{ ClientProject : "owns (client)"
  User ||--o{ Post : authors
  ClientProject ||--o{ ProjectUpdate : has
  ClientProject ||--o{ ProjectFile : has
  ClientProject ||--o{ ProjectInvoice : bills
  ClientProject ||--o{ ProjectMilestone : has
  ProjectUpdate ||--o{ ProjectUpdateComment : has
  Post ||--|{ PostTranslation : "en / es"
  Post ||--o{ PostRevision : "last 20"
  PortfolioItem ||--|{ PortfolioTranslation : "en / es"
  Post ||--o{ PostSlugRedirect : "old slugs"
  PortfolioItem ||--o{ PortfolioSlugRedirect : "old slugs"
  PortfolioItem ||--o{ PortfolioRevision : "last 20"
  Lead ||--o{ LeadReply : has
```

- **User** — `email` (unique, lower-cased), `passwordHash` (Argon2id), `name`, `role` (`ADMIN` | `CLIENT`), `isActive`, `locale` (`EN` | `ES`, default `EN`: language of the emails the platform sends them), `weeklyDigest` / `lastDigestAt` (the weekly summary), `monthlyReport` (default true: the monthly website report, section 8.2; both are optional emails with a one-click unsubscribe link, see "Unsubscribe links" in section 4.3), `registeredAt?` (decision 17: when the person created the account themselves or accepted the team's invitation; null for a client account the team prepared for an email — a proposal, a project — which nobody can sign in to until its owner registers with that email), `username?` (unique, lower-cased; see "Username" under section 6, "My account"), `avatarKey?` (file name of the profile photo, see "One profile" under section 6), `signupApp?` (decision 29: the web app — `videochat`, `swipe`, `cruising`, `meetings`, `citas` — the person signed up from; null on codeqlick.com or for accounts the team created). `SignupToken.app?` carries it from `/auth/register` until the email is confirmed.
- **Client companies:** a CLIENT user with `memberOfId` is a person of the company whose main client account is `memberOf` (one level: a member can't have members, and projects and proposals must belong to a main account). The main account owns projects, requests and proposals; its active members see and act on all of them (the portal checks the caller's *account*: `accountOf(user)`, carried in the access token as `acc`), and the client emails (updates, comments, replies, status changes, proposals, weekly summary) go to the owner and every active member, each in their language. What a member writes (messages, comments, approvals) is signed with their own name, and team notifications reply to them.
- **Session** — one row per refresh token: `tokenHash` (SHA-256, unique), `familyId`, `expiresAt`, `revokedAt`, `replacedById`, `userAgent`, `ip`.
- **PasswordResetToken** — one row per "forgot my password" email or welcome email: `tokenHash` (SHA-256, unique), `expiresAt` (60 minutes for a reset, 7 days for a welcome email), `usedAt`. Asking again deletes the user's unused tokens, so only the latest link works. An unregistered client's invitation to create their account is one of these too (7 days). **SignupToken** — a self-registration waiting for its email to be confirmed: `email`, `name`, `passwordHash`, `locale`, `tokenHash` (unique), `expiresAt` (24 hours), `usedAt`. **EmailChangeToken** — a change of the account's email waiting for the new address to confirm it: `newEmail`, `tokenHash` (unique), `expiresAt` (24 hours), `usedAt`; asking again replaces the user's pending one.
- **Lead** — contact form submission, or a business the team added itself (`createdById?`: who; a prospect, section 8.2): `name`, `email?` (always set from the forms; a prospect may have none until the team adds it), `company?`, `projectType?` (what they need: `web`, `custom`, `mobile`, `design`, `support`, `not-sure`), `budget?` (only older leads: the form asked for a range before), `message`, `locale`, `status` (the sales process from the wiki's Client Engagement: `NEW` → `TALKING` → `CALL` → `PROPOSAL` → `WON` / `LOST`; `SPAM` is not a stage and stays off the board), `lostReason?` (`PRICE`, `NO_RESPONSE`, `NOT_A_FIT`, `CHOSE_OTHER`, `TIMING`, `OTHER`; kept only while LOST), `followUpOn?` (date), `clientId?` (the account it became), `notes?`, `siteRequest?` (JSON `{ preset, business, domain? }`: when it came from the basic-website request, which onboarding then builds the site from), and its instant preview: `previewSlug?` (unique), `previewStatus?` (`BUILDING` | `READY` | `FAILED` | `REMOVED`), `previewBuiltAt?`, `previewFollowUpAt?` (when the follow-up about it was emailed; never for a prospect) (decision 16, section 8.2), and `proposalId?` (the proposal drafted from the request, `POST /admin/leads/:id/proposal`; null again if it is deleted).
- **SitePurchase** — a request's preview bought online (section 8.2, decision 20): `leadId`, `stripeSessionId` (unique, the Stripe Checkout Session), `amountCents` (the total charged, VAT included), `currency` (`eur`), `status` (`PENDING` → `PAID` | `EXPIRED`), `email?` (the buyer's, from Stripe), `paidAt?`, `projectId?` (the project of the site made from it), `stripeInvoiceId?` (the invoice Stripe issued for it), `error?` (why the site couldn't be made on its own; the team finishes it).
- **LeadReply** — an email answer sent to a lead from the admin panel: `leadId`, `authorId?` (null once the user is deleted), `authorName` copied at the time, `subject`, `body`. Only stored once the email was accepted by the SMTP server.
- **Post / PostTranslation** — `slug` (unique), `status` (`DRAFT` | `PUBLISHED`), `publishedAt`, `coverImageUrl?`, `contentUpdatedAt?` (below), `newsletter` (default true: email it to the newsletter's subscribers, section 4.14), `newsletterSentAt?` (when its email went out, or was skipped; null until the post is live and the job has seen it); translation per `locale` with `title`, `excerpt`, `content` (Markdown).
- **NewsletterSubscriber** — a blog reader who signed up for new posts by email (section 4.14), not linked to `User`: `email` (unique, lower-cased), `locale` (`EN` | `ES`: the language of the emails), `status` (`PENDING` | `ACTIVE` | `UNSUBSCRIBED`), `confirmTokenHash?` (SHA-256, unique) and `confirmExpiresAt?` (48 hours), `confirmSentAt?` (the last confirmation email), `confirmedAt?`, `unsubscribeToken` (32 random bytes, base64url, unique; kept in clear because it goes in every email and can only unsubscribe), `unsubscribedAt?`, `createdAt`.
- **Tag / PostTag** — blog topics shared by both languages: `slug` (unique, used in `/blog?tag=`), `nameEn`, `nameEs`; `PostTag` joins posts and tags (both sides cascade, so deleting a tag just removes it from its posts). Up to 10 tags per post.
- **Scheduling (posts and case studies):** content is live when `status = PUBLISHED` and `publishedAt <= now`. A published item dated in the future is *scheduled*: the public API (lists, detail, so also the sitemap and RSS) hides it until then and it appears on its own, with no job to run, within the public pages' 5-minute revalidation. `publishedAt` is set on the first publish unless one is given; clearing it on a published item means now. The admin panel types it in Madrid time.
- **IndexNow (posts and case studies; roadmap item proposed by the agent team, 2026-11-14; decision 74):** when content changes, the API tells search engines that support IndexNow (Bing, Yandex, Seznam, Naver, Yep) at once instead of waiting for their crawlers. Off unless `INDEXNOW_ENABLED=true` (4.5).
  - **Key:** 32 hex characters, the start of HMAC-SHA256 of `JWT_ACCESS_SECRET` with the purpose `indexnow-v1`, so it needs no setting of its own and stays the same across restarts. `GET /v1/indexnow/key` hands it to the website (404 while off), which serves it as plain text at `/indexnow-key.txt`; submissions name that file as `keyLocation`.
  - **When:** after a post or case study is saved, when it is live before or after the change (created live, published, edited while live, a revision restored while live (decision 72), unpublished, made a draft, deleted, its slug changed). A scheduled item is announced when its date comes: every 5 minutes (`IndexNowService`, 4.6) the API submits the posts and case studies that went live in the last 10 minutes.
  - **What:** the item's addresses in both languages (`<SITE_URL>/en/blog/<slug>` and `/es/blog/<slug>`, or `/en/work/<slug>` and `/es/work/<slug>`), the old ones too after a slug change, and the list page in both languages (`/en/blog` and `/es/blog`, or `/en/work` and `/es/work`). One `POST https://api.indexnow.org/indexnow` with JSON `{ host, key, keyLocation, urlList }` (`host` from `SITE_URL`), 10-second timeout.
  - **Limits:** an address already submitted in the last 10 minutes is left out (kept in memory); nothing is sent when no address is left. The submission runs after the change is saved and never fails or delays it: a failure (network, a status other than 200 or 202) is logged as a warning and not retried, since the sitemap still lists the content.
- **Updated date (posts and case studies):** `contentUpdatedAt` (null until set) is when the text last really changed after going live. A save (an edit, or restoring a revision, decision 72) sets it to now when the item was live before it (published, `publishedAt <= now`), stays published, and the readers' content changed: a translation's title, excerpt (summary for case studies) or body, a translation added or removed, or the cover image. Slug, tags, status, date, sort order, services, technologies, client, link and testimonial changes don't move it, and neither does publishing again after unpublishing. Existing rows start null (no backfill: old `updatedAt` values don't say what changed). The public views give it as `contentUpdatedAt` only when it is at least 24 hours after `publishedAt` (a fix on launch day is not an update); otherwise `null`.
- **PortfolioItem / PortfolioTranslation** — same publishing model (including `contentUpdatedAt?`) plus `clientName?`, `projectUrl?`, `technologies[]`, `services[]` (the website's service keys: web, custom, mobile, design, consulting, support), `sortOrder`, `testimonialAuthor?`; translation has `title`, `summary`, `content`, `testimonialQuote?`, `testimonialRole?`. A case study has a testimonial in a language when it has an author and a quote in that language; it is shown on the case study and on the home page.
- **PostSlugRedirect / PortfolioSlugRedirect** — old addresses of a post or case study after a slug change (decision 71): `slug` (unique), `postId` (or `portfolioItemId`; cascade delete), `createdAt`. A slug is editable in the admin panel; when an item that has a publication date (`publishedAt` set before the save: published now or before, or scheduled) gets a new slug, its old slug is stored pointing to the item. A draft that was never published (no `publishedAt`) leaves nothing behind. A redirect points to the item, not to a slug, so renames never chain: after A → B → C, both A and B lead to C. In the same transaction as the save, giving any item (new or edited) a slug that is a stored redirect deletes that redirect (the new owner wins), and renaming an item back to one of its old slugs deletes that row. Nothing else changes: the activity log records the save as the usual update.
- **PostRevision / PortfolioRevision** — revision history of posts and case studies (decision 72): `postId` / `portfolioItemId` (cascade), `authorId?` (who made the change that replaced it; null once the user is deleted), `createdAt` (when it was replaced), `snapshot` (JSON: the item's content as it was until then). A post's snapshot is `{ coverImageUrl, tagIds, translations: [{ locale, title, excerpt, content }] }`; a case study's is `{ coverImageUrl, clientName, projectUrl, technologies, services, testimonialAuthor, translations: [{ locale, title, summary, content, testimonialQuote, testimonialRole }] }`. Publishing fields (`status`, `publishedAt`), `slug` and the case study's `sortOrder` are not content and are never part of it. Every admin update (`PATCH`) whose snapshot differs from the current one stores the current one as a revision in the same transaction as the change (tag ids and translations compared without regard to order); creating an item stores nothing, nor does a PATCH that changes nothing or only those other fields. The last 20 per item are kept: older ones are deleted in the same transaction. Images used only by a revision don't count as used in the media library.
- **ClientProject** — `clientId`, `name`, `description?`, `phase` (`DISCOVERY` | `DESIGN` | `DEVELOPMENT` | `QA` | `LAUNCHED` | `SUPPORT` | `ON_HOLD`), `progress` (0–100), `startDate?`, `dueDate?`, `stagingUrl?`, `productionUrl?`, `showHours` (the client sees the hours logged on it; off by default), and its watched domain: `domainName?`, `domainSource` (`RDAP` | `MANUAL` | `OVH`), `domainExpiresAt?` (date), `domainCheckedAt?`, `domainWarnedFor?` (`<day>:<30|7>`, the last reminder). With `RDAP` the expiry is read from the registry's RDAP server (found in IANA's bootstrap, `data.iana.org/rdap/dns.json`); TLDs without RDAP (`.es`) take a date typed by the team. A domain the team's OVH account manages (the client made it the technical contact, decision 15) switches to `OVH` on its next check: the expiry is OVH's (`GET /domain/{name}/serviceInfos`), `.es` included; one OVH stops managing goes back to `RDAP`. 30 and 7 days before it, the team inbox and every person of the client account are emailed, once each per expiry date. A managed site's own domain becomes the project's watched domain when it has none. Its maintenance plan (section 8.2, decision 110): `maintenanceStatus` (`NONE` | `PENDING` | `ACTIVE` | `PAST_DUE` | `CANCELED`, default `NONE`, from the Stripe subscription), `maintenanceSubscriptionStatus?` (Stripe's own status), `maintenanceSubscriptionId?` and `maintenanceCustomerId?` (unique), `maintenancePeriodEnd?`, `maintenanceCancelAtPeriodEnd` (default false), `maintenanceSince?` (when it last turned on), `maintenanceIncludedReason?` and `maintenanceIncludedAt?` (given for free by the team). The plan is on while `ACTIVE`, `PAST_DUE` or included.
- **ProjectUpdate** — `title`, `body` (Markdown), `authorId`, `createdAt`, `approvalRequested` (the team asks the client to approve it), `approvedAt?`, `approvedById?`. A **release** is an update with `version`, `environment` (`STAGING` | `PRODUCTION`) and `url?`: the version deployed, where, and where to try it; posting one also sets the project's `stagingUrl` or `productionUrl` to that URL. A project may have a release key (`releaseTokenHash`, SHA-256 of `cqr_…`, and `releaseTokenCreatedAt`) for its deploy pipeline.
- **ProjectUpdateComment** — a comment on an update by the client or the team: `body` (plain text, max 5000), `authorId?`, `createdAt`. Comments by an ADMIN are shown as the team's.
- **ProjectMilestone** — a step of the project shown to the client as a timeline: `title`, `description?` (plain text, max 2000), `dueDate?`, `completedAt?` (set when the admin marks it done; null while open), `position` (order set by the admin, 0-based). The first milestone not done is the "next" one.
- **ProjectFile** — a file shared with the project's client: `name` (original name, cleaned), `contentType` (as sent by the uploader; informational), `size`, `uploadedById?`. The content is stored on disk at `UPLOADS_DIR/project-files/<id>`, never under a public URL.
- **ProjectInvoice** — an invoice the team issued for a project (decision 23), as the PDF made by the team's invoicing tool: `number` (unique, the invoice's own number), `issuedOn`, `dueOn?` (dates), `amountCents` (the total, VAT included), `currency` (`eur`), `paidAt?` (set by the team), `fileName`, `size`, `uploadedById?`. The PDF is stored at `UPLOADS_DIR/invoices/<id>`, never under a public URL. Its status is derived: `PAID` with `paidAt`, `OVERDUE` once `dueOn` has passed, `DUE` otherwise.
- **SupportTicket** — a request a client opens from the portal: `number` (sequential, shown as `#12`), `clientId`, `projectId?` (one of the client's projects), `type` (`BUG` | `CHANGE` | `QUESTION`), `priority` (`LOW` | `NORMAL` | `HIGH` | `URGENT`), `status` (`OPEN` | `IN_PROGRESS` | `WAITING_CLIENT` | `RESOLVED` | `CLOSED`), `title`, `resolvedAt?` (set when it becomes RESOLVED or CLOSED, cleared when it is reopened), `maintenanceIncluded?` (a `CHANGE` request opened on a project whose maintenance plan is on: `true` when it was one of the month's 3 included changes, `false` past them; null otherwise; section 8.2).
- **TicketMessage** — the request's conversation, oldest first: `body` (plain text, max 20 000), `authorId?`. The first message is the client's description; messages by an ADMIN are shown as the team's.
- **TicketAttachment** — a file added to a support request by the client or the team: `name`, `contentType`, `size`, `uploadedById?`. Stored on disk at `UPLOADS_DIR/ticket-files/<id>`, like project files.
- **Proposal** — a proposal or quote for a client: `number` (sequential, `#7`), `clientId`, `projectId?` (one of the client's projects), `createdById?`, `title`, `summary` (Markdown: scope), `terms?` (Markdown: payment, deadlines), `currency` (`EUR`), `validUntil?` (date; last day it can be accepted, Madrid time), `status` (`DRAFT` | `SENT` | `ACCEPTED` | `DECLINED` | `WITHDRAWN`), `sentAt?`, `respondedAt?`, `acceptedName?` (the full name the client typed to accept it), `declineReason?`. `expiryRemindedAt?`: when the reminder that it is about to expire went out (section 4.6). Only drafts can be edited or deleted; a sent one gets a new version by duplicating it. Nothing is charged: accepting is a recorded agreement.
- **ProposalTemplate** — a starting point for new proposals ("Web básica"): `name` (unique), `title`, `summary`, `terms?`, `validDays?` (a proposal made from it is valid that many days from the day it is created), `items` (JSON `[{ description, quantity, unitPrice }]`, cents). Admin → Propuestas → Nueva offers them; any proposal can be saved as one.
- **ProposalItem** — a line of a proposal: `position`, `description`, `quantity` (whole units), `unitPrice` (cents). The total is computed, never stored.
- **UpdateDraft** — an update sent with a project's release key and waiting for the team's review: `title`, `body` (Markdown), `version?`, `environment?`, `url?`. The client never sees it; publishing creates the ProjectUpdate (a release when it has a version) from the reviewed text and deletes the draft.
- **MaintenancePlan** — a monthly hour allowance for a project (one per project): `active`, `name`, `monthlyMinutes`, `alertPercent` (default 80), `alertedMonth`/`alertedLevel`. Its time entries draw it down per Madrid calendar month; logging time that crosses the threshold or 100 % emails the team inbox, once a month per level. The client sees the use while it is active. Nothing is billed.
- **SiteForm** — the contact form of a static client site (one per project): public `key` (it goes in the site's HTML), `enabled`, `recipients` (1–5 emails), `submissions` counter, `lastSubmissionAt`. Messages are emailed; when the project has a managed site (section 8.2) a copy is also kept as a **SiteFormMessage** — `formId`, `name`, `email`, `phone?`, `message`, `createdAt` — for the client to read in the portal, deleted after 12 months (or by the client). Other sites' messages are only emailed. **SiteFormDay** — `formId`, `day` (Madrid), `count`: messages per day, for the visits chart.
- **ManagedSite** — a basic website built from content stored here (one per project, section 8.2): `slug` (unique: folder and preview subdomain `<slug>.<SITES_PREVIEW_DOMAIN>`), `preset` (the demo it started from; its images are usable by name), `content` (JSON: the template's `Site` type without `url`, `form` and `demo`, validated by `siteContentSchema`; uploaded photos are `media/<file>`), `domain?` (the client's own, unique; never under `SITES_PREVIEW_DOMAIN` or the platform's own domain), `domainState?` (`UNREGISTERED`: nobody has it; `ELSEWHERE`: registered but not resolving to `SITES_SERVER_IP` or not answering over HTTPS yet; `LIVE`: it serves the site — null without a domain or before its first check), `domainCheckedAt?`, `mailState?` (where the domain's email goes, from its MX records: `NONE`, `OVH` — `*.ovh.net` — or `OTHER`; checked with the domain, null while unregistered), `status` (`NONE` | `BUILDING` | `LIVE` | `FAILED`), `lastBuildAt?`, `lastBuildError?` (the end of the build log), `release?`, `reportedMonth?` (`yyyy-mm`: the last month its monthly report went out for), `umamiWebsiteId?` (its website in Umami, created on its first build when Umami is set up; the build then adds the cookieless tracker, `UMAMI_SCRIPT_URL`, and the template's privacy and cookie pages mention it). Creating one starts from the preset with the project's name and the client as legal owner, creates the project's site form when missing and sets the project's `stagingUrl` (preview) and `productionUrl` (the domain once LIVE, the preview until then). A domain is published as itself and `www.` (the builder's `domains`). Each check connects it through OVH when the team manages it: its zone's apex and `www` `A`/`AAAA`/`CNAME` records are replaced by one `A` to `SITES_SERVER_IP` unless they already point there (other records, such as MX, are never touched; each change is in the activity log). Turning LIVE rebuilds the site on the domain, turns on its uptime monitor and emails the client; a LIVE domain that still resolves here stays LIVE when HTTPS fails for a moment. Every change is built at once (`url`, `demo: false` and the form's key added); a failed build keeps the previous release online. A site may have a **draft** the client is editing (decision 18): `draftContent?` (the whole content, valid against `siteContentSchema`), `draftPreset?` (the style it starts from, when the client changed it), `draftUpdatedAt?` and `draftKey?` (random, the address of its preview: `/site-draft/<key>/`); publishing it replaces `content` (and `preset`) and clears it. **ManagedSiteRevision** — the content before each change (`authorId?`), the last 20 kept. `newsEnabled` (default false): the client has switched on the site's news section (section 8.2); like `menuEnabled`, a column of its own, not part of `content`, so switching it never touches the draft. `newsBuiltAt?`: when the last successful build started (the posts it shows are those not expired by then); a failed build leaves it, so the hourly job retries. **SiteNewsPost** — a news item or offer of a managed site (section 8.2, decision 55), outside `content` and its draft/publish cycle: `siteId`, `slug` (unique per site; made from the title when the post is created and never changed afterwards, so its address is stable; a slug that is only digits gets a suffix), `title` (≤ 120), `summary` (≤ 300; the list, Google and social previews), `body` (plain text, ≤ 10 000; a Markdown subset, see section 8.2), `photo?` (`media/<file>`, from the site's photo uploads), `publishedAt?` (null = draft; set when published, and kept if the post is edited), `expiresAt?` (end of a Madrid day, for offers: after it the post is hidden from the site), `authorId?`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. Up to 200 per site. A post's state is derived: `DRAFT` (no `publishedAt`), `PUBLISHED` or `EXPIRED` (`expiresAt` in the past). Posts are not in `ManagedSiteRevision`; they cascade with the site, and their photos stay in the media library. English twins (decision 106): `titleEn?`, `summaryEn?`, `bodyEn?` (same limits; empty falls back to the Spanish text on the English pages). A site's English version lives in its content (`english`, `en`, section 8.2), not in a column, so it follows the draft.
- **ClientReview** — the client's review of a project (one per project): `status` (`REQUESTED` | `SUBMITTED` | `PUBLISHED` | `HIDDEN`), `rating` (1–5), `quote`, `authorName`, `authorRole?`, `consent` (may go on the website), `locale` (the language it was written in), `requestedAt`, `submittedAt?`, `publishedAt?`. Asked for (email to every person of the account) when the project's phase moves to `LAUNCHED`, once, or from the project page. The team publishes it only with consent; published reviews show on the home page's testimonials, after the case studies' ones, in their own language.
- **ProjectCredential** — access a client shares for a project instead of emailing it: `label`, `url?`, `username?`, `secret` and `notes?` sealed with AES-256-GCM (key derived from `CREDENTIALS_SECRET`, purpose `project-credentials-v1`), `createdById?`, `viewedAt?`. The people of the client account add and delete theirs but never read a secret back; the team reveals one at a time (logged as `project.credential_viewed`, and the client sees when) and can delete them all at the end. Adding one emails the team inbox, never with the secret.
- **SiteMonitor / SiteCheck** — uptime monitoring of a client project's production site (`productionUrl`): `enabled`, `status` (`UNKNOWN` | `UP` | `DOWN`), `statusSince`, `failures` in a row, `lastCheckedAt`, `lastError`, `certExpiresAt` (read at most hourly), `certWarnedFor`. `MonitoringService` (API process, every 5 minutes, off in tests) requests each enabled site (up below HTTP 400 within 15 s, redirects followed) and stores a `SiteCheck` (`ok`, `statusCode`, `responseMs`, `error`; kept 90 days). Two failures in a row make it DOWN and email the team inbox; its return emails too; a certificate with under 14 days left is warned about once. The server itself (disk ≥ 85 %, memory ≥ 90 %: time for a bigger plan) is watched by `deploy/monitor/monitor.sh`.
- **ProjectBrief** — the client's questionnaire for a project (one per project): `status` (`REQUESTED` | `SUBMITTED`), `answers` (JSON by question: `business`, `goals`, `success`, `audience`, `mustHave`, `niceToHave`, `references`, `brandStatus` none/partial/complete, `brandColors`, `brandTone` formal/friendly/technical/playful, `brandAssetsUrl`, `content` client/codeqlick/both, `domain`, `services`, `deadline`, `deadlineReason`, `other`), `requestedAt`, `submittedAt?`. Created when a lead is converted (no email: the welcome email brings them in), asked for by email when a proposal with a project is accepted or from the project page. Sending needs `business`, `goals`, `audience` and `mustHave`; it can be edited and sent again.
- **ProjectTask** — work on a client project: `title`, `description?` (for the team only), `status` (`TODO` | `IN_PROGRESS` | `DONE`), `doneAt?`, `ticketId?` (the support request it came from). Turning a request into a task moves an open request to `IN_PROGRESS`; when all of a request's tasks are done it becomes `RESOLVED` (the client is emailed, as for any status change).
- **TimeEntry** — time spent on a client project: `projectId`, `userId?` (who logged it), `date` (the day, Madrid), `minutes` (1–1440), `description` (max 500). Logging and deleting entries are recorded in the activity log with the minutes.
- **Setting** — site settings edited in the admin panel, one JSON document per `key`. `calculator`: the cost calculator's prices — per project type (`web`, `custom`, `mobile`, `design`, `support`) `{ min, max, weeksMin, weeksMax }` in euros/weeks for a medium size, size factors `{ small, medium, large }`, extras (`languages`, `accounts`, `payments`, `admin`, `integrations`) `{ min, max }`, and `enabled`. An estimate is the type scaled by the size plus the extras, rounded out to hundreds; types and extras with a maximum of 0 are not offered.
- **Booking** — a video call booked from the website: `name`, `email`, `company?`, `message?`, `locale`, `startsAt`/`endsAt`, `status` (`CONFIRMED` | `CANCELLED`), `slotKey` (the start while confirmed, unique, null once cancelled: no double booking), `cancelTokenHash` (SHA-256 of the token in the visitor's cancel link), `sequence` (calendar revision), `leadId?`. Its settings are the `Setting` `booking`: `enabled`, `meetingUrl` (a fixed Google Meet link), `calendarEmail` (optional: also gets the team's invitations and cancellations, so calls land in that calendar, e.g. the owner's Gmail), `durationMinutes` (15–120, default 30), `minNoticeHours` (default 24), `horizonDays` (default 21) and `weekly` free hours per weekday in Madrid time (`[{ start: 'HH:MM', end: 'HH:MM' }]`). Free slots are those hours cut into calls, after the notice, minus confirmed calls. Booking moves the open lead with that email to `CALL` (or starts one there) with the call's day as follow-up; both sides are emailed an iCalendar invitation (`METHOD:REQUEST`; cancellations send `METHOD:CANCEL` with a higher `SEQUENCE`).
- **BookingPage** / **Appointment** — Citas (section 4.9): a person's booking page (`ownerId`, `slug` unique, `title`, `description`, `durationMinutes`, `location`, `video`, `meetingRoomId?`, `askPhone`, `timeZone`, `weekly`, `minNoticeHours`, `horizonDays`, `enabled`) and the appointments booked on it (`pageId`, `ownerId`, `name`, `email`, `phone?`, `note?`, `locale`, `startsAt`/`endsAt`, `status` reusing `BookingStatus`, `cancelTokenHash`, `sequence`, `reminderSentAt?`). Both cascade with their owner.
- **VideochatProfile** photo verification (section 4.7): `verificationStatus` (NONE · PENDING · APPROVED · REJECTED), `verificationKey?` (private file while pending), `verificationNote?`, `verificationSentAt?`, `verifiedAt?`.
- **SwipeProfile** / **SwipePhoto** / **SwipeVote** / **SwipeMatch** / **SwipeMessage** / **SwipeReport** / **SwipeBlock** / **SwipeTakedown** / **SwipeBlockedHash** — Swipe (section 4.10). Profile per account (`userId` PK: 18+ confirmation and terms version, `nickname`, `gender`, `seeking[]`, `interests[]`, verification `status`/`key`/`note`/`sentAt`/`verifiedAt`, suspension `suspendedAt`/`Until`/`Reason`). Photo (`ref` 8 characters unique, shown on the card; `storageKey` of the private file; `width`, `height`; `hash` dHash; `status` PENDING · LIVE · REJECTED · HIDDEN; `rejectReason`; `likes`; `inAlbum`; reviewer). Vote (PK voter + photo, `ownerId`, `like`). Match (`userAId` < `userBId` unique, `lastMessageAt`, read marks per side) with its messages (≤ 1000 characters). Report (reporter, reported, `photoId?`, `reason`, `details`, chat `transcript` JSON, status, reviewer). Block (both ways hide everything). Takedown (anyone's removal request: `email`, `photoRef`, matched `photoId?`, `reason`, `details`, `locale`, status OPEN · REMOVED · CLOSED, `response`). Blocked hash (removed images, refused on upload). Everything but takedowns and blocked hashes cascades with the account.
- **CruisingProfile** / **CruisingSpot** / **CruisingReview** / **CruisingReport** / **CruisingTakedown** / **CruisingCheckIn** / **CruisingConversation** / **CruisingMessage** / **CruisingBlock** — Cruising (section 4.15). Profile per account (`userId` PK: `birthYear`, `termsVersion`, `acceptedAt`, `emailAlerts`, suspension `suspendedAt`/`suspendedUntil`/`suspensionReason`; phase 2: `nickname?` unique, `bio`, `country?`, `lookingFor[]`, `showAge`). Spot (`slug` unique, `name`, `type`, `lat`/`lng` Decimal(8,5), `countryCode`, `city`, `citySlug`, `description`, `bestTimes?`, `status` PENDING · LIVE · REJECTED · HIDDEN · REMOVED, `rejectReason`, `createdById?` set null with the account, `reviewedAt`, `ratingAvg`/`ratingCount` denormalised). Review (`spotId` + `userId` unique, `rating` 1–5, `text?`, `status` LIVE · HIDDEN). Report (reporter, `targetType` SPOT · REVIEW · PROFILE · CONVERSATION, `spotId?`, `reviewId?`, `reportedId?`, `reason`, `details`, chat `transcript` JSON, status OPEN · DISMISSED · ACTIONED, reviewer). Takedown (anyone's removal request: `email`, `spotLink`, matched `spotId?`, `reason`, `details`, `locale`, status OPEN · REMOVED · CLOSED, `response`). Phase 2: check-in (`userId`, `spotId`, `anonymous`, `expiresAt`), conversation (`userAId` < `userBId` unique, `lastMessageAt`, `readAAt`/`readBAt`, `notifiedAAt`/`notifiedBAt`) with its messages (≤ 1000 characters), block (`blockerId` + `blockedId`). Phase 4: the profile's verification (`verificationStatus` NONE · PENDING · APPROVED · REJECTED, `verificationKey`, `verificationNote`, `verificationSentAt`, `verifiedAt`, and the daily counters `verificationTries`/`verificationTriesSince` and `uploads`/`uploadsSince`), **CruisingMedia** (`ref` unique, `ownerId`, `kind` PHOTO · VIDEO, `status` PROCESSING · LIVE · HIDDEN · FAILED, `storageKey`, `posterKey`, `width`, `height`, `durationSeconds`, `hash` (dHash of the photo or poster), `failure`, `cover`, `position`, `reviewedAt`/`reviewedById`), **CruisingBlockedHash** (`hash`, `reason`), reports gain the target MEDIA (`mediaId`) and the reasons NON_CONSENSUAL and STOLEN, takedowns gain `mediaRef`/`mediaId`. Everything but spots and takedowns cascades with the account; a spot stays with `createdById` null.
- **LinkCheck** — a link found in published content and its last check (4.6, Broken links): `url` (absolute: relative links resolved against the page's own address, `SITE_URL/<locale>/blog|work/<slug>`), `sourceType` POST · PORTFOLIO, `sourceId`, `locale` (the translation it appears in); unique `url` + `sourceType` + `sourceId` + `locale`; `internal`, `status` OK · BROKEN, `statusCode?`, `error?` (timeout, DNS, refused, private address…), `failures` (checks failed in a row), `brokenSince?`, `checkedAt`. Rows of links no longer in the content are deleted at the next run; deleting a post or case study deletes its rows.
- **ErrorGroup** — an unexpected error and its repeats (section 4.11): `fingerprint` (SHA-256, unique), `source` API · WEB_SERVER · WEB_BROWSER, `message`, the last occurrence's `stack`, `method`, `path` (no query string), `reference` (request id or Next.js digest) and `userAgent`, `count`, `firstSeenAt`, `lastSeenAt`, `resolvedAt`.
- **SiteAudit** — a free website check (section 4.12): `url` (as checked: the start URL, normalised), `host`, `name?`, `email`, `locale`, `status` (`PENDING` | `DONE` | `FAILED`), `score?` (0–100), `result?` (JSON: the final URL, the four categories' scores and each check's id, status and values), `error?` (why it failed, in English), `leadId?` (the lead it made), `finishedAt?`. Kept 180 days.
- **SavedReply** — a reusable text for lead replies and project comments: `title` (only the admin sees it) and `body`. The admin panel puts `{name}` (lead or client) and `{project}` in when it inserts the text.
- **Codeqlick Cloud** (section 4.13): **CloudTeam** (`slug` unique, `name`, `plan` `FREE` \| `PRO`, `requireTwoFactor` (default false)); **CloudMember** (`teamId` + `userId` unique, `role` `OWNER` \| `ADMIN` \| `MEMBER`, `termsVersion`, `termsAcceptedAt`); **CloudInvite** (`teamId`, `email`, `role`, `tokenHash` unique, `invitedById?`, `expiresAt`, `acceptedAt?`); **CloudToken** (`userId`, `teamId?` (restricted to it), `name`, `prefix`, `tokenHash` unique, `readOnly` (default false), `expiresAt?`, `expiryWarnedAt?`, `lastUsedAt?`, `lastUsedIp?`); **CloudDeviceCode** (`deviceCodeHash` unique, `userCode` unique, `clientName`, `userId?`, `approvedAt?`, `deniedAt?`, `consumedAt?`, `lastPolledAt?`, `expiresAt`); **CloudProject** (`teamId`, `slug` unique, `name`, `productionDeploymentId?`, `productionLockedAt?`, `productionLockedById?` (the person, kept as `null` when they leave), `suspendedAt?`, `suspendedReason?`, `deletedAt?`; password protection: `protection` `OFF` \| `PREVIEWS` \| `ALL` (default `OFF`), `protectionHash?` (Argon2id), `protectionDays` (default 7), `protectionVersion` (default 0), `protectionUpdatedAt?`; the bypass token for automation: `protectionBypassHash?` (SHA-256 hex of the token, unique), `protectionBypassPrefix?` (its first 12 characters), `protectionBypassCreatedAt?`, `protectionBypassCreatedById?` (the person, kept as `null` when they leave), `protectionBypassLastUsedAt?`; the shared analytics link: `analyticsShareHash?` (unique), `analyticsShareSealed?`; the weekly check of production, decision 81: `productionChecks` (Boolean, default false), `productionCheckNextAt?`); **CloudDeployment** (`projectId`, `shortId` (unique per project), `target` `PREVIEW` \| `PRODUCTION` (as uploaded), `message?`, `commit?`, `branch?`, `sizeBytes`, `fileCount`, `keptAt?`, `config?` (JSON: the parsed rules of `codeqlick.json`, null when it has none; 4.13 "Redirects, rewrites and headers"), `createdById?`, `tokenId?`); **CloudDomain** (`projectId`, `name` unique, `verificationToken`, `state` `PENDING` \| `VERIFIED` \| `LIVE` \| `ERROR`, `verifiedAt?`, `checkedAt?`, `lastError?`); **CloudEvent** (`teamId`, `actorId?`, `actorName`, `tokenId?`, `action`, `targetLabel?`, `details?` JSON, `ip?`); **CloudReport** (`url`, `projectId?` (matched from the address), `reason`, `details`, `email`, `status` `OPEN` \| `CLOSED`, `note?`, `closedAt?`). Phase 1b: **CloudForm** (`projectId`, `name`, `key` unique, `enabled`, `notify`, `recipientIds` (users), `spamFilter` (default on), `overLimitMonth?`); **CloudFormSubmission** (`formId`, `data` JSON, `email?`, `page?`, `spam` (default false), `spamReason?` (`markup` \| `links` \| `field` \| `page` \| `repeat` \| `manual`), `contentHash?` (SHA-256 hex), `createdAt`); **CloudMonitor** (`teamId`, `projectId?`, `name`, `kind` `HTTP` \| `HEARTBEAT`, `url?`, `keyword?`, `keywordMode` `CONTAINS` \| `NOT_CONTAINS`, `key?` unique, `periodMinutes?`, `graceMinutes?`, `enabled`, `status` `UNKNOWN` \| `UP` \| `DOWN` \| `PAUSED`, `statusSince`, `failures`, `lastCheckedAt?`, `lastPingAt?`, `deadlineAt?`, `lastError?`, `certExpiresAt?`, `certCheckedAt?`, `certWarnedFor?`, `alertIds` (users), `lastDownAlertAt?`, `lastUpAlertAt?`, `slowMs?`, `slowCount` (default 0), `slowSince?`); **CloudMonitorCheck** (`monitorId`, `checkedAt`, `ok`, `statusCode?`, `responseMs?`, `error?`: HTTP checks and heartbeat calls); **CloudMonitorEvent** (`monitorId`, `status`, `at`, `error?`); **CloudStatusPage** (`teamId` unique, `enabled`, `title`, `intro?`, `items` JSON: `[{ monitorId, label }]`, `subscriptions` (default false: email subscriptions, decision 105), `emailWindowStart?` and `emailsInWindow` (default 0: the hour's send counter)); **CloudStatusNotice** (`teamId`, `kind` `INCIDENT` | `MAINTENANCE`, `title`, `state`, `monitorIds`, `startsAt?`, `endsAt?`, `resolvedAt?`, `startNotifiedAt?`, `createdById?`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`); **CloudStatusNoticeUpdate** (`noticeId`, `state?`, `body`, `createdById?`, `createdAt`); **CloudStatusSubscriber** (`teamId` (cascade), `email` (lower-cased; unique with `teamId`), `locale` `EN` \| `ES`, `status` `PENDING` \| `ACTIVE` \| `UNSUBSCRIBED`, `confirmTokenHash?` (SHA-256 hex, unique), `confirmExpiresAt?` (48 hours), `confirmSentAt?`, `confirmedAt?`, `unsubscribeToken` (32 random bytes, base64url, unique, kept in clear like the newsletter's), `unsubscribedAt?`, `createdAt`; indexes on `teamId` + `status` and on `email` + `confirmSentAt`; no IP); **CloudStatusDispatch** (the outbox of notice emails: `teamId` and `noticeId` (cascade), `updateId?` (cascade), `kind` `NOTICE` \| `UPDATE` \| `RESOLVED` \| `MAINTENANCE_STARTED`, `cursor?` (id of the last subscriber handled), `sent` (default 0), `completedAt?`, `createdAt`); **CloudAudit** (`teamId`, `projectId?` (set for preview and scheduled checks; set null when the project is deleted), `userId?` (null for a preview or scheduled check, which no member runs), `kind` `MANUAL` \| `PREVIEW` \| `SCHEDULED` (default `MANUAL`; decisions 69 and 81), `url`, `score`, `result` JSON, `createdAt`: only finished checks are kept; index on `projectId` + `kind` + `createdAt`). Phase 2: **CloudGitInstallation** (`installationId` unique, `teamId`, `accountLogin`, `accountType`, `createdById?`, `suspendedAt?`); the project's Git link and settings on **CloudProject** (`gitInstallationId?`, `gitRepoId?` and `gitRepo` (`owner/name`), `gitBranch` (production, default `main`), `rootDir`, `installCommand`, `buildCommand`, `outputDir`, `nodeVersion`, `buildBranches`, `buildPullRequests`, `previewChecks` (Boolean, default false: check each pull request's preview, decision 69), `onlyRootChanges`; the rebuild schedule: `rebuildFrequency` `OFF` \| `HOURLY` \| `DAILY` \| `WEEKLY`, `rebuildMinute?`, `rebuildHour?`, `rebuildWeekday?`, `rebuildNextAt?`, `rebuildFailures`); **CloudEnvVar** (`projectId`, `key`, `valueSealed`, `production`, `preview`, `branch` (`''` = every preview; project + key + branch unique)); **CloudBuild** (`projectId`, `number` (per project), `trigger` `PUSH` \| `PULL_REQUEST` \| `MANUAL` \| `SCHEDULE` \| `HOOK`, `hookName?` (a deploy hook's name at the time), `target`, `branch`, `commit`, `commitMessage?`, `author?`, `pullRequest?`, `noCache` (default `false`: the build was asked to run without the dependency cache), `status` `QUEUED` \| `RUNNING` \| `SUCCEEDED` \| `FAILED` \| `CANCELED`, `error?`, `log` (≤ 1 MB), `deploymentId?`, `auditId?` (the website check of its preview, decision 69; set null when the audit is deleted after its 30 days), `keptAsPreview` (a production build deployed while production was locked), `builder?`, `queuedAt`, `startedAt?`, `heartbeatAt?`, `finishedAt?`, `createdById?`); **CloudGitDelivery** (`id` = GitHub's delivery id, `receivedAt`; kept a day); **CloudPullComment** (`projectId` + `pullRequest` unique, `commentId`); **CloudDeployHook** (`projectId`, `name`, `branch`, `prefix`, `tokenHash` unique, `createdById?`, `lastCalledAt?`, unique `projectId` + `name`; deleted with its project, when the repository is disconnected or when another is connected). **CloudUsageAlert** (`teamId`, `key` `builds` \| `formSubmissions` \| `analyticsEvents` \| `storage` \| `deploymentsPerHour`, `period` (`yyyy-mm`, Madrid), `percent`, `used`, `limit`, `createdAt`; unique on `teamId` + `key` + `period`; kept 13 months; decision 54).
- **ActivityLog** — who changed what: `actorId?` (null once the user is deleted) and `actorName` copied at the time, `action` (`<target>.<verb>`, e.g. `post.published`), `targetType`, `targetId?`, `targetLabel?` (title, name or email at the time), `details?` (JSON: what changed, e.g. `{ phase, progress }`). Written by the services after each admin change and each change to one's own account (password, two-step verification, signing out devices); a failed write is logged and never fails the change.

All ids are UUIDs. All tables have `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. `Locale` is an enum `EN` | `ES`; the HTTP API uses lowercase `en` | `es`.

### 4.3 Authentication

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  participant Br as Browser
  participant Web as Next.js (BFF)
  participant Api as NestJS API
  Br->>Web: POST login form (server action)
  Web->>Api: POST /v1/auth/login {email, password}
  Api-->>Web: {accessToken, refreshToken, expiresIn, user}
  Web-->>Br: Set-Cookie cq_at, cq_rt (httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax)
  Br->>Web: GET /en/portal
  Web->>Api: GET /v1/portal/projects (Authorization: Bearer cq_at)
  Note over Web: proxy.ts refreshes cq_at with cq_rt when it has expired
```

- **Access token:** JWT (HS256), 15 minutes, claims `sub`, `role`, `email`, `name` and `sid` (the session's `familyId`, which marks the current device in the sessions list).
- **Refresh token:** 48 random bytes, base64url, 30 days. Only its SHA-256 hash is stored. Each refresh **rotates** the token (old one revoked, `replacedById` set). Presenting an already-rotated token is treated as theft: the whole `familyId` is revoked.
- **Logout** revokes the current session.
- **Two-step verification required for admins** (`ADMIN_TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED`, true in production): `RolesGuard` refuses admin routes with 403 `Two-step verification is required for admin accounts` while the admin's account has no TOTP (read from the database, so turning it on takes effect at once). Account routes (`/auth/*`) carry no `@Roles`, so the setup still works. `GET /auth/2fa` reports `required`, and turning it off is refused (409). The admin layout then shows only the account page with a notice, and `apiFetch` sends that 403 to it.
- **Sessions list:** a session (device) is a `familyId`; its live row (the latest rotation) gives the browser, the IP and the last activity. Signing a device out revokes its family; it stays in until its access token expires (15 minutes at most). A refresh token whose row was revoked without a replacement (signed out) is rejected without the reuse alarm.
- **New-device sign-in email:** when a sign-in completes (`POST /auth/login` without two-step verification, or `POST /auth/2fa/verify`), the API describes the request's User-Agent as a browser and an operating system (`common/user-agent.ts`, the same rules as the website's `lib/user-agent.ts`) and compares it with every session row the account still has (live, signed out or expired but not yet cleaned up: about the last 60 days). When no row describes the same pair, the account gets an email in its language: when (Madrid time), the browser and system, the IP address, a button to its account page (`/admin/account` for admins, `/portal/account` otherwise), where it can sign that device out and change the password, and a link to reset a forgotten password. Nothing is sent when neither the browser nor the system can be told, nor for the sign-ins that create or claim an account (registration, invitation, sign-up confirmation). The email goes in the background: a mail failure is logged and never blocks the sign-in.
- **Cleanup:** sessions and password reset tokens are deleted 30 days after they expire (the API checks twice a day). The 30 days keep reuse detection working for a rotated token that comes back shortly after expiring.
- **Bots and fake accounts (owner's request 2026-11-08):**
  - **Disposable email addresses** (10minutemail, Mailinator, YOPmail…) can't register or become an account's email: `POST /auth/register` and `POST /auth/email` answer `400` with the field error `email: disposable` when the domain, or any domain it is under, is on the community list `disposable-email-domains` (the npm package `disposable-email-domains-js`, kept current by Dependabot). Accounts the team creates or invites are not checked.
  - **Cloudflare Turnstile** on sign-up and sign-in: the website's `/register` and `/login` (on every host, web apps included) show Turnstile's widget (managed mode, light theme, the page's language) when `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` is set, and the Server Action sends its token as `captcha`. With `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` set, the API checks the token with Cloudflare's `siteverify` (secret, token and the visitor's IP; 5 s timeout) before anything else and answers `400` with the field error `captcha` when it is missing or refused. If Cloudflare can't be reached the request goes through (logged): an outage must not lock everyone out, and the other limits still apply. Off without the keys (development, tests); `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` without the secret only shows the widget. The CSP allows `https://challenges.cloudflare.com` for scripts, frames and connections.
- **Breached passwords (roadmap "Refuse passwords found in known data breaches", decision 56):** every place that **sets** a password (sign-up `POST /auth/register`, `POST /auth/register/invitation`, `POST /auth/password-reset/confirm`, `POST /auth/password`, and an admin's `POST /admin/users` / `PATCH /admin/users/:id` with `password`) first checks it against Have I Been Pwned's Pwned Passwords with k-anonymity (`PasswordBreachService`): the API sends only the first 5 hexadecimal characters of the password's SHA-1 to `GET https://api.pwnedpasswords.com/range/<prefix>` (header `Add-Padding: true`, so the answer's size gives nothing away) and compares the remaining 35 characters itself; padding rows (count 0) don't count. A match answers `400` with the field error `{ path: <the password field>, message: 'breached' }` (`password` or `newPassword`) and nothing is changed: no token is spent, no email is sent. On sign-up the check runs before the email is looked up, so the answer is the same whether or not the email has an account. **Fail open:** a timeout (2 s) or any error lets the password through and logs a warning, so a third party's outage never blocks sign-ups. Signing in is never checked. Switched on by `PASSWORD_BREACH_CHECK` (`true` in production; off by default, so development, tests and CI never call out). The website shows "This password has appeared in a data breach…" under the field (EN/ES). No personal data leaves the server: 5 characters of a hash match about a thousand unrelated passwords.
- **Brute force:** login is limited to 5 attempts per minute per IP; the service runs an Argon2 verify even when the email doesn't exist, so timing doesn't reveal accounts.
- Deactivating a user (`isActive=false`) revokes all their sessions.
- **Two-step verification (optional, per user):** an RFC 6238 authenticator app (TOTP, SHA-1, 30 s, 6 digits, ±1 step). With it on, a correct password returns `{ twoFactorRequired, challengeToken }` instead of tokens; the challenge is a 5-minute JWT signed with its own key (so it never passes as an access token) and the web app keeps it in the httpOnly cookie `cq_2fa` until the code is entered at `/[locale]/login/verify`. The secret is stored encrypted (AES-256-GCM, key derived from `JWT_ACCESS_SECRET`: rotating that secret means setting two-step verification up again). An accepted time step can't be reused. Ten single-use recovery codes (`xxxx-xxxx`, stored as SHA-256) replace the app when the phone is lost.
- **Passkeys (roadmap "Sign in with a passkey", decision 104):** WebAuthn with `@simplewebauthn/server` and `/browser` (exact versions). The relying party is `codeqlick.com` (`WEBAUTHN_RP_ID`), so one passkey works on the website and every app host (`*.codeqlick.com`); the API accepts the origins of the website and of the hosts in `WEBAPPS` (`WEBAUTHN_ORIGINS`, defaulting to them). A person adds up to **10** passkeys from **My account → Security** after typing their password (as for turning two-step verification off): `register/options` returns options with `residentKey: required` and `userVerification: required` and the passkeys already registered as `excludeCredentials`; `register/verify` stores `Passkey` (`credentialId` unique, `publicKey`, `counter`, `transports`, `deviceType`, `backedUp`, `name` ≤ 40, `createdAt`, `lastUsedAt`). Challenges are single-use rows (`WebAuthnChallenge`: `purpose` REGISTER or LOGIN, the user for REGISTER, 5 minutes) deleted on use and by the twice-daily cleanup. **Signing in:** the login page (every host) offers "Sign in with a passkey" and conditional UI (`autocomplete="username webauthn"`, the browser suggests passkeys in the email field); `login/options` (public) returns a challenge with no allowed credentials (discoverable) and its id; `login/verify` checks the assertion (origin, RP ID, user verification, the stored public key, a counter that only grows unless it is 0) and returns `AuthTokens` like a password sign-in: a passkey counts as **both factors**, so two-step verification isn't asked, the new-device email and the session rules apply, and an inactive account is refused. Turnstile isn't asked for it (a passkey can't be scripted). Renaming and deleting (password again) from the same page; adding one emails "A passkey was added to your account" and every change is in the activity log; the account export lists them (name, dates, device type, not the key). **Admins:** `ADMIN_TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED` still means an authenticator app (TOTP); passkeys don't replace it, they are a faster way in.
- **Unsubscribe links (optional emails, decision 70):** the two recurring emails a person can turn off, the weekly summary (`weeklyDigest`, kind `digest`) and the monthly website report (`monthlyReport`, kind `report`), each carry a link that turns that one off without signing in. Transactional emails (password reset, welcome, invitations, email change, security notices, project updates, proposals, support answers…) never carry it. Every optional email has the headers `List-Unsubscribe: <PUBLIC_API_URL/email/unsubscribe?token=…>` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` (RFC 8058, which Gmail and Yahoo require for recurring mail), and a visible "Unsubscribe" link in its footer (text and HTML, `email-layout.ts`) to the website's `SITE_URL/{locale}/email/unsubscribe?token=…` in the person's language. The token is `<payload>.<signature>`: the payload is the base64url of `<userId>:<kind>`, the signature the base64url HMAC-SHA256 of the payload with a key derived from `JWT_ACCESS_SECRET` (its HMAC of the label `email-unsubscribe`), compared in constant time. No new variable, no table, no expiry: a link works as long as the account exists. Rotating `JWT_ACCESS_SECRET` invalidates old links; the person then signs in and changes it in My account. The endpoints (section 5, Public: `POST` / `GET /email/unsubscribe`, `GET` / `PUT /email/preferences`) need no session, are limited per IP (120/min for the one-click `POST`, which mail providers call from a few shared addresses; 30/min for the rest) and answer `404` alike for an invalid token and a deleted account (also when the account is deleted between reading and saving). A `GET` never changes anything, because mail scanners and link previews open links; only the provider's one-click `POST` and the website's `PUT` do. `GET /email/unsubscribe` (the header's URL opened in a browser) redirects to the website's page. Tokens never reach the logs: the request logger replaces the value of every `token` query parameter with `[redacted]`. Gmail only offers its one-click button when the DKIM signature covers both `List-Unsubscribe` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post`, so the sending server (OVH's SMTP) must sign those headers. Preferences are still edited in My account (`/auth/preferences`).

### 4.4 Errors

All errors use RFC 9457 *problem details* with `Content-Type: application/problem+json`:

```json
{ "type": "about:blank", "title": "Bad Request", "status": 400, "detail": "Validation failed", "instance": "/v1/leads", "errors": [{ "path": "email", "message": "Invalid email" }], "requestId": "…" }
```

Unknown errors return 500 without leaking internals; the stack goes to the logs with the request id. They are also recorded for Admin → Errors (section 4.11); an `HttpException` with a 5xx (a service not set up, the API saying it is unavailable) is a known state and is not.

### 4.6 Background work

Scheduled work runs inside the API process (timers that start on boot, off when `NODE_ENV=test`); nothing else needs a scheduler:

- **Uptime monitoring** of client sites every 5 minutes (`MonitoringService`, see SiteMonitor).
- **Session and reset-token cleanup** twice a day (`TokenCleanupService`).
- **Domain renewal** every 6 hours (`DomainsService`): expiry dates older than a day are refreshed (OVH or RDAP) and the reminders due are sent.
- **Request previews** every 6 hours (`SitePreviewsService`): previews older than 30 days, or of converted or spam leads, are taken down, and the follow-ups due are sent (section 8.2).
- **Monthly website report** (clients): `SiteReportsService` checks every hour and, from the 1st at 09:00 Madrid time, sends last month's report of each managed site whose `reportedMonth` is older (section 8.2). Off in tests.
- **Expired offers** every hour (`SiteNewsService`): a managed site with news switched on and a published post whose `expiresAt` is after the start of the site's last successful build (`expiresAt` > `newsBuiltAt` and in the past, so the live site still shows it) is rebuilt, which hides the post (section 8.2). Idempotent, so a restart or a missed hour loses nothing. Off in tests.
- **Managed sites' domains** every 10 minutes (`SiteDomainsService`): each domain not yet LIVE (and every LIVE one after 6 hours) is connected through OVH when the team manages it and its state checked (see ManagedSite).
- **Status page subscribers** (`CloudStatusSubscribersService`, section 4.13 "Email subscriptions"), off in tests: every minute it turns maintenance windows that have started into dispatches and sends the open dispatches in batches within each team's hourly cap; daily it deletes unconfirmed sign-ups, old `UNSUBSCRIBED` rows and finished dispatches.
- **Weekly summary** (clients): `WeeklyDigestService` checks every hour, in the API process. It sends this week's summary to every active client with `weeklyDigest` whose `lastDigestAt` is before this week's Monday 08:00 Madrid time, so a restart or a missed Monday neither skips nor repeats it. For each project the email lists the updates and completed milestones of the last 7 days, updates waiting for approval and the next milestone. A week with nothing to tell sends nothing, but still counts as done. Clients turn it off in their account (`PATCH /auth/preferences`) or from the email's unsubscribe link (section 4.3) and admins on the user page, which also has a "send now" button (`POST /admin/projects/weekly-digest/:clientId` → `{ sent }`, or `{ sent: false, reason: 'no-news' }`). Off in tests.
- **Proposals about to expire** every 6 hours (`ProposalRemindersService`): a `SENT` proposal whose `validUntil` (Madrid day) is today or within the next 3 days, sent more than a day ago and not reminded yet (`expiryRemindedAt`), emails every person of the client account in their language ("it can be accepted until …", with the link to it, or the invitation for someone who hasn't made the account theirs, as when it was sent) and tells the team inbox (`LEADS_NOTIFY_TO`, English). Once per proposal. Off in tests.
- **Newsletter** (`NewsletterService`, section 4.14), off in tests: every 10 minutes it emails each post that has just gone live to the active subscribers; daily it deletes `PENDING` sign-ups whose confirmation expired more than 7 days ago and `UNSUBSCRIBED` rows unsubscribed more than 12 months ago.
- **Scheduled posts and case studies** need no job to appear: they are hidden until their `publishedAt` (see Scheduling). Only their IndexNow announcement waits for a timer: `IndexNowService` every 5 minutes, when `INDEXNOW_ENABLED` (see IndexNow). Off in tests.
- **Form messages** daily (`SiteFormsService`): messages kept from managed sites' forms older than 12 months are deleted. Off in tests.
- **Website checks** (`SiteAuditsService`, section 4.12): run one by one as they are asked for; on boot the ones left `PENDING` from the last hour run again and older ones are marked `FAILED`. Checks older than 180 days are deleted daily. Off in tests.
- **Broken links** weekly (`LinkChecksService`, proposed by the agent team 2026-11-14): every hour it checks whether the last full run is 7 days old (or there was none) and then reads every link of the published posts and case studies (a run that fails is retried 6 hours later, not every hour; the times are kept in `settings` under `linkChecks`), in both languages (Markdown links and images, not code). Internal links (`/blog/<slug>`, `/work/<slug>`, with or without locale, or absolute on `SITE_URL`) are checked against the database (live, with at least one translation: the site shows another language when the link's one is missing), uploaded images (`/media/…`) on disk, and the site's own static routes (pages with a parameter in the address, such as `/citas/<slug>`, and other files with an extension count as unknown, never broken); anchors (`#…`) and `mailto:` are skipped. External `http(s)` links get a `HEAD` through the website check's `safeFetch` (private addresses refused on every hop, at most 5 redirects; `GET` when `HEAD` answers 405 or 501), 10 s timeout, user agent `CodeqlickLinkCheck/1 (+https://codeqlick.com)`, one request at a time per host and at most two a second overall; each distinct URL is fetched once per run. A link is **broken** after two failed checks in a row (4xx other than 429, 5xx, or a network error; 429 and a 403 that is a bot challenge (`cf-mitigated: challenge`, a Cloudflare `cf_chl` marker, AWS WAF or Vercel challenge headers) count as unknown, not failures; the same two strikes apply to internal links), so a site that blinks once isn't reported; it is OK again at the next good check. No email: Admin → Content → **Broken links** lists the broken ones of live content (links of unpublished or deleted content drop out at once; link, status or error, since when, where: post or case study with its language and an "Edit" link), and the dashboard counts them. "Check again" re-checks one link at once (10 a minute). Off in tests.
- **Cruising** (`CruisingService`, section 4.15), off in tests: every 10 minutes it emails once per conversation when the partner's messages have waited 15 minutes unread; every 30 minutes it sends the team digest; every hour it deletes check-ins that expired more than 24 hours ago conversation starts older than a day, and nickname changes older than 14 days. Phase 4 (`CruisingMediaService`): the video queue transcodes one video at a time in the API process (`nice ffmpeg`; videos still PROCESSING after a restart go back in the queue, or FAIL as `interrupted` when their upload is gone), and every 6 hours (first a minute after start) it deletes the files no row points to (older than an hour) and stale uploads of the work folder (older than 2 hours).
- **Old resolved errors** daily (`ErrorsService`): resolved groups not seen for 30 days are deleted. Off in tests.
- **Codeqlick Cloud** (section 4.13), off in tests: `CloudHostingService` every hour deletes deployments past the last 20 of each project (never production, never kept ones) with their files, projects deleted 30 days ago, and expired device codes, invitations and tokens (it first emails people about tokens expiring within 7 days or just expired, see API tokens); events older than 365 days daily. `CloudDomainsService` every 10 minutes checks the domains due (see Custom domains). Phase 1b: `CloudMonitorsService` every minute (HTTP checks due, late heartbeats, alerts; checks older than 90 days daily); `CloudFormsService` daily deletes submissions older than 180 days and spam older than 30 days; audits of kind `MANUAL` and `PREVIEW` older than 30 days and of kind `SCHEDULED` older than 90 days are deleted by `CloudAuditsService` once a day. `CloudProductionChecksService` (decision 81) every minute runs the weekly website checks of production that are due (see Production checks). Phase 2: `CloudBuildsService` every 30 seconds fails running builds without a heartbeat for 2 minutes; daily it empties logs older than 30 days, deletes builds older than 90 days and deliveries older than a day.

On the host, `deploy/monitor/monitor.sh` (cron, every 5 minutes) watches the server and the stack, and the `backup` container dumps the database daily (section 8).

### 4.7 Video chat (`modules/videochat`)

Codeqlick's first own web app: a random 1:1 video chat at **videochat.codeqlick.com** (spec and decisions also in the Notion roadmap row "Random video chat"). Decisions 21 and 22.

- **Who:** signed-in accounts only (registration confirms the email). Explicit sexual content between consenting adults is allowed (owner's decision, 2026-11-07); minors, non-consent, recording without permission, harassment, hate and anything illegal are not. Before entering, a person gives their date of birth (checked ≥ 18, **not stored**) and accepts the rules (`VIDEOCHAT_TERMS_VERSION`; a new version asks again). `VIDEOCHAT_AGE_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED=true` also requires `ageVerifiedAt` (an age-verification provider or the team, `PUT /admin/videochat/users/:userId/age-verification`); off by default.
- **Media never touches the server:** WebRTC browser to browser, or through **coturn** (TURN) when no direct path exists. The API hands out STUN/TURN URLs (`TURN_URLS`) and TURN credentials valid 12 hours (`<expiry>:<userId>` signed with HMAC-SHA1 of `TURN_SECRET`, coturn's `use-auth-secret`).
- **Signaling:** a WebSocket on the API's HTTP server at `/v1/videochat/ws` (`ws` library, no Nest adapter). Nginx maps `wss://videochat.codeqlick.com/ws` to it. It only accepts `Origin`s in `VIDEOCHAT_ORIGINS` (any origin is refused in production without the header), at most 5 sockets per IP and 64 KB per message, and a socket must send `{ type: 'auth', ticket }` within 5 s. The **ticket** comes from `POST /videochat/ticket`, which the website calls server-side (BFF: no token in the browser): 32 random bytes, single use, 30 s, kept hashed in memory. One live socket per account: a new one closes the old (`error: replaced`, close 4409). Pings every 30 s drop dead sockets.
- **Matching:** in memory (`Matchmaker`; one API instance, scaling out would need a shared store). A `join` pairs with a compatible person waiting, ranked by: first, someone this person hasn't talked to in the last 24 hours (the API remembers each account's last 20 partners, in memory; if only people already met are waiting, they still match); then, the most interests in common; then, who waited longest. Among the best three of that ranking it picks at random; never two sockets of one account, two people who blocked each other (either way), or the same two again within 60 s. The newcomer gets `matched { initiator: true }` and makes the WebRTC offer. Every 5 s the API pairs people already waiting who may meet now (two who just skipped each other, once the 60 s are over), so two people alone never wait forever. While waiting, `waiting { others }` tells how many more people wait who can't be paired with this one now (filters, a block or the 60 s); the room then says so and links to the filters. **Widen the search:** after 30 s waiting with filters on (`seeking`, same country or same language), the room offers "Search without my filters this time" (`widen`): that wait ignores the person's own filters, never the other person's, and the next `join` (after a call or `next`) uses them again; the saved filters don't change. `waiting { others, filtered, widened }` tells the room whether the person has filters and whether this wait is widened.
- **Messages:** browser → server `auth`, `join`, `next`, `leave`, `widen`, `like`, `call { contactId }`, `answer { callId, accept }`, `signal { data: { sdp } | { candidate } }` (relayed as is), `chat { text ≤ 500 }`, `report { reason, details? }`, `block`, and the group messages below; server → browser `ready`, `online`, `waiting`, `matched`, `liked`, `mutual`, `calling`, `incoming`, `call-ended`, `signal`, `chat`, `partner-left`, `reported`, `blocked`, `suspended`, `error { code }`. Limits per socket: 400 messages/min (over it, closed 4429), 20 `next`/min, 20 chat messages/10 s, 200 signals/min, 5 reports or blocks/min; tickets 10/min per account and 30/min per IP.
- **Text chat extras:** while someone types, the partner sees "typing…" (`typing { on }`: sent when typing starts and when it stops — after 3 s without a key, on send, or when the box is emptied; relayed as is, shown for at most 6 s without news). **Quick reactions:** six fixed emojis (❤️ 😂 😮 🔥 👍 😘; `react { emoji }`, anything else is refused) float over the other person's video and are added to the chat log; they count toward the chat limit (20 per 10 s). An incoming message or reaction plays a short soft tone (a "Sounds" switch in the chat turns tones off; remembered on the device) and the tab title flashes while the tab is hidden.
- **Chat translation:** in browsers with the built-in Translator and Language Detector APIs (Chrome and Edge 138+ on desktop), the chat shows a "Translate" switch (remembered on the device). With it on, each message from the other person (or people, in a group) in another language than the page's is translated on the device and shown translated, with "See original"; nothing is sent to a server. Without the APIs the switch isn't shown. The first use may download the browser's language model.
- **Report and block** act on the current partner or on the one of a call that ended less than 5 minutes ago. Both end the call and store a block (reporting someone also blocks them).
