A report keeps the call's last 30 text messages (`from: reporter | reported`). **Suspension** (`VideochatProfile.suspendedAt`, `suspendedUntil`, `suspensionReason`): automatic after reports from 3 different people within 24 hours (dismissed reports don't count), and at once after one `MINOR` report, which also emails `LEADS_NOTIFY_TO` (no personal data, a link to the admin page); both last until the team reviews them. The team suspends for 24 hours, 7 days, 30 days or for good. A suspended account is disconnected (`suspended`, close 4403) and gets no tickets until the suspension ends or is lifted; a timed one ends by itself (`suspendedUntil` in the past counts as not suspended).
- **Mutual contacts:** during a call or up to 5 minutes after it (like report and block), a person can press "I like them" (`like`). When both have liked each other they become **contacts** (`VideochatLike` rows both ways; a contact is a mutual pair) and both are told at once (`mutual { nickname }`) if connected. Contacts are listed on `/contacts` (nickname, interests, whether they are in the video chat right now and free); "Call" opens the room with `?call=<contactId>`, which sends `call` once connected. A person can **call** a contact who is connected and not in a call (`call { contactId }`): the contact gets `incoming { callId, partner }` wherever they are in the room (searching or idle), and accepts or declines (`answer { callId, accept }`); the caller gets `calling { callId, partner }` and can hang up; unanswered after 30 s it ends (`call-ended { callId, reason: declined | timeout | busy | offline | cancelled }`; searching, next or stop by either side cancels it, and a contact who was searching searches again). A call to someone who is not a contact gets `error { code: not-contact }`. On accept both leave the queue and start a normal call (report, block and next work as usual; the caller makes the offer). Only between mutual contacts with no block either way; at most 10 calls a minute per socket. Removing a contact (`DELETE /videochat/contacts/:id`) deletes the caller's like, so the pair is no longer mutual; blocking deletes both likes. `contactId` is the other account's id, only ever shown to their contacts.
- **Group calls (owner's request 2026-11-08):** up to 4 mutual contacts in one video call (`GROUP_MAX`), browser to browser as a mesh (each one sends to all the others, like Meetings), over the same socket. Whoever is in a group (or starts one) invites a contact who is connected and free (not in a call, ringing or another group): `group-invite { contactId }`. The first invite creates the group with the inviter alone in it (`group { groupId, self, members[] }`). The contact gets `group-invited { groupId, from, size }` wherever they are in the room and accepts or declines (`group-answer { groupId, accept }`); unanswered after 60 s, or when the group ends or fills, it ends (`group-invite-ended { groupId, reason: declined | timeout | busy | offline | full | gone | blocked | not-contact }`, sent to both). Nobody joins a group where a member blocked them or was blocked by them. Joining leaves any 1:1 call or queue; the newcomer gets `group` with the people inside and makes a WebRTC offer to each one (`group-signal { to | from, data }`, relayed only between members); the others get `group-joined { member }`. `group-leave` (or `join`/`next`, or closing the socket) leaves it, the others get `group-left { memberId }`; the last one may stay alone and invite again; an empty group is forgotten. Members' ids are per connection, never account ids. `group-chat { text ≤ 500 }` goes to everyone in the group (`group-chat { from, text }`, same limit as the 1:1 chat). In the room, `group-contacts` returns the person's contacts with whether each can be invited now (`group-contacts { items }`). **Safety:** every member has Report and Block (`group-report { memberId, reason, details? }`, `group-block { memberId }`): the same reports, blocks and automatic suspensions as 1:1; a report keeps the reporter's and the reported person's last group messages (`from: reporter | reported`), and both take the reporter out of the group. The contacts page has "Group" next to "Call" (`/room?group=<contactId>`, which sends `group-invite` once connected). Group calls are not in the statistics.
- **Public profile and filters (all free):** a nickname (the account's username, see "Username" in section 6; never the account name), gender (`WOMAN` · `MAN` · `NONBINARY`, optional), who they want to meet (`seeking`, any of the three; empty means anyone), country (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, optional) and language (ISO 639-1, optional), with "same country only" and "same language only". **Interests:** up to 5 from a fixed list (`chat`, `friends`, `flirting`, `music`, `movies`, `series`, `gaming`, `sports`, `fitness`, `travel`, `books`, `art`, `photography`, `tech`, `food`, `anime`, `languages`, `nature`, `pets`, `fashion`, and a +18 group shown apart: `spicy`, `explicit`, `showing`, `watching`, `roleplay`, `fetish`, `bdsm`, `striptease`, `mutual`, `couples`, `lgbtq`, `nostrings`; no free text, so nothing to moderate). They don't filter anyone out: they make people with interests in common meet first, and the call shows the partner's interests, highlighting the shared ones. Filters hold **both ways**: two people match only when each one's gender is in the other's `seeking` (an empty `seeking` takes anyone, a person without gender only matches empty `seeking`), and each one's "same country/language only" is met. `matched` carries the partner's public profile `{ nickname, gender, country, language, interests, verified }` (nulls when unset). Changes apply from the next socket, which the room opens after saving.
- **Appeals:** a suspended person can appeal once per suspension (`VideochatAppeal`, a message ≤ 2,000 characters). The team accepts (lifts the suspension) or rejects it, with an optional answer, and the person gets an email either way. **History:** every suspension and lift is a `VideochatSanction` row (who, automatic or not, why, until when, which report), shown on the admin's page for the person. Suspending, lifting, dismissing reports, resolving appeals and age checks go to the activity log (target `videochat`).
- **Experience:** the landing shows how many people are online (`GET /videochat/online`, public); the room shows it live too (`online { count }`, sent on `ready` and, every 5 s, to people not in a call or group when the number changed); in the room, `Esc` is "next", a match plays a short tone and flashes the tab title, a lost socket reconnects by itself (1, 2, 4… up to 30 s, with a new ticket), and a call whose connection fails (ICE `failed`, or no media within 15 s) moves on to the next person. The room works on phones (portrait: the partner's video full width, own video as a corner thumbnail, the buttons in an even grid and the chat below). **On phones:** "Switch camera" (front ↔ back, shown when the device has more than one camera; the new track replaces the old one in the call without renegotiating, and only the front camera is mirrored); "Full screen" (the call fills the screen with the buttons over the video; the browser's full screen where it exists, else the same layout inside the page; Esc or the button leaves it); a **weak connection** badge on the partner's video when, for two samples in a row (every 3 s, `getStats`), the round trip is over 600 ms, more than 8% of video packets are lost or the video drops under 8 frames per second (it goes after two good samples); the screen stays on during a call (Wake Lock, where supported); a match or a contact's call vibrates (where supported). In portrait the buttons sit in a bar fixed to the bottom of the screen (above the home indicator), at least 48 px high, with "Next" the widest.
- **App on the phone (PWA):** on the video chat's host, `/manifest.webmanifest` describes the video chat (name "Codeqlick Videochat", `start_url` `/`, `display: standalone`, light theme colours, the brand icons), so it installs as an app: an "Install the app" button where the browser offers it (`beforeinstallprompt`), and the steps for iPhone (Share → Add to Home Screen) elsewhere. The main site keeps its own manifest.
- **"Tell me when someone is there" (Web Push, opt-in per device):** on `/settings` a person turns on notifications for that browser. The browser registers `/videochat-sw.js` (the service worker only shows notifications and opens the video chat when one is tapped; no caching), subscribes with the server's VAPID public key and the website stores the subscription (`PUT /videochat/push`; `DELETE` removes it). When someone has been waiting alone for 20 s, the API notifies, through `web-push`, people who are not connected, are allowed in (onboarded, not suspended), have not blocked or been blocked by them and whose filters match the waiting person's both ways; at most once every 6 hours per account and at most 20 accounts per notification, at most one round a minute. The notification is in the subscription's language and carries no name or profile ("Someone is waiting in the video chat"). A subscription the push service reports gone (404/410) is deleted; at most 10 per account (the oldest go). Off while `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` are empty (`VAPID_SUBJECT` defaults to `mailto:hello@codeqlick.com`). On iPhone, notifications need the app installed first (iOS 16.4+).
- **Peak time alerts (opt-in per account):** with notifications on, `/settings` offers "Also tell me at peak time" (`VideochatProfile.peakAlerts`, `PUT /videochat/push/peak { on }`). Every 5 minutes the API checks whether it is peak time: the current hour (Europe/Madrid) is one of the 3 hours with most calls over the last 14 days (at least 3 calls in it; the ranking is cached for an hour) and at least 5 people are connected now. Then it notifies, through every push subscription they have, accounts that asked for it, are not connected, are allowed in and were not told in the last 20 hours (`peakNotifiedAt`), at most 200 per round ("It's peak time in the video chat: N people are online now"). Off while notifications are off.
- **Statistics (admin):** `/admin/videochat/stats` shows how the video chat is used, for the last 7, 30 or 90 days (days and hours in Europe/Madrid): people who used it each day, new members (first 18+ confirmation), calls (split into under and over a minute), average call length and average wait before a match, reports, calls by hour of the day, and totals for the period (plus members, open reports and suspended accounts now). The data is kept apart from moderation and **anonymous**: `VideochatCallLog` stores only when a call started and ended, its length and the average wait of the two people (never who talked to whom); `VideochatActiveDay` stores which accounts used the video chat on which day (for daily counts). Both are deleted after 180 days (a cleanup in the API, twice a day). A call is logged when it ends.
- **Verified badge (owner's request 2026-11-09):** optional. In `/settings`, a person with a nickname sends a photo of themselves holding a paper with their nickname, "Videochat" and the date (`POST /videochat/verification`, multipart `file`; re-encoded to WebP without metadata like Swipe's; 5 a day). It is stored privately under `SWIPE_UPLOADS_DIR/videochat/` (`VideochatPhotoStorage`; Swipe's orphan cleanup only lists its own top-level files) and the team reviews it on `/admin/videochat` ("Verification photos"). Approving or rejecting deletes the photo (only the outcome stays: `VideochatProfile.verificationStatus` NONE · PENDING · APPROVED · REJECTED, `verificationNote`, `verifiedAt`) and emails the person, with the note when rejected. Approved people carry `verified: true` in what partners see (`matched`, `calling`, `incoming`), and in contacts: a fuchsia tick next to the nickname. The team can take it away from the person's admin page (`DELETE /admin/videochat/users/:userId/verification`). Files nothing points to (a deleted account's) go after an hour, checked every 6 hours. It is not age verification: `ageVerifiedAt` stays separate.
- **Your week (owner's request 2026-11-09):** `/settings` starts with the person's own figures for the last 7 days and the 7 before (`GET /videochat/stats`): chats (their `VideochatActiveDay.calls`, Europe/Madrid days), likes received and new contacts (pairs whose later like fell in the window).
- **Suspicious accounts (admin, owner's request 2026-11-08):** `/admin/videochat/suspicious` lists accounts that look like bots or fake profiles, highest score first (up to 50; suspended ones left out). Signals over the last 7 days, counted per account and day on `VideochatActiveDay` (`calls`, `shortCalls`: under 10 s, `skippedFast`: the other person ended it under 10 s): **reported** by different people in the last 30 days (+3 each, at most +9), **skipped at once** (`skippedFast` ≥ 80 % of at least 15 calls: +3; ≥ 60 %: +1), **machine-like volume** (150 calls or more on a day: +2), **disposable email** (+2) and **new account with a lot of calls** (joined the video chat less than 3 days ago and 30 calls or more: +1). From a score of 3 the account is listed with its reasons, figures and links to its page and to suspend it. "Not suspicious" (`VideochatProfile.suspicionClearedAt`) hides it for 30 days, unless someone new reports it after that.
- **Fast moderation (admin):** on `/admin/videochat` each report shows, beside it, the reported person's history: member since, reports against them by status (open, acted on, dismissed, all time), how many different people reported them, past suspensions and the last one's reason and date. Keyboard shortcuts (shown on the page; ignored while typing in a field): `j` / `k` next / previous report, `x` select it, `d` dismiss, `s` suspend (with the duration chosen on that report), `o` open the person's page, `?` show the shortcuts. Reports can be selected (one by one or all on the page) and dismissed or suspended in bulk: a bulk suspension suspends each person once and closes all their selected reports as ACTIONED; reports no longer open are skipped.
- **Ads (ExoClick, owner's decision 2026-11-08):** banner zones only (300x250, ExoClick's asynchronous tag); no popunders, interstitials or push, and never on screen with a call. Three zones (`lib/videochat-ads.ts`): `waiting` under the room while searching, `between` under the room after "Stop", `pages` at the bottom of the landing and of `/settings`. `components/videochat/AdZone` shows a labelled box ("Publicidad" / "Advertisement") with the `<ins>` tag, adds `https://a.magsrv.com/ad-provider.js` once per page and asks it to serve on every mount; a zone without an ID renders nothing, so a release turns each one on. Only the video chat's and Swipe's hosts (4.10) widen the CSP for ExoClick (`script-src`, `connect-src` and `frame-src` add `*.magsrv.com`, `*.exoclick.com`, `*.exosrv.com`, `*.exdynsrv.com`, `*.realsrv.com`); full screen hides the room's ads. Spec: Notion row "Video chat: ExoClick ads".
- **Scale:** one API instance holds the queue and the sockets in memory, which fits the single VPS (decision 21). Running several instances would need the queue and the relay in a shared store (Redis pub/sub keyed by user); it is left out until one instance is not enough.
- **Search (SEO, owner's request 2026-11-08):** the landing and `/rules` are indexed; room, contacts and settings send `noindex`. Every video chat page sends `<meta name="rating" content="adult">` (Google's label for sexual content, so SafeSearch filters it) and keeps ExoClick's verification tag. `metadataBase`, canonical and hreflang are absolute on the video chat's host (`lib/webapp-seo.ts`: `https://videochat.codeqlick.com/{locale}{path}`, x-default the default locale). On that host `robots.txt` disallows the account pages (`/*/room`, `/*/contacts`, `/*/settings`, login and register, the long `/*/videochat/` URLs) and points to its own `sitemap.xml`, which lists the landing and the rules in both languages (`WEBAPP_PUBLIC_PATHS`); other hosts keep the website's. The landing carries keyword-focused titles and text, six reasons, an eight-question FAQ and JSON-LD (`WebApplication` free and 18+, `Organization`, `FAQPage`); `/{locale}/og` is its share image. Spec: Notion row "Video chat: SEO (rank on Google)".
- **Data:** `VideochatProfile` (`userId`, `adultConfirmedAt`, `termsVersion`, `termsAcceptedAt`, `ageVerifiedAt?`, `suspendedAt?`, `suspendedUntil?`, `suspensionReason?`, `nickname?`, `gender?`, `seeking[]`, `country?`, `language?`, `sameCountryOnly`, `sameLanguageOnly`, `interests[]`), `VideochatReport` (`reporterId`, `reportedId`, `reason` MINOR · NON_CONSENSUAL · ILLEGAL · HARASSMENT · HATE · SPAM · OTHER, `details?`, `transcript` JSON, `status` OPEN · DISMISSED · ACTIONED, `reviewedById?`, `reviewedAt?`), `VideochatBlock` (`blockerId`, `blockedId`), `VideochatLike` (`fromId`, `toId`, `createdAt`; mutual when both directions exist), `VideochatSanction` (`userId`, `kind` SUSPENDED · LIFTED, `reason?`, `until?`, `byId?` — null when automatic, `reportId?`), `VideochatAppeal` (`userId`, `sanctionId`, `message`, `status` OPEN · ACCEPTED · REJECTED, `response?`, `reviewedById?`, `reviewedAt?`), `VideochatCallLog` (`startedAt`, `endedAt`, `durationSec`, `waitSec?`; no user), `VideochatActiveDay` (`day`, `userId`), `VideochatPushSubscription` (`userId`, `endpoint` unique, `p256dh`, `auth`, `locale`); `VideochatProfile.pushNotifiedAt?` is when the account was last notified, `peakAlerts` (default false) and `peakNotifiedAt?` the peak time alerts. All cascade with the user.

### 4.8 Meetings (`modules/meetings`)

Codeqlick's second own web app: video meetings by link at **meetings.codeqlick.com** for everyone (no adult content; spec also in the Notion roadmap row "Meetings"). Decision 24.

- **Rooms:** a signed-in account creates rooms (`MeetingRoom`: `code` unique, `ownerId`, `title` 1–80, `lobby` default true, `createdAt`, `lastUsedAt?`; cascades with the user), at most 20. A room is a permanent link, `meetings.codeqlick.com/{locale}/<code>`, until its owner deletes it; the code is `abc-defg-hij` from the letters without i, l and o, read forgivingly (case, spaces and dashes don't matter). Nothing about the meetings held is stored: no media, no chat, no list of who joined; only `lastUsedAt` (when someone enters an empty room).
- **Who:** anyone with the link. Signed-in people join with their account's name (`POST /meetings/rooms/:code/ticket`); guests type a name of 2–40 characters (`POST /meetings/rooms/:code/guest-ticket`, public, 10/min/IP). Tickets are like the video chat's: 32 random bytes, single use, 30 s, kept hashed in memory, with the ICE servers (the video chat's coturn; TURN usernames are `<expiry>:<user id>` or `<expiry>:guest-<random>`).
- **Waiting room:** when `lobby` is on, the owner goes straight in and everyone else knocks (`waiting`); owners inside get `knock { id, name }` and answer `admit { id, accept }` (declined: `declined`, close 4403). Without the owner inside, people wait. The owner turns it off from the room (`lobby { on }`, stored) or the room list (`PATCH`); turning it off lets everyone knocking in. At most 20 knocking.
- **Meeting:** up to **6 people** inside (the 7th gets `full`, close 4409), each connected to each other browser to browser (a WebRTC mesh: media never reaches the server). Whoever enters gets `ready { self, room, peers, knocks }` and makes the offers to everyone already inside; the others get `joined { peer }`. `signal { to, data }` relays SDP and ICE to one person (`signal { from, data }`). Each connection always has an audio and a video slot, so screen sharing replaces the camera track without renegotiating. `media { audio, video, screen }` tells the others what each one sends. Text chat (`chat`, 500 characters, 20 per 10 s) goes to everyone inside and isn't stored. The owner can remove someone (`remove { id }`: `removed`, close 4403; they may knock again) and end the meeting for everyone (`end`: `ended`, close 4410). Deleting the room ends its meeting too.
- **Signaling:** a WebSocket at `/v1/meetings/ws` on the API's HTTP server (Nginx maps `wss://meetings.codeqlick.com/ws`). It accepts only `Origin`s in `MEETINGS_ORIGINS`, at most 10 sockets per IP, 64 KB per message and 400 messages a minute; `auth { ticket, audio?, video? }` within 5 s. Pings every 30 s drop dead sockets. Every gateway sees every upgrade request: each one takes its own path (`common/websocket`), and unknown paths are dropped. Live meetings are in memory (one API instance, like the video chat).

### 4.9 Citas (`modules/citas`)

Codeqlick's third own web app: online bookings at **citas.codeqlick.com** for anyone with a service (a hairdresser, a teacher, a consultant…); spec also in the Notion roadmap row "Citas". Decision 26.

- **Pages:** a signed-in account creates up to 10 booking pages. Each has a public link `citas.codeqlick.com/{locale}/<slug>` (3–40 lowercase letters, digits and dashes, unique; the app's routes such as `editar` and `cancelar`, the sign-in pages, the web apps and the main site's sections are reserved), a title, a description, a duration (15–120 min), where it happens (free text), its own time zone (IANA, default `Europe/Madrid`), weekly free hours in that zone (ranges per weekday, no overlaps), a minimum notice (0–720 h), how far ahead (1–180 days), whether the phone is required, and an on/off switch (a paused page keeps its link but takes no bookings). With **video**, the page gets a Meetings room (section 4.8) owned by the same person, and every appointment carries its link.
- **Free times:** the page's weekly hours cut into appointments of its duration (the same `freeSlots` as the website's calls, generalised with a time zone), after the notice and within the horizon, minus the owner's confirmed appointments on **any** of their pages (one person can't be in two places).
- **Booking:** public, 5/min/IP, with a honeypot (and the web treats a form filled in under 3 s as one: a fake success, nothing stored). Inside a transaction with a per-owner advisory lock, the time must still be free; otherwise `409`. Both sides get an email with an iCalendar invitation (`METHOD:REQUEST`); the visitor's has a cancel link `citas.codeqlick.com/{locale}/cancelar?token=…` (32 random bytes, stored as its SHA-256).
- **Reminder:** about 24 h before each confirmed appointment, whoever booked gets a reminder email in their language (when, where, the video link, reply-to the owner). It can't carry the cancel link (only its hash is stored), so it points to the confirmation email's link and the booking page. `CitasRemindersService` runs every 15 minutes in the API process (off in tests) and sends for appointments starting in 2–24 h, skipping those booked less than 26 h ahead (their confirmation is fresh). Each reminder is claimed by setting `Appointment.reminderSentAt` before sending, so it goes out once.
- **Cancelling:** the visitor from that link (shows the appointment first; past appointments can't be cancelled), or the owner from their list. Either way both sides are emailed with `METHOD:CANCEL` and a higher `SEQUENCE`. Deleting a page cancels its upcoming appointments first.
- **Privacy:** pages are `noindex`; the owner sees their visitors' name, email, phone and note; the account export (`/auth/me/export`) includes the person's pages and their appointments.

### 4.10 Swipe (`modules/swipe`)

Codeqlick's fourth own web app, at **swipe.codeqlick.com**: adults share intimate photos of themselves, others swipe them (right likes, left skips), and two people who liked a photo of each other match and can chat. Spec in the Notion roadmap row "Swipe 18+". Decision 27. It is listed on the site's `/apps` page with an 18+ warning (owner's request, 2026-11-08); since 2026-11-09 (owner's request) its header and its favicon (the site's, which Google shows in results) carry the Codeqlick logo, and its public pages (`/`, `/rules`, `/takedown`) are indexed like the video chat's: canonical and hreflang on its own host, Open Graph image at `/{locale}/og`, `rating: adult` so SafeSearch filters it, its own sitemap and a robots.txt that keeps out `/deck`, `/photos`, `/matches`, the images and the sign-in pages, which also send `noindex`.

- **Entering:** signed-in accounts only. Like the video chat: date of birth checked ≥ 18 and **not stored** (only its year, since round 2), plus the rules (`SWIPE_TERMS_VERSION`; a new version asks again). No identity document: the owner chose declared age (2026-11-08), so the safeguards below rest on the team's review.
- **Verification photo before uploading:** to upload, a person first sends a photo of themselves holding a paper with their nickname, "Swipe" and the date. The team approves or rejects it (`/admin/swipe`); only the team sees it, and a rejected one is deleted. Moderators reject anyone who doesn't clearly look adult.
- **Verified badge and your week (owner's request 2026-11-09):** cards, the matches list and the chat header show a fuchsia tick next to a nickname whose verification photo was approved (`verified` on cards and match partners; since only verified people can upload, every card has it). `/photos` starts with the person's week once they can upload (`GET /swipe/stats`): views (any vote on their photos), likes, «me encanta» and new matches, for the last 7 days and the 7 before (index `swipe_votes(owner_id, created_at)`).
- **Photos:** up to 10 active per person, 10 uploads a day, JPEG/PNG/WebP up to 10 MB with explicit consent ("only I appear, I am 18+, I took it or let it be taken, I want it here"). The browser shrinks them first (≤ 1600 px, JPEG ≤ 900 KB, because the apps' Nginx sites take 1 MB). The API re-encodes with sharp (rotation applied, ≤ 1600 px, WebP, **metadata stripped**: no camera data or location), computes a 64-bit dHash and refuses images within 6 bits of a blocked hash. Files live in `SWIPE_UPLOADS_DIR` as `<uuid>.webp` (mode 0600); a cleanup every 6 hours deletes files no row points to (deleted photos and accounts). Every photo starts **PENDING** and only the team publishes it (LIVE), comparing it with the verification photo.
- **Seeing photos:** `GET /swipe/photos/:id/file` streams one to its owner, an admin, or a person who can see it in the deck (LIVE, not blocked either way, owner not suspended), otherwise `404`. The web proxies it (`/{locale}/swipe/img/<id>`) with `Cache-Control: private`. Cards show the nickname, gender, interests, the photo's ref and a watermark overlay (nickname · swipe.codeqlick.com); images can't be dragged or long-pressed.
- **Deck and matches:** 10 LIVE photos at a time, never my own, already voted, blocked or from a suspended owner, filtered by whom I want to see (`seeking`). A like counts on the photo; when the owner has also liked one of my photos, a match is created (once) and returned with the vote. A match's chat is text only (≤ 1000 characters, 20/min), the web polls every 4 s; ending a match deletes its chat, blocking also hides both people from each other.
- **Reports:** from a card or a chat (MINOR · NON_CONSENSUAL · STOLEN · ILLEGAL · HARASSMENT · SPAM · OTHER, 20 a day). MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL and STOLEN **hide the photo at once** (HIDDEN) until reviewed, and MINOR emails the team. From a chat the last 30 messages are saved with the report. The team dismisses (a hidden photo goes back live if nothing else is open) or acts: remove the photo, block its image, suspend the person (24 h, 7 d, 30 d or permanent).
- **Removal requests (DSA):** a public form at `/{locale}/swipe/takedown`, no account needed (5/min/IP, honeypot): email, the photo's ref (optional), reason and details. A matching ref hides the photo at once; the team is emailed and the person gets an acknowledgement. Answered from `/admin/swipe` within 24 hours: remove (deletes the photo and blocks its image) or close (restores it); the person is emailed the answer in their language.
- **Privacy:** the date of birth isn't stored (only its year, for the age filter); files only reach browsers through the access checks; the account export includes the Swipe profile, photos (refs and statuses), likes given, messages sent and reports made; deleting the account deletes everything (files within 6 hours).
- **Improvements, round 2 (owner's request, 2026-11-08):**
  - **Emails to people** (in their account's language, never with a photo, a nickname or message text, so a glance at the inbox gives nothing away): verification approved or rejected (with the team's note), photo published or rejected (with the reason), a new match (to the person who didn't make it), a «me encanta» on one of my photos, and unread messages: a check every 10 minutes emails once per match when the partner's messages have waited 15 minutes unread, and not again until I read them (`SwipeMatch.notifiedAAt/BAt`). A profile switch turns off the match, «me encanta» and message emails (`emailAlerts`, on by default); the team's decisions are always mailed (the reason for a removal is owed under the DSA).
  - **Team digest:** every 30 minutes, when something new is waiting (verifications, photos, open reports, removal requests) since the last digest, `LEADS_NOTIFY_TO` gets one email with the counts and the link to `/admin/swipe`, at most once an hour. MINOR reports and removal requests still email at once.
  - **Own legal texts:** `/{locale}/swipe/terms-of-use` and `/{locale}/swipe/privacy-policy` (owner from `LEGAL_OWNER_*`, like the site's). The privacy policy names the special category data (sex life, GDPR art. 9.2.a: explicit consent at upload), what is kept and for how long, OVHcloud as the only processor, and the rights; both are linked from the entry screen, the footer and the rules. `SWIPE_TERMS_VERSION` moves to `2026-11-08.2`, so everyone accepts them on their next visit (which also stores the birth year of people who entered before).
  - **Phone app and notifications:** on the Swipe host the manifest installs "Swipe" (its own icon, no Codeqlick logo) and `/swipe-sw.js` shows Web Push notifications for a new match, a new message and a «me encanta», with the same VAPID keys as the video chat (off without them). Subscriptions in `SwipePushSubscription` (`POST/DELETE /swipe/push`); the profile has the switch.
  - **Filters:** besides whom I want to see (`seeking`), an age range (18–99) and «only my country». The age comes from the **birth year only** (`birthYear`, stored from the date of birth on entry; people who entered before set it in their profile); people without it only appear when the range is the full one. The country is optional (`country`, ISO code from a fixed list).
  - **Richer profile:** a sentence about me (`bio`, ≤ 160 characters) shown on cards and chats, with the interests that already exist.
  - **Undo and «me encanta»:** `POST /swipe/votes/undo` undoes my last vote when it is under 10 minutes old and didn't make a match (a like is subtracted). A vote can be a «me encanta» (`SwipeVote.superLike`, 3 a day): the owner is told (email and push) and the photos of whoever loved one of theirs come first in their deck, marked.
  - **Watermark in the file:** photos are stored with the nickname, the photo's ref and swipe.codeqlick.com burnt into a corner (the hash is taken before, so blocking still works). Photos stored before are marked once by the background task (`SwipePhoto.watermarked`).
  - **Safer chat:** until each person has sent 5 messages in a match, a message with a phone number, an email, a link or an outside app or handle (WhatsApp, Telegram, Instagram, Snapchat, OnlyFans, @name…) is refused with an explanation (scams and spam). Ending a match stays in the chat's menu.
  - **Blurred photos:** a profile switch (`blurPhotos`) shows the deck's and the chats' photos blurred until tapped.
- **Private album (owner's choice 2026-11-09):** besides the 10 public photos, up to 5 photos in a private album (`SwipePhoto.inAlbum`). They are reviewed like any photo, never appear in the deck (and can't be voted), and only people matched with the owner see them: the chat shows a closed «Álbum privado de …» with the thumbnails (blurred when the viewer chose blurred photos), the matches list shows how many there are (`partner.albumPhotos`), and the file route lets a viewer through only with a match. A photo moves into or out of the album from «Mis fotos» without a new review; reports, takedowns and admin tools work on album photos as on any other (the admin lists tag them).
- **Ads (ExoClick, like the video chat, 4.7):** the same 300x250 banner tag, from a separate ExoClick site for swipe.codeqlick.com with its own verification tag. Two zones (`SWIPE_AD_ZONES` in `lib/videochat-ads.ts`): `deck` under the deck, `pages` at the bottom of the landing and of the matches list; never inside a chat or over a photo. `AdZone` takes `app="swipe"`; a zone without an ID renders nothing, so the release that fills the IDs turns them on. Swipe's host gets the same CSP as the video chat's (ExoClick's hosts in `script-src`, `connect-src`, `frame-src`).

### 4.11 Errors log (`modules/errors`)

Owner's choice (2026-11-09): know about a bug before a person writes in, without an outside service. Unexpected errors from three places are grouped in `ErrorGroup` and shown in Admin → Errors:

- **API:** `ProblemDetailsFilter` records every error that is not an `HttpException` (the ones that become a generic 500), with the request's method, path, request id and user agent.
- **Website server:** `src/instrumentation.ts` (`onRequestError`) posts every error Next.js reports while rendering, in a Server Action or a route handler to `POST /errors` (`source: server`, with the digest the visitor's error page shows). Production only; an `ApiError` with a 4xx (the visitor's doing) or 503 (the API down, which the server monitor alerts on) is left out.
- **Visitors' browsers:** `components/layout/ErrorReporter` (in the root layout) listens for uncaught errors and promise rejections, and the error pages (`[locale]/error.tsx`, `(site)/error.tsx`) report what they catch. Errors with a digest came from the server and are skipped; so is noise nobody can fix (`lib/error-report.ts`: extensions, cross-origin scripts such as the ads, network failures, chunks of an older release). At most 5 reports per page load, through a Server Action (the BFF) that forwards the visitor's IP. Production only.

**Grouping:** the fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the source, the message without ids, numbers, URLs and quoted values, and the first stack frame of the app's own code without line numbers or bundle hashes, so the same bug stays one group across occurrences and releases. A repeat adds to `count` and updates the last occurrence's details.

**Alerts:** a new group, or a resolved one that happens again (it opens again), emails the team inbox (`LEADS_NOTIFY_TO`, English) with the message, where, and a link to it; at most 10 alert emails an hour. Later repeats only count.

**Limits:** `POST /errors` is public (the website calls it server-side), validated and limited to 30/min/IP; past 300 open groups no new ones are created. Resolved groups not seen for 30 days are deleted.

**Content Security Policy reports (decision 109):** browsers report what the website's CSP blocked (or would block, while it is report-only: section 6.4) to the website's `/api/csp-report`, which forwards them to `POST /csp-reports` (public, 20/min/IP, the visitor's IP forwarded for the limit only). The body is either one `report-uri` report (`{ "csp-report": { … } }`) or a Reporting API list (up to 20 items; only `type: csp-violation` is read). Each violation counts in a group of source `CSP` whose fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the directive (`effective-directive`, else the name of `violated-directive`) and what was blocked: the **origin** of a blocked URL (never its path or query), or `inline`, `eval`, `wasm-eval`, `data`, `blob`, `trusted-types-*`. The message reads `script-src-elem blocked https://example.com`; the sample of the last one keeps the page's path (no query) and, in `stack`, the source file's origin and path with its line and column, the first 40 characters of `script-sample` and the disposition (`report` or `enforce`), plus the user agent. **No IP is stored.** Dropped as noise: reports caused by browser extensions (a blocked URL or source file with `chrome-extension:`, `moz-extension:`, `safari-extension:`, `safari-web-extension:`, `ms-browser-extension:`) and anything without a directive. CSP groups never send the alert email (an extension or an old open tab can produce many) and have their own cap of 100 open groups, so they can never use up the 300 of real errors; past 600 violations a minute in all, the rest of that minute is dropped. Admin → Errors lists them like the others, labelled "CSP".

### 4.12 Website check (`modules/site-audits`)

Owner's choice (2026-11-10, roadmap "Free website check"): a free check of anyone's website that brings in clients for the basic website and for improvements. A visitor gives their website's address, their email and (optionally) their name on `/website-check`; the API checks the site in the background and emails them a report in their language with the score, the main problems in plain words and a link to the full report (`/website-check/<id>`), which also invites them to ask for a basic website or write to us. The team inbox gets an email per check, and each check is a lead (`projectType: web`, `company` = the host, the message a summary with the report's link) so it can be followed up; it is not acknowledged like a contact form lead (the report is the answer).

**What is checked** (each check is `pass`, `warn` or `fail`; a category's score is the share of points, a warn counting half; the score is the average of the four):

- **Speed:** time to the first byte of the page (< 0.6 s pass, < 1.5 s warn), the page's HTML weight (< 150 KB, < 500 KB), compression (gzip or Brotli), scripts in `<head>` that block the page (≤ 2, ≤ 5) and, with `PAGESPEED_API_KEY`, Google's mobile performance score (≥ 90, ≥ 50) and Largest Contentful Paint (≤ 2.5 s, ≤ 4 s).
- **Mobile:** a `viewport` with `width=device-width`, zoom not blocked (`user-scalable=no` or `maximum-scale=1`), the page's language (`<html lang>`) and an icon for phones' home screens (`apple-touch-icon`).
- **Google:** title (10–65 characters), meta description (50–160), exactly one `<h1>`, images with `alt` (none missing; ≤ 20 % warn), canonical link, not hidden from search engines (`noindex` fails), `robots.txt` and a sitemap (`/sitemap.xml` or listed in `robots.txt`), Open Graph title and image for sharing, structured data (JSON-LD).
- **Security:** HTTPS, `http://` sending to `https://`, the certificate valid with more than 14 days left, HSTS, the protective headers (`Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, frame protection by `X-Frame-Options` or `frame-ancestors`, `Referrer-Policy`), no mixed content (`http://` scripts, styles, images or frames on an HTTPS page) and no software versions on show (`Server`, `X-Powered-By`, `<meta name=generator>`).

The texts of every check (title and advice, Spanish and English) live in the API (`site-audit-texts.ts`), so the email and the report page say the same.

**Safety:** only `http`/`https` on ports 80 and 443, no credentials in the URL; every connection's address is resolved by the API and refused when it is private, loopback, link-local, shared, multicast or reserved (IPv4 and IPv6), so the check can't reach the server's own network; the resolved address is the one connected to (no second lookup). At most 5 redirects, each one checked again; 15 s per request, the page read up to 3 MB. Requests say `Codeqlick-WebsiteCheck/1.0 (+https://codeqlick.com/website-check)`.

**Limits:** `POST /site-audits` is limited to 3/min/IP and 5 checks a day per email; the same email and host within 24 hours returns the earlier check instead of a new one. Checks run one at a time; a check that can't reach the site ends `FAILED` and the visitor is emailed that we couldn't open it (with the reason in plain words). The report page shows no email or name.

### 4.13 Codeqlick Cloud (`modules/cloud`)

Owner's choice (2026-11-12): developer tools in the spirit of AWS, Vercel or GitHub, built in phases from what the platform already runs. **Codeqlick Cloud** is a console at **cloud.codeqlick.com** (a web app of section 6.3, same accounts), a public HTTP API under `/v1/cloud`, and a command-line tool (`codeqlick-cli/`, the `codeqlick` command). Roadmap rows "Cloud: …" in Notion; decisions 32–36.

**Phases.** Each phase ships on its own and only starts when the one before works in production.

| Phase | Services | Runs code from users? | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Console, teams, API tokens and CLI login; **static hosting** (deployments, previews, rollbacks); custom domains | No | The hosting domain (`CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN`, decision 33) and its wildcard certificate (8.3) |
| 1b | The platform's existing tools opened to developers: forms for static sites (`site-forms`), uptime and cron heartbeats with public status pages (`monitoring`), the website check as an API and a GitHub Action (`site-audits`) | No | Phase 1 |
| 2 | Builds from Git (specified below, decisions 40–42 and 51); build notifications, web analytics and a key-value store (decisions 43–45); plans and billing through Stripe, managed PostgreSQL and S3-compatible object storage (decisions 46–48, for now on the production VPS) | Yes (builds) | Stripe live (decision 20); a build host (for now the production VPS, every build sandboxed by gVisor; a separate server once builds are frequent, decision 41); storage servers or OVH Object Storage |
| 3 | Containers and apps (long-running processes, like Heroku or Render) | Yes | Several servers with microVM isolation and someone on call |

Git hosting itself is left to GitHub (or a self-hosted Forgejo): developers already keep their code there, and phase 2 connects to it. Phases 2 and 3 are only outlined here; each gets its full spec before its code.

#### Teams and projects

- **Team:** what owns projects. `name`, `slug` (3–30, unique; the console's URLs), a `plan` (only `FREE` until phase 2). Anyone signed in creates a team, accepting the Cloud terms (`/terms-of-use` on the console, versioned like Swipe's: the member row stores the version and when). A person belongs to at most 10 teams and owns at most 3.
- **Terms:** a new `CLOUD_TERMS_VERSION` makes every member accept again (decision 105; first with `2026-11-17`). `GET /cloud/me` says `termsVersion` (the current one) and `termsAccepted` (false while any of the caller's memberships has an older version; true with no teams). While it is false the console shows only "The Cloud terms changed": a link to them and "Accept" (`POST /cloud/terms/accept { acceptTerms: true, termsVersion }`, session only, which updates `termsVersion` and `termsAcceptedAt` on all the caller's memberships; `409` for an old version). API tokens and the CLI keep working meanwhile, so deploys from CI never stop over a terms change; the console is where people read and accept.
- **Members and roles:** `OWNER` (everything, including deleting the team and transferring ownership; at least one), `ADMIN` (members, invitations and projects), `MEMBER` (deploys, domains and reading everything). Up to 10 members per team. Invitations go by email (link valid 7 days, `cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/invite?token=…`, the token stored as SHA-256) and are accepted by a signed-in account **with that email** (forwarded links don't work for someone else), accepting the terms. Owners and admins remove members; anyone leaves a team (not its last owner).
- **Requiring two-step verification:** a team's owners can require two-step verification (section 4.3) of every member (`requireTwoFactor`, off by default; decision 57), like GitHub organisations or Vercel teams. Turning it on is refused (`409 Turn on two-step verification on your own account first`) unless the owner turning it on has it. While it is on, a member whose account has no two-step verification gets `403 This team requires two-step verification` on every team route, through a session or an API token alike (checked by `CloudTeamsService.access` / `accessById` with the account's state read from the database, so turning it on or setting it up takes effect at once). Only three things stay open to them: seeing the team itself (`GET /cloud/teams/:team`, which then says `twoFactorBlocked: true`), the teams list (`GET /cloud/me`, which also carries `twoFactorBlocked`), and leaving the team. Turning it on or off is recorded (`team.two_factor_required`, details `{ enabled }`). The members list shows who has two-step verification; invitations to such a team (email and the invite page) say it is required. A member who later turns two-step verification off is simply blocked again until they set it up; the owners are not emailed. Team emails chosen by membership (form submissions, monitor alerts, build notifications) keep reaching a blocked member, since they only go to their own confirmed address; the console's token form leaves blocked teams out.
  - **Console:** the team's **Members** page has the switch (owners only; others see whether it is on) and a column "Two-step verification" (yes / no). A blocked member sees, on every page of the team, only a banner "This team requires two-step verification" with a link to set it up in My account (`/portal/account` on the main host) and the button to leave the team.
- **Project:** one deployable thing. `name`, `slug` (3–40 lowercase letters, digits and single dashes, unique across Cloud: it is the project's address `<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`; reserved: `www`, `api`, `app`, `admin`, `cloud`, `console`, `docs`, `status`, `mail`, `cdn`, `static`, `assets`, `codeqlick` and the web apps' names), `productionDeploymentId?`, `suspendedAt?` / `suspendedReason?`. Up to 10 per team. Deleting a project takes its sites down and deletes its deployments and domains at once; the row stays 30 days (`deletedAt`) so nobody else can take its address while old links still point at it, then the slug is free.
- **Moving a project to another team** (roadmap proposal of 2026-11-14, decision 58): a studio builds a site in its own team and hands it to the client's team without a new project, a new address or deploying again. `POST /v1/cloud/projects/:id/transfer` `{ team: '<target slug>' }`, with a signed-in session only (like members and teams; a token gets `401`).
  - **Who:** an `OWNER` of the project's team who is also `OWNER` or `ADMIN` of the target team.
  - **Checks, in this order:** the project (live, not deleted) and the caller's role in its team: `404` "Project not found" (unknown or deleted) or "Not found" (not a member), `403` "Only a team owner can do this"; the target team: `404` "Team not found", `403` "Only a team admin can do this"; the same team: `400` "The project is already in that team"; suspended: `403` "This project is suspended"; a `QUEUED` or `RUNNING` build: `409` "Wait for the project's builds to finish" (or cancel them). Then the target team must have room for what counts against its limits (its plan's `CLOUD_PLANS`), each a `409`:
    - projects: "A team has at most <n> projects";
    - storage (the project's kept deployments added to the team's): "The team has <free> MB of storage left; this project's deployments take <size> MB";
    - monitors (those tied to the project, added to the team's): "A team has at most <n> monitors";
    - what the project holds against the target plan's per-project limits (domains, forms, variables, key-value store, databases, buckets) is not checked: as when a team goes back to FREE (Plans and billing), what is over keeps working and nothing more can be added.
  - **One transaction:** the project's row and both teams' rows are locked (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, in id order), the project, both memberships and every limit above are read again inside it (a project deleted, suspended or moved meanwhile: `409` "The project changed; try again"), then everything below is written. Nothing is copied on disk: deployments live under `deployments/<id>`, addresses under `hosts/<slug>…` and `domains/<domain>` in `CLOUD_ROOT`, databases and buckets by their own names in `cloud-pg` and Garage, and none of them names the team. The disk guard doesn't apply.
  - **What moves with it** (rows of the project, unchanged): its address and slug, deployments and production, domains, forms and their submissions and keys, environment variables, builds and their logs, pull request comments, notification channels (Slack, Discord and webhook ones as they are), analytics and its switch, the key-value store, databases (same connection strings and passwords) and buckets with their access keys. The month's builds, deployments and form submissions of the project count for the target team from then on (they are counted through the project).
  - **What is rewritten:**
    - `CloudProject.teamId`;
    - `CloudProject.productionChecks` is switched off and `productionCheckNextAt` cleared (decision 81): the history stays behind with the source team, so the target team's admins switch the weekly check on again;
    - the team's monitors tied to the project (`CloudMonitor.projectId`): their `teamId`, with their checks, events and heartbeat URLs; the source team's status page drops them from its `items`;
    - `CloudAnalyticsEvent.teamId` of all the project's events, after the commit, in batches of 10,000, the current Madrid month first (until they are moved, the month's events still count for the source team), then the older ones; the analytics counts cached for both teams are dropped;
    - the people lists: `CloudForm.recipientIds`, `CloudNotifyChannel.recipientIds` (email channels) and the moved monitors' `alertIds` keep only members of the target team; a list left empty becomes the person who moved it. (Sending already skips people who aren't members, so this keeps the console's lists true and nobody without a seat gets the target team's mail.)
  - **Git:** an installation belongs to one team, so the link is kept only when one of the target team's installations (not suspended) can see the repository (`gitRepoId`, through the installation's repositories, before the transaction): `gitInstallationId` becomes that installation's (and `gitRepo` its current name). Otherwise `gitInstallationId`, `gitRepoId` and `gitRepo` are cleared as by "Disconnect" (the build settings and variables stay), and the target team's admins connect it again in the **Git** tab. GitHub not set up or not answering counts as not seen.
  - **What stays behind:** the source team's events (the project's past), website checks (`CloudAudit`, the team's, the project's scheduled ones included), monitors not tied to the project, and abuse reports (`CloudReport`, by project, unchanged). Tokens restricted to the source team no longer reach the project (`404`); a folder's `codeqlick.json` still names the old team, so `codeqlick` answers "There is no project … in the team …" until `codeqlick init` is run again (or `team` edited).
  - **After the commit:** the target plan's connection limit on the project's databases and its bucket quotas follow within 10 minutes, set by the regular sweeps (`CloudDatabasesService.check`, `CloudStorageService.sync`). Events `project.transferred_out` in the source team and `project.transferred_in` in the target, both with the project's slug as label and details `{ from, to, monitors, git: 'kept' | 'dropped' | 'none' }` (team slugs). The target team's owners (except the person who moved it) get an email in their language (plain text and branded HTML): who moved which project from which team, its address, that its domains, forms and data came with it, whether the Git link was dropped, and a button to the project in the console.
  - **Answer:** `200 CloudProject` (its `team` now the target) with `git: 'kept' | 'dropped' | 'none'`.
  - **Console:** the project's settings gain "Move to another team", shown to the team's owners: a select of the caller's other teams where they are `OWNER` or `ADMIN` (from `/cloud/me`; with none, a line saying so), what moves and what changes (Git, tokens restricted to this team, `codeqlick.json`), a confirmation typing the project's slug, then a redirect to `/console/<target>/<project>` with a notice when the Git link was dropped.
- **Events:** every change in a team is recorded (`CloudEvent`: who, through which token if any, `action` such as `project.created`, `deployment.created`, `deployment.promoted`, `deployment.kept`, `deployment.unkept`, `domain.added`, `member.invited`, `token.created`, a label, details and the IP) and listed newest first in the console. Kept 365 days. Admin actions on Cloud also go to the platform's activity log.
- **Exporting events:** a team's admins and owners download its events as CSV (`GET /cloud/teams/:team/events.csv`, session or token): optional `from` and `to` (dates, `YYYY-MM-DD`, Madrid days, inclusive; at most 365 days apart; default the last 30 days) and `action` (an exact action such as `domain.added`, or a prefix ending in `.` such as `domain.`). Columns: `date` (UTC, ISO 8601), `actor`, `token`, `action`, `target`, `details` (JSON), `ip`; newest first. Written as it is read, 1,000 rows at a time (a busy year never sits in memory), UTF-8 with BOM, cells that start like a formula (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab, carriage return) get a leading `'`, like the leads export. File name `<team>-events-<from>-<to>.csv`. 10 exports an hour per IP (`@Throttle`; `429`). Exporting isn't recorded as an event.
  - **Console:** the team's **Events** page gets the period (from, to) and action filters, applied to the list too, and "Export CSV" with the same filters.

#### Authentication for machines

- **API tokens:** `cqk_` + 32 random bytes (base64url). Shown once; only the SHA-256 and the first 12 characters (to recognise it) are stored. A token belongs to the person who made it and acts as them, with their role in each team, optionally **restricted to one team**. Expiry 30, 90 or 365 days or never; the console shows when and from where (IP) it was last used. Tokens deploy and manage projects, deployments and domains; members, invitations, tokens and teams themselves need a signed-in session. Up to 20 tokens per person. Revoking deletes it. Tokens only open `/v1/cloud/*`: anywhere else they get `401`. Deactivating an account makes its tokens useless at once (checked on every request).
- **Expiry emails** (owner's roadmap item, 2026-11-14): a token that expires is announced to its person, so CI pipelines don't break silently. **Warning:** a token with an expiry that has 7 days or less left gets one email, when it was used or created in the last 30 days (a forgotten, unused token isn't worth an email); `expiryWarnedAt` marks it so it is never sent twice. Tokens of a deactivated account are skipped. **Expired:** when the hourly cleanup deletes expired tokens, those used in the last 7 days are announced first in an "expired" email (the token no longer exists afterwards, so this is the last moment); unused ones are deleted quietly. One email per person per run lists all their tokens in that state: name, first 12 characters, team restriction (or "all teams"), expiry date (Madrid time) and last use, with a button to the console's tokens page (`cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/console/tokens`) to make a new one and a reminder to update `CODEQLICK_TOKEN` where it is used. It is sent in the person's language, as plain text plus branded HTML. It runs inside the hourly Cloud cleanup (`CloudTokensService.cleanup`), off in tests like the rest. The console's tokens list marks a token that expires within 7 days ("Expires in N days", warning colour). No API change: `TokenView` is unchanged.
- **Access level** (proposed by the agent team, 2026-11-14; decision 61): a token is **Full** (the default) or **Read only**, chosen when it is made and never changed afterwards (make a new token instead). Existing tokens stay Full. A read-only token may only call `GET`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` on `@CloudApi()` routes; any other method answers `403` (4.4) "This token is read only". The rule is the method alone, with no exceptions per route: `POST`s that look like reads (`/domains/:domainId/check`, `/audits`, a notification channel's `/test`) change or create something, so they are refused too. CLI device-flow tokens ("CLI · <machine>") are always Full; signed-in sessions are unaffected. The `token.created` event (recorded in the team the token is restricted to; an unrestricted token has no team to record it in) records `readOnly` in its details.
- **Sending a token:** `Authorization: Bearer cqk_…`. `JwtAuthGuard` sends anything starting with `cqk_` to `CloudTokensService` (routes marked `@CloudApi()` only); the request's user then carries `via: 'token'` and `token: { id, name, teamId, readOnly }`. The read-only check happens there too, right after the token is resolved, so every `@CloudApi()` route, including ones added later, is covered without code of its own.
- **CLI sign-in (device flow, like RFC 8628):** `codeqlick login` asks `POST /cloud/cli/device` for a `deviceCode` (secret, kept by the CLI) and a `userCode` (`ABCD-EFGH`, no ambiguous letters), opens `cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/cli?code=ABCD-EFGH` and polls `POST /cloud/cli/device/token` every 5 s. On that page the signed-in person sees the code, the machine's name (sent by the CLI) and when it was asked for, and approves or denies. Approving creates a 90-day token named "CLI · <machine>", handed to the CLI once on its next poll. Codes expire after 10 minutes and are deleted after a day. For CI, a token made in the console goes in `CODEQLICK_TOKEN` instead.

#### Static hosting

- **Deployment:** an immutable upload of a built folder (the output of `astro build`, `vite build`, `next export`, plain HTML…). `codeqlick deploy [dir]` packs the folder as `tar.gz` (dotfiles left out except `.well-known/`; `.codeqlickignore` lines with `*` wildcards left out too) and sends it to `POST /cloud/projects/:id/deployments` as multipart (`bundle` plus `target` `preview` or `production`, and optionally `message`, `commit`, `branch`, all shown in the console, and `config`, the project's `codeqlick.json`: see "Redirects, rewrites and headers"). The API checks and unpacks it into its own folder before answering:
  - only regular files and folders (links, devices and anything else refused); relative paths without `..`, each part 1–255 bytes of printable characters; at most 10,000 files, 100 MB unpacked and 50 MB uploaded;
  - an `index.html` at the root;
  - the team's storage (the sum of its kept deployments) stays under its plan's limit, and 30 deployments an hour per team.
  Anything refused answers `400` (or `413` for size, `429` for the rate) with the reason, and nothing is kept. Each deployment records its size, file count, who made it and with which token.
- **Addresses:** every deployment answers at `https://<slug>--<short id>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` (`short id`: its first 8 hexadecimal characters) for as long as it is kept; the production deployment also at `https://<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` and the project's verified domains. A `production` deployment becomes production as soon as it is unpacked. **Promote** makes any kept deployment production (that is how a rollback works: `codeqlick rollback` promotes the one before). Switching is atomic (a link replaced by a rename), so visitors never see half a site.
  - **Branch address:** a preview deployment with a `branch` (Git build or `codeqlick deploy --branch`) also gets a fixed address `https://<slug>--b-<part>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` that always serves the **newest preview deployment of that branch**. `part` is the branch lowercased, every run of characters outside `[a-z0-9]` replaced by one `-`, leading and trailing `-` trimmed (`feat/New_Thing` → `feat-new-thing`). The whole label stays within 63 characters: when `part` is empty or longer than `63 - slug.length - 4`, it is cut to that budget minus 7 characters (trailing `-` trimmed) and followed by `-` and the first 6 hexadecimal characters of the branch's SHA-256. Branches whose names give the same label (`feature/x`, `feature-x`) share the address: it serves the newest preview of any of them. The project's production branch (`gitBranch`, default `main`) gets none: it is production, and changing the production branch removes the new one's address and gives the old one its own. The link is set with the new deployment's own; when a deployment is deleted by hand or by the hourly cleanup, the branch address is recomputed from the database and points at the newest remaining preview of that branch, or is removed when none is left (the row is deleted and the address re-pointed before the files go, and a relink checks again after writing so a slower one can't leave an older deployment linked). A suspended project's branch addresses show the suspended page like its other addresses, and are restored when it is lifted; deleting the project removes them. Branch addresses are not kept apart from deployments: they are links like the others (8.3).
- **Serving:** Nginx serves the files straight from disk (8.3): `index.html` for folders, `<path>.html` without the extension, `404.html` when it exists for anything missing, and `200.html` (when it exists) for every missing path instead, which is how single-page apps route. Assets under `/_astro/`, `/assets/` and `/_next/static/` and any file with a content hash in its name are cached for a year; HTML is revalidated. Every response carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and `Strict-Transport-Security`; HTTP goes to HTTPS. Deployment addresses (the `--` ones) answer with `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`. A `codeqlick.json` can change how paths are answered (redirects, rewrites, headers, clean URLs, trailing slashes): see below. A project without production, suspended or deleted answers a Codeqlick page "nothing here" (404) or "suspended" (451).
- **Status badge:** every project has a public SVG badge for READMEs, `GET /cloud/badges/<slug>.svg`: `codeqlick` on the left and, on the right, `building` (blue) while its latest production build is queued or running, `failed` (red) when that build failed and no production deployment was made after it (a CLI deploy after a failed build clears it), else `deployed` (green) when it has a production deployment and `not deployed` (gray) when it has none. Canceled builds don't count. A deleted or suspended project answers the gray `unknown`, exactly like a slug that doesn't exist (same status code and headers). The badge is public by nature: whether a slug is taken is already visible when creating a project, and the production state is what the site itself shows. Cached 60 s in the API and by browsers (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=60`); `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin` so other sites can show it. The SVG is drawn from fixed texts and brand colours (no font files; widths from a fixed per-character estimate, like the status page badge). The project's settings tab in the console shows it and the Markdown to copy, linking to the production address.
- **Files and comparison:** any member (or token) can list what a kept deployment contains and compare it with production or another kept deployment of the same project, without downloading anything; file contents are never served through these routes. The list is every file's relative path and size, sorted by path (UTF-8 byte order); it holds the first 10,000, while `count` and `totalBytes` cover every file. A comparison gives `added` (in this deployment, not in the other), `removed` (in the other only) and `changed` (in both, with a different size or SHA-256), each sorted by path with sizes, plus the count of unchanged files and both totals. Hashes are only computed for non-empty files of equal size, streamed from disk, and kept in memory per deployment (deployments are immutable): at most 50 deployments and about 64 MB, least recently used dropped first, and a deployment's hashes dropped when it is deleted; the same file is never hashed twice at once and at most two comparisons run at a time, so comparing again is instant. Comparing with production when the project has none answers `409`; comparing a deployment with itself is allowed (everything unchanged). Read from the deployment's folder on disk, so a deployment whose files are gone answers `404`. Console: a deployment's page has a **Files** tab (the list with a filter box, sizes, totals) and, unless it is production, **Compare with production** (the three groups, collapsed when empty). CLI 0.5: `codeqlick files <deployment> [--json]` and `codeqlick diff <deployment> [other] [--json]` (`other` defaults to production; a deployment is given by its id or short id, as for `promote`). No change to storage, limits or plans.
- **Production lock after a rollback:** promoting a deployment older than the current production one (console, `codeqlick rollback` or `promote`, by a person or a token) **locks** production (`productionLockedAt`, `productionLockedById`; promoting another older one while locked keeps the first lock's date and person), so the bug just rolled back can't come back on its own. While locked, production **builds from Git** (a push to the production branch, "Deploy again" on it) still build and deploy, but as previews: the deployment keeps `target` `PRODUCTION` "as uploaded" but production doesn't move, the build's log ends with "Production is locked after a rollback: promote this deployment to publish it", the build is marked `keptAsPreview`, its commit status and pull-request comment link the preview, and its build notifications say the preview is ready and production is locked instead of "published". Explicit releases by a person or token are honoured: `codeqlick deploy --prod` (an upload with `target` `production`) and promoting a deployment newer than the current production one make it production and **unlock**. Admins (also through their tokens) can unlock without publishing anything; the next production build then goes live as usual. The console shows a banner on the project ("Production is locked to <short id> since <date>, by <name>") with **Unlock** (ADMIN) and marks builds that were kept as previews (`keptAsPreview` on `CloudBuild` in the API); the team's events record `project.production_locked` and `project.production_unlocked`. CLI 0.5: `codeqlick rollback` and `promote` say when production is now (or still) locked, and `codeqlick unlock` unlocks.
- **Kept deployments:** the last 20 of each project not kept by hand, plus its production one and the ones kept by hand; older ones are deleted with their files every hour (`CloudHostingService`). A member can keep a deployment (`CloudDeployment.keptAt`), at most 5 per project (`KEPT_BY_HAND`, free and PRO alike; past that `409`). Kept deployments are never cleaned and do not take one of the 20 places; their files still count towards the team's storage. Keeping a kept deployment, or un-keeping one that is not kept, changes nothing and records no event. Un-keeping returns it to the normal window by its date: if it is older than the newest 20, the next hourly cleanup deletes it (the console warns before). Deleting a kept deployment by hand is still allowed unless it is production.
- **Download** (decision 60): any member (session or token) gets a kept deployment's files back as `tar.gz` from `GET /cloud/projects/:id/deployments/:deploymentId/archive`, to debug what is live or to leave Cloud. The API builds it on the fly from `deployments/<id>` (the `tar` package: `gzip`, `portable`, `cwd` the folder, its entries listed by name so paths are the uploaded ones without `./`, dotfiles included, except that entries whose name starts with `@` keep a `./` prefix (tar would read them as archives to append); links are never followed, and the folder holds only files and folders anyway) and streams it: `Content-Type: application/gzip`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<slug>-<short id>.tar.gz"`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, no `Content-Length`. Nothing is stored and no limit changes. Allowed while the project is suspended (the files are the team's; only serving them to visitors stops). 10 downloads an hour per team, counted from the team's `deployment.downloaded` events (`429` past it); each download is recorded as that event (labelled `<slug>--<short id>`, like the other deployment events) once the archive's first bytes are ready, just before streaming starts, so an attempt that fails before then (`500`, or `404` if the folder vanished) doesn't count. A deployment that is deleted, or whose folder is gone, answers `404` "Deployment not found"; hosting not set up, `503`. If the folder is removed while streaming (the hourly cleanup), the connection is cut and the archive is incomplete.
  - **Console:** a "Download" action on each deployment of the project's page, through a BFF route that pipes the API's stream without buffering it.
  - **CLI** (`codeqlick`, next minor release): `codeqlick download [id]` (its short id or id among the last 50, like `promote`; the production deployment when omitted) saves `<slug>-<short id>.tar.gz` in the current folder, or `-o, --out <file>`; it refuses to overwrite an existing file unless `--force`, writes to `<file>.part` and renames it when complete, so a cut download leaves nothing behind. Unpacking is left to `tar -xzf`. Events `deployment.kept` and `deployment.unkept`. The CLI does not keep or un-keep yet (planned; `codeqlick deployments` is unchanged).
- **Limits of the free plan** (the API's `CLOUD_PLANS`, shown on the usage page): 3 teams owned, 10 projects per team, 1 GB of storage per team, 100 MB and 10,000 files per deployment, 30 deployments an hour, 5 domains per project, 10 members, 20 tokens per person, 100 redirects and rewrites and 30 header rules per deployment; and in phase 1b, 3 forms per project and 1,000 submissions a month per team, 10 monitors per team, 20 website checks an hour per team (preview checks count too), 500 status-page subscribers and 1,000 subscriber emails an hour per team (`statusSubscribers`, `statusEmailsPerHour`; PRO 5,000 and 10,000). Traffic is fair use: Nginx limits each address to 50 requests a second (bursts of 100) and the team is told before anything is suspended for traffic.

#### Custom domains

A project serves its production deployment on up to 5 domains of its own (`docs.example.com`, `example.com`, `www.example.com`: each is added separately).

1. **Add:** refused when it is `CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN` or under it, `codeqlick.com` or under it, `SITES_PREVIEW_DOMAIN` or under it, a managed site's domain, or another project's (unique). One exception (decision 53): a domain that is exactly one of `CLOUD_PLATFORM_DOMAINS` is accepted when the project belongs to the team whose id is `CLOUD_PLATFORM_TEAM_ID` (the id, because anyone can register a slug) (that is how the documentation site gets `docs.codeqlick.com`). Other teams still get the refusal; subdomains of a listed domain are not covered; with either variable empty there is no exception. The domain is then verified, pointed and served like any other.
2. **Verify ownership:** a TXT record `_codeqlick.<domain>` with `cq-verify=<token>` (random per domain). Nothing is published for a domain until it is verified, so nobody can point a domain they don't own at us and claim it.
3. **Point it:** an `A` record to `SITES_SERVER_IP`, or for a subdomain a `CNAME` to `<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`. Once it resolves here, the API writes `domains/<domain>` (the project's slug inside) in `CLOUD_ROOT`; the host's `sync-sites.sh` (every minute) runs `cq-site add <domain> cloud <slug>`, which serves the project's production folder with a Let's Encrypt certificate. When it answers over HTTPS the domain is `LIVE`.

States: `PENDING` (not verified), `VERIFIED` (verified, not pointing here or not over HTTPS yet), `LIVE`, `ERROR` (it stopped resolving here, with the reason; it comes back on its own). `CloudDomainsService` checks the ones not `LIVE` every 10 minutes for 7 days (then daily) and the `LIVE` ones every 6 hours; "Check now" in the console checks one at once (10/min). Removing a domain deletes its file and `sync-sites.sh` unpublishes it.

#### Password protection

Owner's request (2026-11-13; Notion CQP-149): a project can ask visitors for a password before they see its deployments, and optionally its production too, so work in progress can be shown to a client without being public. Decision 50.

- **Setting** (on `CloudProject`, every plan): `protection` `OFF` (default) | `PREVIEWS` (every deployment address `<slug>--<short>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` and every branch address `<slug>--b-<part>.<…>`) | `ALL` (the previews plus production: `<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` and the project's custom domains). One password per project (8–128 characters), stored only as an Argon2id hash (`protectionHash`, like account passwords); `protectionDays` (how long access lasts once the password is entered: 1, 7 or 30; default 7); `protectionVersion` (an integer, raised whenever the password or the mode changes, which ends every access given before). Turning it on needs a password; switching between `PREVIEWS` and `ALL` keeps the password unless a new one is sent; `OFF` deletes the hash, so turning it on again needs a password again. Changing the days doesn't end access already given. The project's admins (team owners and admins) change it, from the console or the CLI; each change is a team event `project.protection_changed` with the mode, the days and whether the password changed (never the password).
- **How Nginx enforces it** (8.3), without reloading: the API keeps one empty marker file per protected kind of address in `CLOUD_ROOT/protect/`: `<slug>.previews` for `PREVIEWS` and `ALL`, `<slug>.production` for `ALL`. They are written or removed on every change, removed when the project is deleted, and checked against the database when the API starts and every hour (with the deployments' cleanup), so a lost or stale file is put right. A marker belongs to the project's slug, not to a deployment, so deploying, promoting and rolling back change nothing. On every request the hosting site works out the slug and the kind from the label (`previews` when it ends in `--<short>`, `production` otherwise; a custom domain's site is `production` with its slug written in), and makes an `auth_request` to an internal location that answers `204` at once when neither the marker nor the deployment's `.cq-rules` exists (see Redirects, rewrites and headers: this one check also carries the deployment's rules), so unprotected sites without rules never reach the API. Otherwise it asks the API (`GET /v1/cloud/edge/check`, formerly `/protection/check`, always as `GET` without the body), sending `X-Cq-Protection-Key` (`CLOUD_PROTECTION_KEY`, section 4.5), `X-Cq-Host` (the address), `X-Cq-Project` (the slug), `X-Cq-Kind` and `X-Cq-Access` (the value of the visitor's `__Host-cq_access` cookie, taken out of `Cookie` by a `map`; the site's other cookies never reach the API). The API answers `204` (let through) or `401`; Nginx answers a `401` with a `302` to `/__codeqlick/password?next=<the path and query>`. Nginx keeps each answer 60 s per address and cookie value (`proxy_cache`), so a page with many files asks the API once; a changed password or mode therefore reaches visitors already let in within a minute (removing a marker, at once).
- **The check:** `204` when the bypass token is valid ("Bypass for automation", below) or the cookie is valid for that address (signature, project, the current `protectionVersion`, not expired), and also when the project isn't protected for that kind (a marker not yet removed), is suspended (its "suspended" page shows as usual) or doesn't exist; `401` otherwise. A request without the right key answers `403`, which the visitor sees as an error: protection fails closed.
- **Visitor's page:** `/__codeqlick/` on every hosting address (the hosting domain's and custom domains) is proxied by Nginx to the API's `/v1/cloud/protection/` with the same headers (and no `auth_request`), so that path of a project's own files is never served. `GET /__codeqlick/password?next=` answers a small Codeqlick page: the logo (inline SVG), the project's name, a password field and a button, in English or Spanish from `Accept-Language` (English otherwise); no external resources, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, `Cache-Control: no-store`. `POST /__codeqlick/password` (form fields `password`, `next`) checks the hash: right, it sets the cookie and answers `303` to `next`; wrong, the page again (`401`) with "Wrong password". `next` is used only when it is a path on the same address (starting with `/`, not `//` or `/\`), else `/`; Nginx can't encode a query, so it sends `$request_uri` as is and the API takes everything after the first `next=` as the path. Tries are limited to 5 a minute per IP (IPv6 by its /64) and project, and 60 a minute per project from everyone, in the API (in memory; over it the page says to wait a minute, `429`) and to 10 requests a minute per IP on `/__codeqlick/password` in Nginx (`limit_req`).
- **Cookie:** `__Host-cq_access` (host-only, `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, `Max-Age` = `protectionDays`). Its value is `v1.<projectId>.<protectionVersion>.<expiry>.<signature>`, the signature an HMAC-SHA256 of the rest and the address, with a key derived from `CREDENTIALS_SECRET` with HKDF-SHA256 (salt `codeqlick`, purpose `cloud-protection-v1`, as `SecretBox` derives its keys). It is valid only on the address it was set on: the hosting domain is shared by every team, so nothing is ever set for the parent domain (decision 33), and a visitor of an `ALL` project enters the password once per address. Changing the password or the mode (a new `protectionVersion`) or `CREDENTIALS_SECRET` ends every cookie.
- **Team members skip it:** where the console links to a protected address (a deployment's, production's or a custom domain's), the link goes to a console route that asks `GET /v1/cloud/projects/:id/protection/ticket?host=<address>` (any member) and redirects to its `url`, `https://<address>/__codeqlick/enter?ticket=…`. The ticket is signed like the cookie (purpose `cloud-protection-ticket-v1`), names the project, the address and a random id, lasts 60 s and works once (its id kept hashed in memory until it expires, like the meetings' tickets). `/__codeqlick/enter` checks it, sets the same cookie and answers `303` to `/` with `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; an expired, used or foreign ticket shows the password page with "This link has expired". A member's access then lasts `protectionDays` like anyone's. The CLI's printed addresses stay plain.
- **Bypass for automation** (decision 80): a project can have one secret that lets scripts through its protected addresses without the password and without a cookie: end-to-end tests in CI, link checkers, `curl`. Like Vercel's Protection Bypass for Automation and Netlify's site-protection tokens.
  - **Token:** `cqb_` + 32 random bytes in base64url (`randomBytes(32)`, as API tokens use `cqk_`, 4.13 "Authentication for machines").
