The agent reads the checkout carefully: a file counts only when its folder, symlinks resolved, is inside the checkout (a symlinked root folder or parent can't make it look at the build host), and it reads only regular files of at most 1 MB (so not a symlinked `package.json`).
  - **Commands:** pnpm → `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (`pnpm install` without a lockfile) and `pnpm run build`; Yarn 2 or later (`packageManager` `yarn@2` or later; without the field, a `.yarnrc.yml` next to `yarn.lock` or in the root folder) → `yarn install --immutable` and `yarn run build`; Yarn 1 → `yarn install --frozen-lockfile` and `yarn run build`; Yarn without its lockfile (chosen by `packageManager`) → `yarn install` (an immutable install refuses to create the lockfile); npm with its lockfile → `npm ci` and `npm run build` (as before); npm without its lockfile → `npm install` (`npm ci` failed there) and `npm run build`. Bun's lockfiles (`bun.lock`, `bun.lockb`) don't count: the image has no Bun, and the log says so.
  - **Log:** one line before the command says what was chosen and why: `Package manager: pnpm (pnpm-lock.yaml)`, `Package manager: Yarn 1 (yarn.lock in ..)`, `Package manager: Yarn 2+ (packageManager in package.json)`, `Package manager: npm (no lockfile)`, `Package manager: custom commands`. It also names what was passed over: another manager's lockfile when `packageManager` decides (`Package manager: Yarn 2+ (packageManager in package.json; pnpm-lock.yaml ignored)`), the other lockfiles of the winning folder when the lockfile decides (`Package manager: pnpm (pnpm-lock.yaml; also found package-lock.json)`), and a Bun lockfile (`bun.lock found, but the build image has no Bun`). When only a `.yarnrc.yml` says Yarn 2+, it adds `set packageManager in package.json so Corepack runs Yarn 2+` (Corepack's default Yarn is 1.x; a committed `yarnPath` still works, so it is a hint, not an error).
  - **No API change:** the agent decides from the checkout; the claim, the database and the defaults stay as they are, so an agent not yet updated builds exactly as before.
  - **Console and CLI:** the Git tab's build field explains that the default commands pick npm, pnpm or Yarn from the lockfile or the `packageManager` field; so does the CLI's README for `git connect`.
- **Back on GitHub:** each build sets the commit status `Codeqlick Cloud` (pending → success with the deployment's address, or failure with the build's page). A pull request gets one comment from the app, updated with each build: the preview's address, the branch's fixed address (not for the production branch) and the build's state. With **Check each preview** on (decision 69), the comment also carries a website check of the preview:
  - **When:** a build whose trigger is a pull request (target preview) succeeds, its deployment is live and `previewChecks` is on. The API then runs the website check (section 4.13, Website check API, "Preview checks") on the deployment's own address (`https://<slug>--<short id>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`), in the background: `finish` neither waits for it nor fails because of it, and neither do the commit status or the build notifications. Branch pushes and manual builds are not checked, nor are production builds.
  - **Limit:** it counts against the team's `auditsPerHour`. Past it the check is skipped (no audit is made).
  - **Result:** the audit is stored (no member) and the build's `auditId` set. When it ends, the API rewrites the comment with the same builder as the build's own report. Only the pull request's latest build updates it: a check that ends after a newer build was made for that pull request doesn't touch the comment.
  - **Comment section "Website check":** while running, `Website check: running…`. Done: the score and the four categories' scores (Speed, Mobile, Google, Security), up to 5 failed checks (their titles only, no advice), the number of warnings and a link to the audit in the console (`<CLOUD_URL>/en/console/<team>/audits/<id>`). Skipped: `Website check skipped: hourly limit reached`. When the preview couldn't be opened or the check ran over 60 s: `Website check: couldn't open the preview (<reason>)`, the reason being the website check's own plain-language one. The comment is in English, like the rest of it (the app has no per-team language).
  - **Only the latest build writes:** before the "running…" line and again before the result, the API checks that the build is still the pull request's latest (cancelled builds don't count); an older build writes the plain success comment and runs no check. No check runs when the GitHub App isn't configured or the repository was unlinked. A check cut short by an API restart is not retried: the comment keeps "running…" until the next push.
  - **Not checked:** Cloud has no password-protected previews, so there is nothing to skip for them. If previews get a password, the check skips them with a note in the comment (to specify then). The commit status's address stays the deployment's own.
- **Console:** the project's **Git** tab (connection, settings, variables), its **Builds** (list with state, branch, commit, duration; a build's page with the live log, refreshed every 3 s while running, and, while the log isn't empty, a "Download log" link and a "Copy log" button (it copies the log shown, then says "Copied"; the link goes to the route handler `/[locale]/cloud/console/<team>/<project>/builds/<number>/log`, next to the deployment archive's, which checks the session, finds the project and pipes the API's log without the browser ever calling the API); "Deploy again", "Cancel"; the "New build" form's "Without cache" checkbox and a failed build's "Retry without cache", and a build run without cache says so; for a build with a preview check, its score with a link to the audit's report). Deployments made by a build show its commit and link to it; the build's page and the project's deployment row show the branch address next to the deployment's own. The Git tab's "Build pull requests" is followed by a checkbox **"Check each preview"** (`previewChecks`).
- **CLI** (`codeqlick` 0.3, owner's choice 2026-11-13; the same `/v1/cloud` routes with the person's token): `codeqlick git` shows the connection and settings (with "check previews" when on; `git connect` has no flag for it, it is changed in the console or through the API); `codeqlick git connect <owner/repo> [--branch] [--root] [--install] [--build] [--output] [--node]` links a repository the team's GitHub installations can see (with none, it prints the console's Git tab to install the app: installing needs the browser); `codeqlick git disconnect`. `codeqlick env` lists the variables (names and targets, never values); `codeqlick env add <KEY> [value] [--production] [--preview]` (both when neither is given; without a value it reads standard input, or asks without echo in a terminal, so secrets stay out of the shell's history); `codeqlick env rm <KEY>`; both take `--branch <name>` for a variable of one preview branch (`env add --branch` implies `--preview`), and the list shows each variable's branch (next CLI release). `codeqlick builds` lists the latest builds; `codeqlick builds start [--branch] [--no-cache]` queues one (`--no-cache` runs it without the dependency cache) and, like `codeqlick logs [number]`, follows its log until it ends (polling `?after=` every 2 s), printing the deployment's address; both exit `1` when the build fails or is canceled, so CI can wait on a build. `codeqlick builds cancel <number>`. `codeqlick logs [number] --out <file>` writes the whole log from the log download route to a file instead of printing and following it (`-o` is the short form, as in `download`); it refuses to overwrite an existing file unless `--force`, and answers the usual error for an unknown build. `--json` prints the API's answer for `git`, `env` and `builds`.

#### Build notifications (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). A project tells people when its builds end, so nobody has to watch the console. Decision 43.

- **Channel** (`CloudNotifyChannel`, up to 5 per project, managed by the team's admins): a `name`, `enabled`, a `kind` and what it is told about:
  - **Email:** team members (1–10, like a monitor's alerts), each in their language.
  - **Slack:** an incoming webhook (`https://hooks.slack.com/services/…`).
  - **Discord:** a channel webhook (`https://discord.com/api/webhooks/…` or `discordapp.com`).
  - **Webhook:** any `https` URL of the team's (port 443, no credentials). Each delivery is a `POST` of JSON `{ event, project: { slug, name }, build: { number, target, branch, commit, commitMessage, author, pullRequest, status, error, keptAsPreview, durationMs, url }, deployment: { url } | null, sentAt }` with `X-Codeqlick-Event` and `X-Codeqlick-Signature: sha256=<HMAC-SHA256 of the body with the channel's secret>`; the secret (`cqn_` + 32 hex) is shown once, when the channel is created.
  - **Language:** Slack, Discord and webhook messages use the channel's `locale` (`en` or `es`, the console's language when it was created); emails use each person's.
  - **Events:** a build **failed** (on by default), a build **succeeded** (off by default; with the deployment's address), and whether **previews** count too (off: only production builds). Canceled builds tell nobody. A build refused because the month's builds ran out counts as failed. Slack, Discord and webhook channels can also be told about **form submissions** (see below). Slack, Discord and webhook channels can also get **monitor alerts** (see below).
- **Secrets:** Slack, Discord and webhook URLs carry their own credential, so they are sealed like environment variables (AES-256-GCM, key from `CREDENTIALS_SECRET`, purpose `cloud-notify-v1`) and write-only: the console shows the host and the last 4 characters. Changing the URL means entering it again.
- **Delivery:** when a build ends, every enabled channel that wants that event is sent one message in the background (never delaying the build or GitHub's status): URLs through the website check's safe fetching (public addresses only, checked on connection; no redirects; 10 s), a `2xx` is success. The channel keeps its last delivery (`lastSentAt`, `lastOk`, `lastError`), shown in the console. Ten failed deliveries in a row switch the channel off (`enabled: false`) and email the team's admins once. "Send a test" sends a sample event at once (a build, or a form submission for channels that get them; both with `event: "test"` so a CRM doesn't file a fake lead) and answers with the result.
- **Messages:** short and in the project's words: "❌ *web* · build #12 failed on `main` (`a1b2c3d` Fix header): The build exited with code 1" with a link to the build; "✅ *web* · `main` published: https://web.codeqlick.app" with links to the site and the build. Slack gets `text` (mrkdwn), Discord an embed (red or green), email the branded layout with a button to the build.
- **Form submissions:** a Slack, Discord or webhook channel can also get the project's form submissions (`formSubmitted`, off by default), from all its forms or only some (`formIds`, up to 20 forms of the project; empty means all; deleting a form removes it from the list, and deleting the last listed one also switches `formSubmitted` off so the channel doesn't start getting every form). Email channels can't (`400`): forms already email their own recipients. Only stored submissions are sent: honeypot hits, refused posts and posts over the month's limit send nothing. Delivery, failure counting and switch-off are the build events' ones, in the background after the submission is stored (never delaying the visitor's answer), with two differences because anyone can post to a form: form events go out one at a time per channel (an in-memory queue of at most 50; when it is full, new ones are dropped: they stay in the console), and a `429` answer is recorded but doesn't count towards the switch-off.
  - **Webhook:** `POST` of `{ event: "form.submitted", project: { slug, name }, form: { id, name }, submission: { id, fields, email, page, createdAt, url }, sentAt }` (`fields` as stored: `[name, value]` pairs in the order sent; `url` the form's submissions in the console) with `X-Codeqlick-Event: form.submitted` and the same signature.
  - **Slack and Discord:** "📨 *web* · new submission to *Contact*" with up to 10 fields as `name: value` lines (each value cut to 300 characters, in the stored order; on Discord, lines that would pass the embed's length are left out whole), "+N more fields" when there are more, and a link to the form's submissions in the console. Visitors write these values, so they are sent as plain text: Slack's control characters (`&`, `<`, `>`) are escaped so they can't make masked links or mentions (a bare address still shows as a link, without a preview), and Discord messages go with `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` so `@everyone` or a user id pings nobody.
  - **Privacy:** submissions usually hold personal data; sending them to a service the team chose is the team's decision as controller (the Cloud terms already make Codeqlick its processor). The console says so next to the switch.
- **Monitor alerts:** a Slack, Discord or webhook channel can also get the alerts of the project's monitors (`monitorAlerts`, off by default): down, back up, certificate warnings, slow and back to normal of every monitor tied to the project (section "Monitors and status pages"), exactly when their emails go (same rules: two failures and 15-minute spacing for down and up, the episode rules of Slow responses for slow). Email channels can't (`400`): monitors already email their own chosen members. Monitors without a project keep email only. The same switch also carries the drops of the weekly production check (event `audit.dropped`, see Production checks, decision 81).
  - **Webhook:** `POST` of `{ event: "monitor.down" | "monitor.up" | "monitor.cert" | "monitor.slow" | "monitor.fast", project: { slug, name }, monitor: { id, name, kind, url }, error, downMinutes, certExpiresAt, responseMs, slowMs, slowMinutes, consoleUrl, sentAt }` (`url` only for HTTP monitors; `downMinutes` on `monitor.up`; `certExpiresAt` on `monitor.cert`; `responseMs` and `slowMs` on `monitor.slow` and `monitor.fast`; `slowMinutes` on `monitor.fast`; otherwise `null`), with `X-Codeqlick-Event` and the same signature.
  - **Slack and Discord:** "🔴 *web* · *API* is down: Timeout after 15 s", "🟢 *web* · *API* is back up after 12 min", "⚠️ *web* · the certificate of *API* expires on 2 Dec 2026", "🐢 *web* · *API* is slow: 2,400 ms (limit 1,000 ms)", "⚡ *web* · *API* is back to normal after 25 min" with a link to the monitor in the console; Discord as a red, green, yellow or orange embed (slow orange, back to normal green). Monitor names and errors are escaped like build messages.
  - Delivery, failure counting and switch-off are the build events' ones, in the background (never delaying the checks).
- **Console:** the project's **Notifications** tab (list with kind, events and last delivery; add, edit, switch off, test, delete). The **Builds** tab's button says "Build <branch>" while the project has no builds yet.

#### Deploy hooks (phase 2)

Owner's request (2026-11-13; roadmap row CQP-150). A deploy hook is a secret URL that builds one branch of a project when something calls it, so a headless CMS (or any service that can call a webhook) rebuilds the site when its content changes and no commit is pushed. Decision 51.

- **Hook** (`CloudDeployHook` of a project): a `name` (1–60, unique in the project), a `branch` (always set: the production branch when none is given; it must exist in the repository when the hook is made, else `400`) and a secret **token**: `cqd_` + 32 random bytes (base64url). The token is part of the hook's URL, `<the API's public address>/v1/cloud/hooks/<token>` (`https://api.codeqlick.com/v1/cloud/hooks/cqd_…`), shown **once**, when the hook is made; only its SHA-256 and its first 12 characters (`prefix`, to recognise it) are stored, like API tokens. The hook also keeps who made it (`createdById`, null once that account is gone), when it was made and when it was last called (`lastCalledAt`).
- **Who and how many:** only projects connected to a repository have hooks (`409` otherwise). A project's admins make and delete them; members list them (session or API token). Up to 5 per project on every plan (`CLOUD_PLANS.*.deployHooksPerProject`; past it `409`). A hook can't be edited: to change its name or branch, make a new one and delete the old one.
- **Revoking:** deleting a hook revokes it at once (its URL answers `404` from then on). Disconnecting the repository, connecting the project to a different repository, or deleting the project deletes its hooks. Changing the production branch leaves them as they are: each keeps building its own branch (a hook made for the old production branch now builds previews of it).
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/cloud/hooks/:token`, public (the token is the credential); `POST` only. It needs no body: any body is ignored (read only up to the API's default size limit, `413` above), so a CMS's payload never matters. A call queues a build of the latest commit of the hook's branch **exactly like "Deploy again"** (`CloudBuildsService`: the branch's head read from GitHub, the month's builds counted, older queued builds of that branch canceled, the commit status and notifications as for any build). The build is **production** when the branch is the project's production branch, else a **preview**, whatever the "build other branches as previews" switch says: a hook is an explicit request. It answers `202 { build: { number, status, url } }`, `url` being the build's page in the console.
- **Refusals:** an unknown or deleted token `404`; a deleted project `404`; a suspended project `403`; a project no longer connected, or a branch that no longer exists in the repository (`404` from GitHub) `409` with the reason; GitHub failing `502`; the GitHub App not set up `503`. When the team's builds for the month have run out the call is handled like a push: the build is recorded as failed with the note "The team reached its N builds this month" (failed commit status and notifications included, nothing is built) and the call answers `429` with that reason. Every build a call queues counts against the plan's monthly builds (`buildsPerMonth`, Madrid calendar month, shared with pushes and "Deploy again") unless it is canceled: a build replaced by a newer one before it started no longer counts.
- **Rate limit:** 10 calls a minute per hook and 60 a minute per IP across hooks (both `429`, counted in the API's memory like the other public endpoints). Bursts within the limit cost little: each new build cancels the queued ones before it for the same branch, so a CMS saving ten times in a row builds once or twice (and only the builds that are not superseded count for the month).
- **The build:** `CloudBuild.trigger` is `HOOK` (`hook` in the API) and the build keeps the hook's name at that moment (`hookName`), so it stays readable after the hook is deleted; it is shown in the console's build list and page as "Deploy hook · <name>". It has no `createdById`; its commit, message and author are the branch head's, as for "Deploy again".
- **Events:** every call that finds a hook of a live project is a team event `deploy_hook.called`, whatever the answer (`202`, `403`, `409`, the month's limit): label the hook's name; details `{ status, build? }` (the HTTP status and the build's number when one was queued); the caller's IP. It has **no person**: `CloudEvent.actorId` is already optional, so it is written like Cloud's other automatic events (`CloudTeamsService.recordSystem`, which takes the caller's IP as an optional last argument for this) with `actorName` `Deploy hook <name>` (cut to 120) and no token. The console's events list shows it like any other. Calls refused before a hook is known (unknown token `404`) or by the rate limit (`429`) record nothing, so a flood can't fill the list. Making and deleting a hook are `deploy_hook.created` and `deploy_hook.deleted`, by the person (and token) that did it, with the hook's name and branch.
- **Keeping the token out of logs:** the API's request log and error log write a hook's path as `/v1/cloud/hooks/cqd_<first 8>…`, never the full token. Nginx's access log of the API writes the hook's path the same way, with only the first 8 characters of the token after `cqd_`.
- **Management API:** `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE /v1/cloud/projects/:id/hooks…` (section 5), with a session or an API token. `lastCalledAt` is only written when a call finds the hook.
- **Console:** the project's **Git** tab gets a **Deploy hooks** card: the list (name, branch, `prefix…`, made by, last called), "Add" with a name and a branch (the production branch preselected), the new URL shown once with a copy button and a warning that it won't be shown again, and "Delete" (with a confirmation saying the URL stops working at once). Without a repository the card says to connect one first. The Builds list marks hook builds.
- **CLI** (`codeqlick` 0.5.0; the project linked in the folder, or `--project`): `codeqlick hooks` lists them; `codeqlick hooks add <name> [--branch <branch>]` makes one (the production branch when `--branch` is missing) and prints its URL once, with a note to paste it in the CMS's webhook; `codeqlick hooks rm <id or name>` deletes one. `--json` prints the API's answer (for `add`, with the `url`).

#### Web analytics (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). A project counts its visits without cookies and without sending its visitors to a third party, and sees them in the console. Decision 44.

- **Switch:** off by default; a project's admins switch it on in its **Analytics** tab (`CloudProject.analytics`). While it is off, events for the project are dropped.
- **Snippet:** `<script defer src="https://api.codeqlick.com/v1/cloud/analytics/script.js"></script>` in the site's pages (sent with `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin`, cached a day). The script (under 1 KB, no cookies, no local storage) sends a page view on load and on every navigation of a single-page app (`history.pushState`, `replaceState`, `popstate`) with `navigator.sendBeacon` as `text/plain` JSON `{ n, u, r, w }` (event name, the page's address, the referrer, the window's width). `window.codeqlick.track('<name>')` sends a custom event (letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `.`, `:`; up to 40). It sends nothing over `http:`, from `localhost` or when the browser asks "Do Not Track". A site with a Content-Security-Policy allows `https://api.codeqlick.com` in `script-src` and `connect-src`.
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/cloud/analytics/event` (public, 4 KB, 60 a minute per IP), always `202` so nobody learns which addresses count. An event is kept only when its page `u` is one of the project's addresses (`https://<slug>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`, its deployments' and its verified domains; like forms), the `Origin` header (when sent) is that page's origin, the project has analytics on and is neither suspended nor deleted, and the User-Agent isn't a bot (empty, or naming a crawler, a headless browser, a link preview or an HTTP library).
- **What is kept** (`CloudAnalyticsEvent`): the project, the event name (`pageview` or the custom one), the path (no query, no fragment; up to 300), the referrer's host when it is another site, the campaign (`utm_source`, or `ref`, up to 60), the browser, the operating system and the device (mobile under 768 px, tablet under 1,024, desktop), and a **visitor id**: the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of a daily salt, the project, the IP and the User-Agent. The salt is random, one per UTC day (`CloudAnalyticsSalt`), and deleted the day after, so ids can't be recomputed or followed from one day to the next: a person is counted once a day. No IP, no cookie, no country.
- **Limits:** 50,000 events a month per team on the free plan (`analyticsEventsPerMonth`, Madrid calendar month; over it events are dropped and the owners get one email that month). Events are kept 365 days, then deleted (daily).
- **Report:** `GET /v1/cloud/projects/:id/analytics?period=24h|7d|30d|90d|365d` (default `7d`) answers the totals (visitors, page views), a series (by hour for `24h`, by Madrid day otherwise), and the top 10 pages, referrers (with campaigns), browsers, operating systems, devices and custom events, each with visitors (and page views or counts).
- **Export** (owner's roadmap, 2026-11-14): `GET /v1/cloud/projects/:id/analytics.csv?period=24h|7d|30d|90d|365d` (default `7d`; session or API token, any member, like the report) returns one CSV for the period with the columns `kind,date,name,visitors,count`: a row per Madrid day (per hour for `24h`, as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:00` in Madrid time) and page (`kind` `page`, `name` the path, `count` its page views), and a row per day (or hour) and custom event (`kind` `event`, `name` the event, `count` its events). Rows are ordered by date, then kind, then count, highest first. `visitors` counts the distinct visitor ids within that day (or hour). UTF-8 with BOM; cells that start like a formula are escaped as in the leads export (`/admin/leads/export.csv`, section 5). At most 100,000 rows: when there are more, the file ends with the first 100,000 (oldest first) and the response carries the header `X-Truncated: true`. The attachment is named `<project slug>-analytics-<period>.csv`. 10 a minute per caller. It works while analytics is switched off: the events already kept still belong to the team. No data-model change. The CLI does not export it yet.
- **Comparison with the previous period:** the report also answers `previous: { visitors, pageviews, complete }`, the totals of the window of the same length that ends where the current one starts (`7d` → the 7 days before those 7; `24h` → the 24 hours before). It is counted exactly like `totals` (visitors are the distinct visitor ids of the window, page views the `pageview` events) over `[since − length, since)` computed with the same `periodRange` rules (Madrid days, or UTC hours for `24h`). Caveats: a visitor id is per day (above), so `visitors` is the number of visitor-days in the window, and a person who comes on three days of the window counts three times, in both windows alike; two windows of the same length are comparable, a window and one of another length are not. The current window includes today so far, so early in the day the change reads lower than it will at night. Events are kept 365 days, so a previous window that starts before that (`365d` always) is still computed over what is kept, and `complete` is `false`; the console shows no change then, because the figure would compare a full window with a partial one.
- **Console:** the project's **Analytics** tab: the switch, the snippet to copy, the period, an **Export CSV** link next to the period selector (it goes through a route of the web app, like the forms' CSV, so the browser never calls the API), the totals, a bar chart and the tables, and a **Share** section (below). Under each total, the change against the previous period, rounded to whole percent (`round((current − previous) / previous × 100)`): "+12 % vs previous 7 days" (up, in the positive style), "−8 % vs previous 7 days" (down, in the negative style), "0 % vs previous 7 days" when equal, "New" when the previous is 0 and the current is above 0, and nothing when both are 0 or `previous.complete` is `false`. In Spanish: "+12 % frente a los 7 días anteriores". The label names the period (`24h`: "previous 24 hours"). The shared public report (Shared link, below) shows the same comparison, from the same `previous` in its answer.
- **Shared link:** owner's choice (2026-11-14). A project's admins can share its analytics read-only with people outside the team (a studio's client), like Plausible's shared links. One link per project: `https://cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/analytics/<token>`, the token being `cqa_` + 24 random bytes in base64url. It is stored as its SHA-256 (`analyticsShareHash`, unique, for the lookup) and sealed (`analyticsShareSealed`, AES-256-GCM, key from `CREDENTIALS_SECRET`, purpose `cloud-analytics-share-v1`, like environment variables) so admins can copy it again from the console. Creating it again replaces it (the old link stops working); deleting it stops sharing. Members see whether it is on; only admins see and copy the link. The token is part of the address, so it appears in access logs like any shared link.
  - **Page:** the same report as the console (period selector 24h to 365d, totals, chart and top tables) for that project, read-only. Title "<project name> · Analytics", `noindex`, no snippet, no team name, no settings and no links into the console. The footer says "Analytics by Codeqlick Cloud" and links to the Cloud landing.
  - **API:** `GET /v1/cloud/projects/:id/analytics/share` (any member) answers `{ enabled, url?, token? }` (`url` and `token` only for admins; `url` is the English address; the console builds the address in the viewer's language from `token`); `POST` (ADMIN, session or API token, like the analytics `PATCH`) creates or replaces the link and answers `{ url, token }`; `DELETE` (ADMIN) answers `204`. `GET /v1/cloud/analytics/shared/<token>?period=` is public (60 a minute per IP) and answers the report without `enabled` and `snippet`, plus `project: { name }`, with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=60`. It answers `404` when the token is unknown, the project is suspended or deleted, or analytics are switched off.
  - **Events:** `analytics.shared` (created or replaced) and `analytics.unshared` in the team's events.
  - **Console:** the Share section shows off or on, "Create link" (or "New link", with a confirmation that the old one stops working), a copy button and "Stop sharing".
- **Privacy:** the team decides to measure and is the controller; Codeqlick processes the events for it (Cloud terms). Nothing is stored on visitors' devices and no personal data is kept, which is how it avoids needing a cookie banner.

#### Key-value store (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). Each project has a small key-value store reached over HTTP, for settings, feature flags, counters and data that scripts, CI jobs and servers share; keys under `public:` are readable by anyone, so a static site can read them from the browser. Decision 45.

- **Entry** (`CloudKvEntry`, by project and key): a key (1–256 characters: letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `:`), a value (any JSON, up to 64 KB once serialised), its size, an optional expiry (`ttl` 1 second to 365 days) and when it was created and updated. An expired entry reads as missing and is deleted within the hour.
- **Limits:** free plan 1,000 keys and 10 MB per project (`kvKeysPerProject`, `kvBytesPerProject`; past them a write answers `409`); 600 requests a minute per caller.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/kv`, session or API token; any member reads and writes): list keys (`?prefix&cursor&limit`, by key), read one, write one (`PUT { value, ttl? }`), delete one, and increment one atomically (`POST …/incr { by?, ttl? }`, `by` an integer, default 1; a missing key starts at 0; `409` when the value isn't an integer). Writes aren't recorded as team events (they are data, not changes to the team).
- **Public reads:** `GET /v1/cloud/kv/<project slug>/<key>` answers the value of a key starting with `public:` as JSON to anyone (CORS for any origin, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=10`, 120 a minute per IP); any other key, a suspended or a deleted project answers `404`. Nothing is writable without a token.
- **Console:** the project's **KV** tab: usage against the limits, keys by prefix with their size and expiry, a key's value, writing one (JSON) and deleting it, and the `curl` lines to use it.

#### Plans and billing (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13). A team moves to **PRO** (the limits of `CLOUD_PLANS.PRO`) by paying a monthly subscription through Stripe; the free plan stays. Decision 46.

- **Price:** a recurring monthly price made in Stripe's dashboard, named by `STRIPE_CLOUD_PRO_PRICE_ID`; its amount and currency are read from Stripe (cached an hour) and shown in the console, plus IVA (the same tax rate as decision 20, `SITE_VAT_PERCENT`). Without Stripe's keys or that price the team's **Plan** page says paid plans aren't available yet and nothing else changes.
- **Upgrade:** a team's owners press "Upgrade to PRO": `POST /v1/cloud/teams/:team/billing/checkout` makes a Checkout Session in subscription mode (the team's Stripe customer, made once with the owner's email and the team's id in its metadata; `client_reference_id` and metadata name the team) and the console sends them there. Stripe's webhook (`POST /v1/payments/stripe`, the same endpoint as decision 20, which tells them apart by the session's mode and metadata) makes the team `PRO` when the session completes.
- **Subscription state** (`CloudTeam`: `stripeCustomerId`, `stripeSubscriptionId`, `subscriptionStatus`, `currentPeriodEnd`, `cancelAtPeriodEnd`, `subscriptionAmountCents`: what the subscription bills a month before VAT, for the admin overview's MRR, section 4.17), kept by the webhook's `customer.subscription.created|updated|deleted`: `active` and `trialing` are PRO; `past_due` stays PRO while Stripe retries (the owners are emailed on `invoice.payment_failed`); `canceled`, `unpaid` and `incomplete_expired` go back to FREE. The event's subscription is read again from Stripe before acting, so events arriving out of order don't matter.
- **Managing it:** `POST /v1/cloud/teams/:team/billing/portal` opens Stripe's customer portal (card, invoices, cancel at the end of the period). Owners only; admins and members see the plan.
- **Going back to FREE** deletes nothing: what is over the free limits keeps working but nothing more can be added (a 409 names the limit), and the monthly limits (builds, analytics, form submissions) apply at once.
- **Console:** the team's **Plan** page: the plan, its limits side by side, the price, the subscription's state and next renewal, and the buttons. Admin → Cloud shows each team's plan.

#### Usage alerts (phase 2)

Owner's request (2026-11-13). A team hears that it is close to a plan limit before anything is refused. Decision 54.

- **What is measured**, per team against its current plan (`CLOUD_PLANS`, so PRO teams get alerts against PRO's limits):

  | Key | Used | Limit | Resets |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `builds` | builds queued this Madrid calendar month | `buildsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `formSubmissions` | form submissions this month, all the team's forms | `formSubmissionsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `analyticsEvents` | analytics events this month | `analyticsEventsPerMonth` | the 1st |
  | `storage` | bytes of the team's kept deployments | `storageBytesPerTeam` | never (frees up when deployments are deleted) |
  | `deploymentsPerHour` | deployments in the last hour (rolling) | `deploymentsPerHour` | rolling |

  The counts are the ones the limits themselves use, so an alert and a refusal never disagree. A limit counts as reached at `used × 100 ≥ limit × CLOUD_USAGE_ALERT_PERCENT`, a constant (80), not an environment variable. The percent shown is `floor(used / limit × 100)`, and can pass 100.
- **Sweep:** `CloudUsageAlertsService` (API process, every 10 minutes, off in tests, `sweep(now)` callable directly) looks for the teams with activity since the last sweep: a build queued, a form submission, an analytics event or a deployment created (by their timestamps; teams with none are not looked at). The first sweep after a start looks back an hour, so a restart loses nothing. For each such team it computes the five measures once and, for each one at or over the threshold, tries to claim an alert (below). Nothing runs on the hot paths (posting a form, recording an event, deploying), which stay as cheap as they are.
- **Once per limit per month:** `CloudUsageAlert` has one row per team, key and period. The period is the Madrid month (`yyyy-mm`) for every key, including storage and deployments per hour, so each limit mails at most once a month. The claim is an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the unique index; only the sweep that inserted the row sends, so two sweeps (or two API processes) never send twice. As with the existing limit emails, a failed send is logged and not retried that month. Rows older than 13 months are deleted by the sweep once a day.
- **Email:** to every `OWNER` of the team (admins and members don't get it: owners are the ones who pay and choose the plan), each in their own language, in the branded layout (`email-layout.ts`; `cloudUsageAlertEmail` in `cloud-emails.ts`; plain text and HTML). Subject, for example: "Your team *web* used 80 % of its builds this month" (Spanish: "Tu equipo *web* ha usado el 80 % de sus builds este mes"); the percent is the real one at that moment. The body gives used and limit ("164 of 200"), when it resets ("on the 1st, Madrid time" for the monthly ones; storage: "when you delete deployments"; deployments per hour: "as the last hour passes"), what happens at 100 % (below), and a button to the team's usage page (`cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/console/<team>`). For a `FREE` team, and only when billing is available (`CloudBillingService.available`), a line says PRO has higher limits, with a link to the Plan page. The existing emails at 100 % (form submissions, analytics events) stay as they are and usually come after this one.
- **At 100 %** (the existing behaviour, repeated in the email): builds are refused, with a note in the console and a failed commit status; form posts get `429`; analytics events are dropped; deployments are refused (`413`, storage; `429`, per hour).
- **Console banner:** every team page shows a warning banner above its content while any limit is at 80 % or more, and an error-styled one while any is at 100 % or more, naming the limits and linking to the usage page (the team's main page). It is always shown while true; nothing is dismissed or stored. It is computed from the usage endpoint (below), which gives each measure's `percent`; the frontend only decides the style.
- **Team events:** an alert sent is recorded as `usage.alert_sent`, a system event (`actorId` null, `actorName` "Codeqlick", no token, no IP) with `details` `{ key, percent, used, limit, period, emailed }` (`emailed`: how many owners). It is listed in the events page like the others.
- **No new settings:** the threshold is a constant, the mail is the platform's, and nothing is configured per team. Alerts per person, other thresholds and Slack or webhook delivery are left for later.

#### Databases (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): managed PostgreSQL databases for projects, **for now on the production VPS** in a PostgreSQL of their own, apart from the platform's. Decision 47.

- **Server:** the `cloud-pg` service (`postgres:18-alpine`, its own volume) listening publicly on `pg.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>:5432` with **TLS only** (the hosting domain's wildcard certificate, copied in when the container starts and every 12 hours, then reloaded) and SCRAM passwords. `pg_hba.conf`: remote clients only over `hostssl`, each role only to its own database (`sameuser`); the administrator role only from the Docker network. `pg` is a reserved project slug. Settings in `CLOUD_PG_ADMIN_URL` (the administrator's connection inside Docker) and `CLOUD_PG_HOST`; without them the **Databases** tab says it isn't set up.
- **Database** (`CloudDatabase` of a project; free: 1 per project, PRO: 5): a `name` (shown), and in PostgreSQL a database and a role both called `cq_<16 hex>`, the role `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE`, owner of its database, with a connection limit (free 5, PRO 20) and `statement_timeout` 60 s; `CONNECT` is revoked from `PUBLIC`. The password (32 random characters) is shown once, at creation and when it is reset; it is never stored (decision 42's reasoning: the console doesn't need to read it back).
- **Size:** free 256 MB, PRO 10 GB per database (`pg_database_size`, checked every 10 minutes and shown). Over it, the database becomes read-only (`default_transaction_read_only`, its sessions ended) and the team's admins are emailed; under it again it is writable again. A session can turn read-only off for itself, so past 150 % of its size the role's connection limit becomes 0 until it is back under (a team deletes data through the console's help or by email). Databases of deleted projects are dropped at the next check.
- **Backups:** each database is dumped daily (`pg_dump -Fc`) next to the platform's backups and kept 7 days. For now they stay on the same server, which the console says; restoring is asked by email.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/databases`, ADMIN to create, reset and delete; members read): read one (`GET …/:databaseId`, the same view; added with the CLI's `db` commands, decision 111), list (`{ id, name, host, port, database, user, sizeBytes, maxBytes, readOnly, connectionUrl (without the password), createdAt }`), create (`{ name }`, the answer adds `password` and the full `connectionUrl` once), reset the password, delete (drops it at once; the latest daily dump stays for 7 days). Recorded as team events.
- **Console:** the project's **Databases** tab: the list with size and state, the connection string to copy (`postgresql://<user>:<password>@pg.<domain>:5432/<database>?sslmode=require`), create, reset the password, delete.

#### Object storage (phase 2)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): S3-compatible buckets for projects, **for now on the production VPS**. Decision 48.

- **Server:** [Garage](https://garagehq.deuxfleurs.fr/) (`dxflrs/garage:v2.5.0`, one node, replication 1, its own volumes) in the `cloud-storage` service. Its S3 API answers at `https://s3.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>` (host Nginx with the wildcard certificate, path-style addresses, region `garage`, no upload size limit, buffering off); its admin API stays inside Docker (`CLOUD_STORAGE_ADMIN_URL`, `CLOUD_STORAGE_ADMIN_TOKEN`). `s3` is a reserved project slug. Without the settings the **Storage** tab says it isn't set up.
- **Bucket** (`CloudBucket` of a project; free: 2 per project, PRO: 10): a name of 3–63 lowercase letters, digits and dashes, unique across Cloud, which is also its name in Garage, and a quota (free 1 GB and 100,000 objects per bucket; PRO 50 GB and 1,000,000) set as Garage's bucket quota, so uploads past it are refused by Garage itself. Size and objects are read from Garage and shown.
- **Access keys** (`CloudBucketKey`, up to 10 per bucket): a name and read-only or read-write; made in Garage for that bucket only. The secret is shown once; only the key's id is kept. Deleting a key deletes it in Garage.
- **Use:** any S3 client or SDK (`endpoint https://s3.<domain>`, `forcePathStyle`, region `garage`); browsers get files through presigned URLs that the team's own server signs, or straight from a public bucket (below).
- **Public buckets** (owner's request 2026-11-17, decision 111): a bucket can be made **public read**, so anyone can download its files without a key, for images, downloads and other assets of a site. `CloudBucket.publicAt` (null: private). Free: 1 public bucket per project, PRO: 10 (`publicBucketsPerProject`; past it `409` "A project has at most <n> public buckets on this plan"; going back to FREE keeps the ones already public, as with every limit). Turning it on is refused for a suspended project (`403`).
  - **Garage** serves it through its website endpoint (`[s3_web]`, port 3902, published on `127.0.0.1:3902` only): the API sets the bucket's `websiteAccess` (`UpdateBucket { websiteAccess: { enabled: true, indexDocument: 'index.html' } }`, admin API v2) on and off. The website endpoint never lists a bucket: a path is a file (a folder's `index.html` when there is one), anything else is `404`.
  - **Address:** `https://s3.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>/_public/<bucket>/<key>` (`publicUrl` in the bucket's view, the prefix with a trailing `/`; null while private). The host Nginx site of the S3 API (`deploy/nginx/cloud-storage.conf.template`) gets one more location: `/_public/<bucket>/…` goes to Garage's website endpoint with `Host: <bucket>` (Garage takes a host that isn't under its `root_domain` as the bucket's global name) and the path after the bucket. `_` is never part of a bucket name, so the prefix can't clash with path-style S3 requests; no DNS record or certificate is needed (it is the same host). Only `GET`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` pass; `Authorization` and cookies are dropped; every answer gets `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` (an HTML file opens with scripts off and an opaque origin, so one bucket's file can't act on another's: sites belong in static hosting), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and HSTS. Reads count toward nothing (no egress limit for now; Nginx's per-IP limit of the S3 site applies).
  - **Safety:** the console lists a public bucket with a warning badge ("Public: anyone with the address can read every file") and asks for confirmation before turning it on; `codeqlick buckets public <bucket> on` asks too unless `--yes`. The bucket's quota and the disk guard apply as before (turning public on or off doesn't grow anything, so it is allowed while the disk is `full`). The 10-minute sweep (`CloudStorageService.sync`) turns website access off for buckets of deleted or suspended projects and sets it again when Garage's state differs from `publicAt` (so a restore or a manual change converges). The abuse rules of static hosting apply to public files.
- **CORS rules** (decision 111): a bucket can have one CORS rule, applied by Garage both to its S3 API (uploads from a browser with presigned URLs) and to public reads. `CloudBucket.cors` (JSON, null: none): `{ origins, methods, headers, exposeHeaders, maxAge }`, where `origins` is 1–10 values, each `*` or an origin `http(s)://host[:port]` (no path, at most 200 characters, lowercased); `methods` 1–5 of `GET`, `HEAD`, `PUT`, `POST`, `DELETE` (default `GET`, `HEAD`); `headers` up to 20 allowed request headers (letters, digits, `-`, or `*`; default `*`); `exposeHeaders` up to 20 (default `ETag`); `maxAge` 0–86,400 seconds (default 3,600). Sent to Garage as `UpdateBucket { corsRules: [{ AllowedOrigin, AllowedMethod, AllowedHeader, ExposeHeader, MaxAgeSeconds }] }` (Garage 2.5's admin API takes S3's field names); clearing sends `corsRules: []`. The sweep (above) sets the stored rule again when Garage's differs from it.
- **Changing a bucket:** `PATCH /cloud/projects/:id/buckets/:bucketId` `{ public?: boolean, cors?: CorsRule | null }` (ADMIN; read-only tokens refused), the changed bucket back. Garage is changed first; the row only when Garage accepted it. Events `bucket.public_enabled`, `bucket.public_disabled` (label the bucket's name), `bucket.cors_updated` (details `{ origins, methods }`) and `bucket.cors_cleared`; nothing is recorded when nothing changed.
- **Deleting a bucket** is refused while it holds objects (empty it first); then it and its keys are deleted.
- **API** (`/v1/cloud/projects/:id/buckets`, ADMIN to change; members read): list (`{ id, name, endpoint, region, sizeBytes, objects, maxBytes, maxObjects, public, publicUrl, cors, keys: [{ id, name, accessKeyId, write, createdAt }], createdAt }`, plus `publicLimit` and `publicCount` next to `items`), create `{ name }`, change (above), delete; `POST …/:bucketId/keys` `{ name, write }` → the key with `secretAccessKey` once; `DELETE …/:bucketId/keys/:keyId`.
- **Console:** the project's **Storage** tab: buckets with usage, keys, and the settings to paste into an S3 client; per bucket (ADMIN) a "Public read" switch with its confirmation, the public address to copy and the warning badge, and a "CORS" form (origins one per line, the methods as checkboxes, headers, max age; Save and Clear). Members see the state.

**Privacy and responsibility of databases and storage:** what teams keep there is theirs (Codeqlick is its processor, Cloud terms); the abuse rules of static hosting apply. Both run on the platform's server until they move to their own (owner's choice, November 2026).

#### Disk guard (one server)

Owner's choice (2026-11-13): everything stays on one machine for now (the platform, builds, `cloud-pg`, Garage and the backups share the VPS's disk), so Cloud must never fill it and take the platform down with it. Decision 49.

- **Measure:** `CloudDiskService` reads the free space of the disk holding `CLOUD_ROOT` (`statfs`) every minute. States: `ok`; `low` when less than `CLOUD_DISK_LOW_GB` is free (default 30); `full` when less than `CLOUD_DISK_MIN_GB` (default 15).
- **While `full`:** creating deployments (CLI uploads and build outputs), databases and buckets answers `507` ("Codeqlick Cloud is short of space; try again later"); every Cloud database is made read-only, as if it were over its size; and every bucket's Garage quota is lowered to its current size, so nothing in Cloud grows. Coming back to `ok` or `low` undoes it at the next check (databases every 10 minutes, quotas within the hour).
- **Builds:** a build is never refused for lack of space, but while the state is `full` the claim sends `cache: null` (build runs without the dependency cache, section "Builds from Git"); its output deployment is refused as above.
- **Telling people:** the server monitor (8.x, `deploy/monitor/monitor.sh`) already emails the team at 85 % of the disk and now also checks `cloud-pg`, `cloud-pg-backup` and `cloud-storage` when the `cloud-data` profile is on. Admin → Cloud shows the disk (size, free, state) and what Cloud keeps on it: deployments, databases and buckets.
- `GET /v1/admin/cloud/disk` (ADMIN): `{ state, totalBytes, freeBytes, lowBytes, minBytes, deploymentsBytes, databasesBytes, bucketsBytes }`.

#### Command line for analytics and the key-value store

`codeqlick` 0.4 (owner's choice 2026-11-13; the project is the one linked in the folder, or `--project`): `codeqlick analytics [--period 24h|7d|30d|90d|365d] [--json]` prints the totals (with the change against the previous period next to each, as in the console: `+12 %`, `-8 %`, `new`, nothing when both are 0 or the previous window isn't complete; `--json` has it in `previous`) and the top pages and sources; `codeqlick kv ls [prefix]`, `codeqlick kv get <key>`, `codeqlick kv set <key> <value> [--ttl seconds]` (the value is JSON, or text when it isn't; `-` reads standard input), `codeqlick kv rm <key>`, `codeqlick kv incr <key> [--by n]`. `--json` prints the API's answer.

#### Command line for monitors

Ships with the next CLI release (roadmap item "CLI: codeqlick monitors (list, pause, resume)"). No API change: it uses the monitor routes of section 5 (`GET /cloud/teams/:team/monitors`, `PATCH /cloud/monitors/:id`) with the person's token or an API token. Monitors belong to the team, not to a project, so the commands need no linked project.

- **Team:** the same rule as `codeqlick audit` and `config check` (`teamOf` in `cli.ts`): `--team <slug>`, else `CODEQLICK_TEAM`, else the `team` of the folder's `codeqlick.json`, else the only team of the account (`whoami`), else the error "Which team? Pass --team <slug> (or set CODEQLICK_TEAM)." (exit `1`).
- **`codeqlick monitors [--team <slug>] [--json]`:** lists the team's monitors, in the API's order. Columns: `name`, `kind` (`HTTP` or `HEARTBEAT`), `state` (`UP`, `DOWN`, `PAUSED` or `UNKNOWN`, from `status`; `PAUSED` also when `enabled` is false), the time in that state (`statusSince`) and the last check (`lastCheckedAt` for HTTP, `lastPingAt` for heartbeats; `-` when none). The list answer has no uptime figure (uptime is computed only for one monitor, `GET /cloud/monitors/:id`, which has the last 24 h of checks and `uptime30`), so the table shows none; the list stays one request. A team without monitors prints "No monitors yet" and exits `0`. `--json` prints the API's `{ items }` unchanged.
  - List fields (`MonitorView`): `id`, `name`, `kind`, `url`, `keyword`, `keywordMode`, `heartbeatUrl`, `periodMinutes`, `graceMinutes`, `projectId`, `enabled`, `status`, `statusSince`, `lastCheckedAt`, `lastPingAt`, `deadlineAt`, `lastError`, `certExpiresAt`, `alertIds`, `createdAt`.
- **`codeqlick monitors pause <id|name>` and `codeqlick monitors resume <id|name>`:** `PATCH /cloud/monitors/:id` with `{ enabled: false }` or `{ enabled: true }`, then print "Paused <name>" or "Resumed <name>" (`--json` prints the returned monitor). The argument is first matched as an id, then as a name **exactly** (case-sensitive) among the team's monitors. No match: "There is no monitor "<x>" in the team "<team>"." Several with that name: an error that lists their ids (and kinds), asking to use an id. Pausing a paused monitor, or resuming a running one, changes nothing and still succeeds (the CLI doesn't send the `PATCH` then). The API's own rules apply: pausing sets the state `PAUSED` and records the event; resuming sets `UNKNOWN` until the next check (a heartbeat gets a new deadline of period + grace from now), so `resume` does not print `UP`.
- **Who can:** listing needs any member of the team; `PATCH` needs **ADMIN** (or OWNER) of the monitor's team (`403` for a MEMBER, shown as the API's message). API tokens work like on every `@CloudApi()` route: a full token can pause and resume, a team-restricted token only in its team, and a **read-only** token can list but pause and resume answer `403` "This token is read only" (decision 61).
- **Exit codes** (as the rest of the CLI: `0` success, `1` any error): `1` for no team, no match, an ambiguous name, not signed in (`401`, "run `codeqlick login`") and any API error (`403`, `404`).
- **Use case:** pause the monitors around planned work so the team isn't alerted and the status page doesn't show an outage, e.g. in a deploy script: `codeqlick monitors pause "Web" && ./migrate.sh; codeqlick monitors resume "Web"` with `CODEQLICK_TOKEN` and `CODEQLICK_TEAM` set (a status-page maintenance notice, section "Monitors and status pages", is the public way to announce it).

#### Command line for databases and storage

`codeqlick` 0.6 (owner's request 2026-11-17, decision 111). The project is the one linked in the folder, or `--project`; every command takes `--json` (the API's answer, unchanged, on standard output) and exits `0` on success, `1` on any error (the API's message). No API change beyond reading one database (`GET /cloud/projects/:id/databases/:databaseId`) and changing a bucket (`PATCH …/buckets/:bucketId`, above).

- **Names:** `<name>` is matched first as an id, then exactly (case-sensitive) as a database's name or a bucket's name among the project's; no match: "There is no database "<x>" in <project>." (or bucket). Databases may share a name: several matches is an error that lists their ids.
- **Confirmations:** what can't be undone or exposes data asks `<question> [y/N]` on the terminal (only `y` or `yes` goes on); `--yes` (`-y`) skips it. Without a terminal (CI) and without `--yes` it refuses: "Pass --yes to confirm" (exit `1`), so a script never hangs or deletes by accident.
- **Databases:**
  - `codeqlick db ls`: name, size against its limit, read-only, the connection string without the password; "No databases yet" when empty.
  - `codeqlick db create <name>`: makes it and prints the full connection URL **once**, with a line saying it won't be shown again.
  - `codeqlick db url <name>`: the password isn't kept, so this **resets** it (`POST …/:databaseId/password`) and prints the new URL once; asks first ("Reset the password of <name>? Apps using the old one stop connecting.").
  - `codeqlick db rm <name>`: asks first ("Delete the database <name> and all its data?"), then `DELETE`.
  - `codeqlick db psql <name> [--reset]`: runs the local `psql` (from `PATH`; "psql isn't installed" otherwise, exit `1`) on the URL without the password, so `psql` asks for it; with `--reset` it resets the password first (asking as `db url` does) and hands it to `psql` through the `PGPASSWORD` environment variable, never on the command line. Standard input, output and the exit code are `psql`'s.
- **Buckets:**
  - `codeqlick buckets ls`: name, size and objects against the quota, `public` with the public address when it is, CORS origins, number of keys; the endpoint and region once at the top.
  - `codeqlick buckets create <name>`; `codeqlick buckets rm <name>` (asks first; the API refuses a bucket that holds objects).
  - `codeqlick buckets keys <bucket>`: the keys (name, access key id, read-only or read-write, created).
  - `codeqlick buckets key create <bucket> [--name <name>] [--write]`: a read-only key unless `--write` (name default `cli`); prints the access key id and the secret **once**, with the endpoint and region to paste into an S3 client.
  - `codeqlick buckets key rm <bucket> <key>` (`<key>` the key's id, access key id or name): asks first.
  - `codeqlick buckets public <bucket> on|off`: `on` asks first ("Anyone with the address will be able to read every file in <bucket>.") and prints the public address; `off` doesn't ask.
  - `codeqlick buckets cors <bucket>` prints the rule; `codeqlick buckets cors <bucket> set <origin...> [--methods GET,HEAD,PUT] [--headers a,b] [--max-age <seconds>]` replaces it; `codeqlick buckets cors <bucket> clear` removes it.
- **Who:** members list; the rest needs ADMIN (the API's `403`). A read-only token lists only.

#### Preview comments (phase 2)

Owner's request (2026-11-17): team members leave comments on a deployment's preview, pinned to a page and a place on it, as on Vercel; the console gathers them in an inbox. Decision 111.

- **Where:** only on **deployment addresses** (`<slug>--<short id>.<CLOUD_HOSTING_DOMAIN>`, Nginx's `$cq_kind` `previews`), never on production (`<slug>.<domain>`), custom domains or branch addresses (a branch address changes deployment, so its pins would move under them). Nothing is added to the user's files or to the pages visitors get: the comments open in a page of Codeqlick's own on the preview's host, and only for a team member who opened it from the console.
- **How it opens (no change to the site, no Nginx change):** `/__codeqlick/` of every hosting address is already proxied to the API (`/v1/cloud/protection/`, Password protection), with `X-Cq-Host`, `X-Cq-Kind` and the protection key that proves Nginx sent it. The console's "Comment" (on a deployment, and from the inbox) asks `POST /v1/cloud/projects/:id/comments/session { deploymentId, path? }` (a signed-in person: session only, a token gets `401`; any member; `403` suspended, `404` unknown deployment) and opens the `url` it answers: `https://<slug>--<short>.<domain>/__codeqlick/comments?path=<path>#token=<token>`. When previews are password-protected the `url` goes first through the members' ticket (`/__codeqlick/enter?ticket=…&next=<that path>`, `enter` now taking a safe `next`, default `/`), so the member never types the password. The fragment never reaches a server or a `Referer`; the page reads it, removes it from the address bar (`history.replaceState`) and keeps it in `sessionStorage` for reloads of that tab.
- **The comments page** (`GET /cloud/protection/comments`, HTML, EN/ES by `Accept-Language`): the preview in a full-size `<iframe>` of the same origin (`src` = `path`, a safe same-host path, default `/`), a small toolbar (comment mode on/off, the open count, show resolved, a link to the console's inbox) and the pins, drawn by the overlay script `GET /cloud/comments/overlay.js` (also at `/cloud/protection/comments/overlay.js`, so the page loads it as `/__codeqlick/comments/overlay.js` from its own origin). The page's own headers: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`. Being of the same origin, the script reads the frame's current path and document (to place pins as the visitor scrolls and navigates) without touching the site's code. A site that forbids being framed (`X-Frame-Options: DENY`, or `frame-ancestors 'none'` in its `codeqlick.json` headers) shows a note asking to allow `SAMEORIGIN` / `'self'` for comments.
- **A pin:** in comment mode a click on the page opens a box at that point; the thread stores `path` (the frame's path and query, 1–1,000 characters, starting with `/`), `x` and `y` (0–100, percent of the **page's** width and height, so the pin stays on the same spot while scrolling), an optional `selector` (a short CSS path of the clicked element, at most 500 characters: an `#id`, else up to 5 `tag:nth-of-type(n)` steps) used to scroll to and outline the element, and `viewportWidth` (the frame's width in pixels, 200–10,000, shown as "desktop"/"mobile" in the inbox). Pins of the current path are shown; resolved ones only with "show resolved".
- **Threads and comments** (`CloudCommentThread` of a project and a deployment; `CloudComment` of a thread): a thread is its first comment plus replies, `resolvedAt`/`resolvedById` (resolve and reopen by any member), `lastCommentAt` for the inbox's order. A comment: `body` (1–2,000 characters, plain text; never rendered as HTML, in the page or the console), its author (`authorId`, kept as `authorName` when the account goes), `mentionIds`, `createdAt`. Its author or a team admin deletes a comment; deleting a thread's last comment deletes the thread. Limits: 100 comments per thread, 2,000 threads per project (`409`), 30 comments a minute per person (`429`), and 60 requests a minute per IP on each of the overlay's writing routes (decision 109). A thread keeps `deploymentShort` (the 8 hex characters) when the hourly cleanup deletes its deployment (`deploymentId` set null); its pins can't be opened then, the inbox still shows it.
- **Mentions:** a comment carries `mentions` (up to 10 user ids, chosen in the box from the team's members by typing `@`; the body shows `@Name`). The API keeps only current members of the project's team, never the author, and emails each one once per comment (`cloudCommentMentionEmail` in `cloud-emails.ts`, plain text and HTML in the branded layout, in their language): "<author> mentioned you on <project>", the comment, the page and deployment, a button to the inbox (`cloud.codeqlick.com/{locale}/console/<team>/<project>?tab=comments`). A failed send is logged, never fails the comment.
- **The overlay's token** (no cookies on the preview host from the console host): `v1.<userId>.<projectId>.<deploymentId>.<expiry>.<signature>`, an HMAC-SHA256 of the rest and the deployment's host with a key derived from `CREDENTIALS_SECRET` (HKDF, purpose `cloud-comments-v1`, like the protection cookie), valid **2 hours**; the page says when it has expired and links to the console to open it again. It is sent as `X-Codeqlick-Comments` (a header, never a cookie, so there is nothing to forge across sites) to the overlay's API, which accepts it only when Nginx's key is right, `X-Cq-Kind` is `previews` and `X-Cq-Host` is the host it was signed for, and then checks again, on every call, that the person is active and still a member of the project's team (two-step rule included), the project is live and not suspended and the deployment exists. It reaches comments only, of that one deployment. The site's own scripts share the page's origin and could read it; that is the team's own code, and the token can do no more than comment on that preview for 2 hours.
- **Production never gets it:** a request with `X-Cq-Kind` `production` (the production address and every custom domain) answers `404` on every comments route, the page included; a token is bound to one deployment address, so it can't be used anywhere else; and nothing is injected into served files.
- **The overlay's API** (Nginx only, as `/__codeqlick/comments/api/…` on the deployment address; the headers above): `GET …/api/me` (`{ user: { id, name }, project: { name, slug }, deployment: { id, shortId }, members: [{ id, name }], inboxUrl, expiresAt }`), `GET …/api/threads` (the deployment's threads with their comments, every path, newest activity first), `POST …/api/threads` `{ path, x, y, selector?, viewportWidth?, body, mentions? }`, `POST …/api/threads/:threadId/comments` `{ body, mentions? }`, `PATCH …/api/threads/:threadId` `{ resolved }`, `DELETE …/api/threads/:threadId/comments/:commentId`.
- **The console's API** (session or token 🔑, any member; read-only tokens read): `GET /cloud/projects/:id/comments?status=open|resolved|all&deploymentId&path` (default `open`; up to 200 threads, newest activity first, with their comments; plus `open`, the count of open threads), `POST /cloud/projects/:id/comments/:threadId/comments` `{ body, mentions? }` (a reply), `PATCH /cloud/projects/:id/comments/:threadId` `{ resolved }`, `DELETE /cloud/projects/:id/comments/:threadId/comments/:commentId`, and the session above.
- **Events** (team activity): `comment.created` (a thread or a reply; label `<short id> <path>`, details `{ threadId, deploymentId, reply, mentions }`), `comment.resolved`, `comment.reopened`, `comment.deleted`.
- **Console:** the project's **Comments** tab (the inbox): open / resolved / all, each thread with its deployment, page, device width, comments and replies (a reply box with an `@` member picker), Resolve or Reopen, Delete on one's own comments (and admins' on any), and "Open on the preview" (the session above, in a new tab). The tab shows the number of open threads. Each deployment of the Deployments tab gets "Comment".
- **Deleted with the project:** soft-deleting a project deletes its threads (with its deployments and domains, in the same transaction), and the rows cascade from the project when it is purged; a project moved to another team keeps them. Comments are the team's data (Codeqlick is its processor); nothing is kept on the preview host.
- **Out of scope for now:** screenshots, attachments, comments from people outside the team (clients with a link), notifications in Slack or by webhook, comments on production.

#### Public documentation (docs.codeqlick.com)

Owner's request (2026-11-13), roadmap item "Cloud: public documentation" (CQP-152). Cloud gets a public, indexable documentation site in English and Spanish, for developers who haven't signed up. It replaces the short `/docs` page of the console (section 6.3). Decision 53.

- **Site:** `codeqlick-docs/`, an Astro 7.4.5 static site with TypeScript 6.0.3 (`astro check` refuses 7.0, decision 19). It uses **exactly the dependencies and versions of `codeqlick-sites/web-basica`** (its `package-lock.json` is copied, with the package name changed), so there is no new package to audit; `sharp`, `@astrojs/sitemap` and the formatter come along, and the template's other font packages simply go unused. No MDX, no UI kit, no client framework. Dependabot gets a group for the folder (section 9).
- **Content:** Astro content collections of plain Markdown: `src/content/docs/en/<slug>.md` and `src/content/docs/es/<slug>.md`, the same slugs in both languages. Frontmatter: `title`, `description` (used for the page's `<meta>` and in search), `group` (the sidebar group: `start`, `hosting`, `services` or `reference`), `order` and optional `draft: true`. A draft page is left out of the build: no route, no sidebar entry, no search entry, no sitemap line. Pages for features whose code is not merged yet are written with `draft: true`; whoever merges the feature removes the flag in the same pull request or right after. A page never links to a draft page except from another draft page (the link check would fail). A page and its twin must have the same `draft` value.
- **Writing:** second person, short sentences, a runnable example for every feature (`codeqlick` commands and `curl`), written for developers from the specs in this section: no server paths, table or service names, or other internals. Features are written as features, not as plans.
- **Routes:** `/en/<slug>/` and `/es/<slug>/`, `/en/` and `/es/` for the introduction. `/` is a tiny page that reads `navigator.language` and sends the visitor to `/en/` or `/es/` with a script; without JavaScript (and for crawlers) it shows two links. Every page has `<link rel="alternate" hreflang="en|es">` to its twin and `hreflang="x-default"` to `/`.
- **Pages** (the same list in both languages, `group` in brackets):

  | Slug | Page | Draft until |
  |---|---|---|
  | `index` | Introduction (what Cloud is, what is free) | |
  | `quickstart` | Quickstart: sign in with the CLI and deploy a folder | |
  | `static-hosting` | Static hosting: deployments, previews, rollbacks, single-page apps, limits | |
  | `custom-domains` | Custom domains: verify, point, states | |
  | `password-protection` | Password protection | its PR is merged (#209) |
  | `codeqlick-json` | `codeqlick.json`: redirects, rewrites, headers | its PR is merged (#211) |
  | `builds-from-git` | Builds from Git: connecting GitHub, build settings, previews of pull requests | |
  | `environment-variables` | Environment variables | |
  | `deploy-hooks` | Deploy hooks | its PR is merged (#210) |
  | `preview-comments` | Preview comments: pins, threads, mentions, the inbox | |
  | `build-notifications` | Build notifications: email, Slack, Discord, signed webhooks | |
  | `forms` | Forms | |
  | `monitors-and-status-pages` | Monitors, heartbeats and status pages | |
  | `website-check` | Website check API and the GitHub Actions use | |
  | `analytics` | Web analytics | |
  | `key-value-store` | Key-value store | |
  | `databases` | Databases | |
  | `object-storage` | Object storage | |
  | `cli` | CLI reference: every command and flag | |
  | `api-tokens` | API tokens and CI (`CODEQLICK_TOKEN`) | |
  | `plans-and-limits` | Plans and limits of the free plan, and PRO | |

  The sidebar groups them as *Get started*, *Hosting* (static hosting to deploy hooks, `build-notifications` and `preview-comments` included), *Services* (forms to object storage) and *Reference* (CLI, API tokens, plans). Usage alerts are written into `plans-and-limits` when they are merged.
- **Brand:** the tokens of section 7 as CSS custom properties, light and dark through `prefers-color-scheme` (no toggle), the "Click Q" logo inline as SVG next to the lowercase wordmark `codeqlick`, and `docs` as a label. The brand fonts are the **same three variable `woff2` files as the website** (Latin subset, OFL; section 3), copied into `public/fonts/` and loaded with `@font-face` and `font-display: swap`, so they come from the docs' own domain; there is no font package and no request to Google. The stacks fall back to `system-ui` and `ui-monospace`. Code blocks use Astro 7's default Markdown highlighting, styled with the brand tokens (`--code-bg`).
- **Search:** an Astro endpoint `src/pages/search-[lang].json.ts` writes, at build time, `/search-en.json` and `/search-es.json`: for each published page its `title`, `description`, URL, headings (text and anchor) and plain text (up to 20,000 characters; the script cuts a short snippet around the match when it shows a result). A vanilla script (no dependency, under 4 KB gzipped) loads the language's index the first time the box is used, splits the query into lowercase terms, and ranks pages by simple term matching (title weighs most, then headings, then text; every term must match; a heading hit links to its anchor). Keyboard: `/` focuses the box (unless typing in a field), Arrow keys move through the results, Enter opens one, Escape closes the list. Results are in a `role="listbox"` with `aria-activedescendant`. Without JavaScript the box is hidden and the sidebar still lists every page.
- **SEO:** a `<title>` of `<page> · Codeqlick Cloud docs`, a description and a canonical per page, `hreflang` as above, Open Graph (title, description, `og:locale`, one shared image), `@astrojs/sitemap` with its `i18n` option (`en`, `es`), `public/robots.txt` allowing everything and naming `https://docs.codeqlick.com/sitemap-index.xml`, heading anchors, and a 404 page (without canonical or `og:url`, since it answers at any missing path). The site never sets `noindex`: deployment addresses (`--`) get it from Cloud (Static hosting) and `docs.codeqlick.com` doesn't.
- **Checks:** `npm run linkcheck` (`scripts/linkcheck.mjs`, Node only) reads the built `dist/` and fails when an internal `href` or `src` doesn't resolve to a built file or folder with `index.html`, an anchor `#id` doesn't exist in its page, a page lacks its twin in the other language (or the twin's `draft` differs), or a page lacks `<title>`, description or canonical (the 404 page needs none).
- **Hosting (dogfooding):** a Cloud project of the Codeqlick team, slug `codeqlick-docs` (`docs` itself is a reserved slug), connected to this repository with Builds from Git: root folder `codeqlick-docs`, install `npm ci`, build `npm run build`, output `dist`, Node 24, production branch `main`, "build other branches" off and "build pull requests" off (CI builds the site on every pull request, and a Cloud preview for each would only cost builds). Merging to `main` publishes; the Deploy workflow of the platform doesn't touch it. Its custom domain is `docs.codeqlick.com`, allowed by `CLOUD_PLATFORM_DOMAINS` / `CLOUD_PLATFORM_TEAM_ID` (Custom domains). The site makes no API calls, so there is nothing else to configure. Owner's steps once the PR is merged and deployed: create the project and connect it, set the two variables (the team id, not its slug) in `/srv/codeqlick/.env` and redeploy, add the `A` record `docs.codeqlick.com` → `SITES_SERVER_IP` through OVH's DNS, add the domain to the project and verify it with the TXT record, then set `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` (below). Steps in `deploy/README.md`.
- **Console:** with `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` set (frontend server env, e.g. `https://docs.codeqlick.com`; empty by default), `/cloud/docs` answers `307` (temporary) to `<CLOUD_DOCS_URL>/<locale>/`, and every link to the documentation in the console, the landing and the emails uses `<CLOUD_DOCS_URL>/<locale>/` too (one helper, `cloudDocsUrl(locale)`). Without it nothing changes: the console keeps its own `/docs` page (`src/content/cloud-docs.ts`), which is removed in a later change once the site is live. The CLI package's `homepage` becomes `https://docs.codeqlick.com` (metadata only: no release, no version bump).
- **Out of scope:** versioned docs, comments, a language switcher that keeps the section's anchor, an API reference generated from OpenAPI (the live `/docs` of non-production environments stays the reference for the API's shapes), an "edit on GitHub" link.

#### Later phases (outline)

- **Later for databases and storage:** their own servers with off-server backups; custom domains for public buckets.
- **3 Apps:** containers from a Dockerfile, isolated in microVMs, on their own servers.

### 4.14 Newsletter (`modules/newsletter`)

Roadmap "Newsletter sign-up" (CQP-30): blog readers had no way to follow new posts besides RSS. A reader gives their email and language; every new post is emailed to them once.

- **Subscribers** are `NewsletterSubscriber` rows (section 4.2), not accounts: readers need no account (decision 67).
- **Sign-up is double opt-in.** `POST /newsletter` always answers `202`, so it never reveals whether an address is subscribed. A disposable address is refused as in `/auth/register` (`isDisposableEmail`, field error `email: disposable`). A filled honeypot (`website`) answers `202` and does nothing.
  - A new address is created as `PENDING`. A `PENDING` or `UNSUBSCRIBED` one gets a new confirmation token (48 hours) and becomes or stays `PENDING`; an `UNSUBSCRIBED` address is never reactivated without a new confirmation, so nobody can resubscribe someone who left. It receives "Confirm your subscription" with a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/confirm?token=…`, at most one such email per address every 10 minutes (`confirmSentAt`; inside that time nothing is sent). An `ACTIVE` address gets nothing.
  - The confirmation page asks for a click (a button and a Server Action), so a mail scanner that opens the link cannot confirm a sign-up made by someone else. `POST /newsletter/confirm` with the token sets `ACTIVE` and `confirmedAt` and clears the token and its expiry. `400` for an unknown or expired token. The `confirmedAt` date and the stored language are the proof of consent.
- **Unsubscribing** is one step and needs no sign-in. Every newsletter email (not the confirmation) carries `List-Unsubscribe: <PUBLIC_API_URL/newsletter/unsubscribe/:token>` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` (RFC 8058, which Gmail and Yahoo require of bulk senders), and a visible link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/newsletter/unsubscribe?token=…`. That page shows a button and the Server Action unsubscribes, so a link scanner opening the link with a GET cannot unsubscribe anyone. A mail client without one-click support opens the header's address in a browser: `GET /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` changes nothing and redirects (302) to that page in the subscriber's language (`en` for an unknown token, so it reveals nothing). Unsubscribing sets `UNSUBSCRIBED` and `unsubscribedAt` and keeps the row, so a later sign-up is told apart; it is idempotent. `MailMessage` (`modules/mail`) gains optional `headers`, passed to Nodemailer, for the two headers.
- **Sending** (`NewsletterService`, section 4.6): every 10 minutes it takes the posts that are live (`liveWhere()`: published and `publishedAt <= now`, so a scheduled post goes out when it appears) with `newsletterSentAt` null, oldest first, at most 3 per run (the rest wait for the next one). For each post it claims it **first** by setting `newsletterSentAt` only where it is still null (one atomic update; a post another process already claimed is skipped), so a post is emailed at most once even with two API processes or if the run crashes (a crash loses that email rather than sending it twice); then, only when `post.newsletter` is true, it emails every `ACTIVE` subscriber (the list is read again for each post), one by one with a 200 ms pause, in the subscriber's language: the post's title and excerpt and a link to `SITE_URL/{locale}/blog/{slug}`. The translation is the subscriber's language, falling back to English (`pickTranslation`; English always exists). Emails are branded like every other (plain text plus HTML, `email-layout.ts`). A failed send is logged and skipped, never retried; each post logs how many were sent and how many failed. A post with `newsletter` false is marked sent without emailing anyone. The migration sets `newsletterSentAt = now()` and `newsletter = false` on every post already live (a post scheduled for later still goes out), so nothing old is emailed and the editor truthfully says it was not.
- **Cleanup:** daily, `PENDING` subscribers whose `confirmExpiresAt` is more than 7 days past are deleted (nobody confirmed; about 9 days after sign-up, as the privacy policy says), and so are `UNSUBSCRIBED` rows whose `unsubscribedAt` is more than 12 months past (the address and date are kept that long only to respect and prove the opt-out).
- **Limits:** `POST /newsletter` 5/min/IP, `POST /newsletter/confirm` 10/min/IP, `POST /newsletter/unsubscribe` 10/min/IP, the one-click `POST /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` 60/min/IP (mail providers post from few addresses), `GET /newsletter/unsubscribe/:token` 30/min/IP.
- **Admin:** Admin → Newsletter lists subscribers with the counts per status, filtered by status and by part of the email, and deletes one on request (erasure; it can sign up again). The activity log records the deletion by id only, never the address. The post editor has "Email it to subscribers" (`newsletter`), disabled once `newsletterSentAt` is set, which it then shows.
