- **Privacy:** the data kept is the email, the language and the consent dates. The privacy policy (`content/legal`) says so: purpose, consent as the basis, unsubscribing from any email, unconfirmed sign-ups deleted after about 9 days, after unsubscribing only the address and date kept for 12 months, deletion on request at once.

### 4.14 Admin search (`modules/search`)

Roadmap proposal of 2026-11-14 ("Admin panel: search everything (Ctrl+K)"): leads, clients, projects, requests, proposals, posts and case studies live on separate admin pages, so finding one record means guessing where it is. One search box, opened with Ctrl+K or Cmd+K anywhere in the admin panel, finds any of them (section 6.2, "Admin search"). Decision 59.

**What is searched.** `GET /admin/search?q` (section 5). `q` is trimmed and 2–100 characters. Each kind matches `q` anywhere, case-insensitive (Prisma `contains` with `mode: 'insensitive'`, as the leads and users lists do), on names, emails, titles and short identifiers only; never on message bodies, notes, Markdown content, secrets or passwords.

| Kind | Matches on | `label` | `sublabel` | Opens (built by the website) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lead` | `name`, `email`, `company` (spam included) | `name` | `company · email` (what exists), or null | `/admin/leads/<id>` |
| `user` | `name`, `email`, `username` (`q` without a leading `@`, lower-cased); every role and kind, deactivated ones too | `name` | `email` | `/admin/users/<id>` |
| `project` | `name`, `domainName`, its managed site's `domain` and `slug`, its client's `name` and `email` | `name` | client's `name` | `/admin/projects/<id>` |
| `ticket` | `title`, its client's `name` and `email`, `number` | `title` | `#<number> · <client name>` | `/admin/support/<id>` |
| `proposal` | `title`, its client's `name` and `email`, `number` | `title` | `#<number> · <client name>` | `/admin/proposals/<id>` |
| `post` | `slug`, any translation's `title`; drafts and scheduled ones too | the title in `locale`, else English | `slug` | `/admin/posts/<id>` |
| `caseStudy` | `slug`, `clientName`, any translation's `title`; drafts too | the title in `locale`, else English | `clientName`, else `slug` | `/admin/portfolio/<id>` |

A `q` that is a number, with or without `#` (`^#?\d{1,9}$`), also matches the request and the proposal with that `number` exactly (`#12`; a one-digit number needs the `#`, as `q` takes at least 2 characters). Managed websites are not a kind of their own: a site's domain or name finds its project, whose page holds the site. Cloud teams and projects stay out (their admin page has its own search and no detail pages); bookings, tags, media and saved replies too.

**Results.** Up to 5 per kind, most recently changed first (`updatedAt` desc, then `id`), only kinds with matches, always in the table's order. The seven queries run in parallel and select only the fields shown. `sublabel` holds data only (names, emails, numbers, slugs), never words to translate. The API returns ids, not addresses: the website owns its routes and builds each link (`lib/admin-search.ts`), so a moved admin page changes nothing in the API.

**Access and limits.** `@Roles('ADMIN')`, so the two-step verification requirement (section 4.3) applies as on every admin route. 120 searches per minute per IP (`@Throttle`); the website forwards the admin's IP, so the limit is per person, not per web server. Searching changes nothing, so nothing goes to the activity log.

**Files.** `admin-search.controller.ts` (`GET /admin/search`), `search.service.ts` (number detection, labels, the locale fallback), `search.repository.ts` (read-only queries over the seven tables), `search.schemas.ts`, `search.service.spec.ts`; an e2e test covers `400` for a 1-character `q`, `403` for a client and the grouping.

### 4.15 Cruising (`modules/cruising`)

Codeqlick's fifth own web app, at **cruising.codeqlick.com**, English and Spanish, worldwide: a directory of gay cruising spots on a map with members' reviews (phase 1), then a members' social network around it (phase 2). Owner's request, 2026-11-15, after gays-cruising.com and mispicaderos.com; the owner chose a full social network on its own subdomain. Notion roadmap rows "Cruising, phase 1 (directory)" and "Cruising, phase 2 (social)". Decision 78. Built on Swipe's patterns (4.10): the same entry, reports, removal form, team digest, emails that reveal nothing and legal texts. `cruising` joins `WEBAPPS` (web and API) and `ADULT_WEBAPPS`; it is listed on `/apps` with an 18+ warning. Phases 1 and 2 are two pull requests.

#### Phase 1: directory

- **Entering:** a person can browse the map, the spot pages and the place lists with no account. To propose a spot, review one or report as a member they need a signed-in account that has entered: date of birth checked ≥ 18 and **not stored** (only `birthYear`), the rules, explicit consent to the processing of special category data (sexual orientation and sex life, GDPR art. 9.2.a, a separate checkbox) and `CRUISING_TERMS_VERSION` (a new version asks again). Declared age, as in decision 27. Entry creates the `CruisingProfile`.
- **Spots:** every spot has a `slug` (from the name, lower-case ASCII, `-2`, `-3` on a clash; it never changes), `name` (≤ 80), `type` (BEACH · PARK · FOREST · REST_AREA · SAUNA · BAR_CLUB · CINEMA · OTHER), `lat`/`lng` (stored rounded to 5 decimals), `countryCode` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, from the same fixed list as Swipe's `country`), `city` (≤ 80; `citySlug` is derived for the place pages), `description` (≤ 1000), `bestTimes` (≤ 200, optional, free text such as "weekday evenings"), `status` PENDING · LIVE · REJECTED · HIDDEN, `rejectReason`, `ratingAvg` (0–5, 2 decimals) and `ratingCount` (LIVE reviews only, recomputed in the same transaction as any review change). A member proposes up to 5 a day. **Every spot starts PENDING and only the team publishes it (LIVE) or rejects it with a reason** (the proposer is emailed, without any content from the spot). The rule: never near schools, playgrounds or places for children; moderators reject when in doubt. The team may correct the fields before publishing. The proposer edits or deletes their own PENDING or REJECTED spot (an edit of a REJECTED one sends it back to PENDING); a spot **REMOVED** by moderation (after a report or a removal request) is final: the proposer sees it as removed, with the reason, and can neither edit nor resend it (owner's decision, 2026-11-15; only the team can publish it again); a LIVE spot cannot be edited by them: "suggest a correction" is a report of type WRONG_INFO with details.
- **Map:** Leaflet (exact version, latest stable 1.x, installed from npm: no CDN) with OpenStreetMap tiles `https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` and the required attribution ("© OpenStreetMap contributors" linking to `https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright`). The Cruising host's CSP adds the tile host to `img-src`, and only that host allows `geolocation=(self)` in `Permissions-Policy` (every other host denies it). `GET /cruising/spots?bbox=minLng,minLat,maxLng,maxLat&type=` returns up to 500 LIVE spots in the box (public, no account; `truncated: true` when there were more; a box crossing the antimeridian is asked for as two). The browser's geolocation only centres the map on the device and is **never sent to the API**. The map asks only for whole cells of a 0.25° grid (each view's box is grown outwards to the grid), so no request reveals where the view, or a located device, is centred. The web loads spots through a route handler (BFF), and a click on the map offers "Propose a spot here" (opens `/new?lat=&lng=`). The tile provider can be replaced if traffic grows (OSM's tile usage policy; decision 78).
- **Place lists:** `GET /cruising/places` (countries with counts and their cities with counts) and `GET /cruising/places/:countryCode/:citySlug` (the spots of a city) are public, LIVE spots only. They feed `/places`, `/places/<country>` and `/places/<country>/<city>`.
- **Spot page:** `GET /cruising/spots/:slug` is public and returns LIVE spots only (otherwise `404`): name, type, country, city, coordinates, description, best times, rating average and count, `createdAt`. Indexed with JSON-LD `Place` (name, description, `geo`, `address` with country and locality), canonical and hreflang on its own host, `rating: adult`. The sitemap comes from `GET /cruising/sitemap`.
- **Reviews:** one per member per spot (`spotId` + `userId` unique), `rating` 1–5, `text` ≤ 1000 (optional), status LIVE · HIDDEN. Published at once (post-moderation: reports and the team deal with abuse), 10 a day (creating or editing counts). The text goes through the same contact filter as Swipe's chat (phone number, email, link, outside app or handle: refused with an explanation). Reviews are visible only to entered members (`GET /cruising/spots/:id/reviews` needs a session and an entered profile); the public page shows only the average and the count. The author shows as "Member" in phase 1 and as the nickname in phase 2. A member edits or deletes their own review.
- **Reports:** a member reports a spot, a review (phase 2 adds a profile and a conversation). Reasons: MINOR · ILLEGAL · DANGER · WRONG_INFO · HARASSMENT · SPAM · OTHER (WRONG_INFO only on a spot and with `details`), `details` ≤ 1000, 20 a day. MINOR and ILLEGAL **hide the spot or review at once** (HIDDEN) until reviewed, and MINOR on any target emails the team at once. The team dismisses (a hidden target goes back live if nothing else is open) or acts: remove (the spot becomes REMOVED, the review is deleted) and optionally suspend the person (24 h, 7 d, 30 d or permanent). A suspended member can browse but cannot propose, review, report or (phase 2) check in or message.
- **Removal requests (DSA):** a public form at `/{locale}/takedown`, no account needed (5/min/IP, honeypot): email, spot link (optional, `…/spots/<slug>` or just the slug), reason and details. A matching LIVE spot is hidden at once **only when the reason is MINOR or ILLEGAL** (owner's decision, 2026-11-15: otherwise anyone could hide the directory from an anonymous form); for any other reason it stays live until the team decides. The team is emailed and the person gets an acknowledgement. Answered from `/admin/cruising` within 24 hours: remove (the spot becomes REMOVED) or close (restores a hidden one); the person is emailed the answer in their language.
- **Emails to people** (in their account's language, never with a spot name, a nickname or a message, so a glance at the inbox gives nothing away): spot published, spot rejected (with the reason), spot removed after a report (with the reason), suspension (with the reason and end), and in phase 2 unread messages. `emailAlerts` (default true) turns off the "spot published" and unread-message emails; the team's decisions are always mailed.
- **Team digest:** every 30 minutes, when something new is waiting (pending spots, open reports, open removal requests) since the last digest, `LEADS_NOTIFY_TO` gets one email with the counts and the link to `/admin/cruising`, at most once an hour. MINOR reports and removal requests still email at once.
- **Own legal texts:** `/{locale}/terms-of-use`, `/{locale}/privacy-policy` (owner from `LEGAL_OWNER_*`, like Swipe's) and `/{locale}/rules`. The rules: adults only; consent always; never where minors or uninvolved people can see or be affected; respect the place and local law; no sale of sex or drugs; no photos of people without consent; report danger. The privacy policy names the special category data (sexual orientation and sex life, GDPR art. 9.2.a: explicit consent on entry), what is kept and for how long, OVHcloud as the only processor, the rights, and that the map's tiles are loaded from the OpenStreetMap Foundation's servers, which receive the visitor's IP address and the tile requested. All three are linked from the entry screen, the footer and each other.
- **Privacy:** the date of birth isn't stored (only its year); the device's position never reaches the API; the account export includes the Cruising profile, the spots proposed, reviews and reports made (phase 2: check-ins and messages sent); deleting the account deletes the profile, reviews and reports made, and the spots stay with `createdById` null.
- **Not in phases 1 and 2:** ads, photos of people (phase 4) and Web Push (phase 3).

#### Phase 2: social

- **Profile:** `nickname` (unique, 3–20 characters `[a-z0-9_]`, lower-cased on save; reserved words (names that pose as the team or the platform) are refused), `bio` (≤ 160), `country?` (fixed list), `lookingFor` (any of CHAT · FRIENDS · DATES · FUN · GROUP), `showAge` (default true; the age shown is the current year minus `birthYear`). Text only in phase 2; phase 4 adds photos and videos. Visible within the app, and only to entered members with a nickname: `GET /cruising/members/:nickname`. A nickname is needed for check-ins and messages; it can't be removed once set, but it can be **changed whenever the member wants, like on Instagram** (owner's decision 2026-11-15, decision 83: no limit, no waiting); the nickname left stays reserved for its owner for 14 days (`CruisingNicknameChange`: nobody else can take it, the owner can go back to it), so nobody can pose as someone right after a change. Conversations, blocks, reports and check-ins follow the account, not the nickname.
- **Check-ins:** "I'm here" at a LIVE spot for 1, 2 or 3 hours (`expiresAt`), one active per person (a new one ends the previous), with an `anonymous` flag; `DELETE` ends it; 10 a day. The spot page, for entered members, shows "N here now" and the nicknames of the non-anonymous ones (never the blocked, never the suspended). **The person's position is never stored or asked for**: a check-in is the spot and nothing else. The background task deletes check-ins that expired more than 24 hours ago.
- **Messages:** a conversation between two members (`userAId` < `userBId` unique) with text messages ≤ 1000 characters, 20 a minute, and at most 10 new conversations a day, counted from `CruisingConversationStart` (one row per conversation opened, kept a day even when the conversation is deleted, so leaving or blocking doesn't refund it). It starts from a profile, a check-in or a review: all three give a nickname. Until each side has sent 5 messages, the contact filter of 4.10 applies. The web polls every 4 s. Unread messages: a check every 10 minutes emails once per conversation when the partner's messages have waited 15 minutes unread (`notifiedAAt`/`notifiedBAt`), and not again until they are read; it respects `emailAlerts`. Leaving a conversation deletes it for both, like ending a Swipe match.
- **Blocks:** blocking a person hides both from each other everywhere: check-ins, authors of reviews (their reviews drop out of each other's lists), profiles (`404`), the conversation (deleted) and new messages. A block is lifted from the member's blocks list.
- **Reports** gain the targets profile and conversation; a report on a conversation saves its last 30 messages with the report, which survive the conversation being deleted.
- **Export and deletion** include check-ins and messages sent; deletion also deletes the conversations the person was in.

#### Phase 3: seeding, favourites and search

Owner's request, 2026-11-16: production had no LIVE spot, so the directory gives nothing to a newcomer, and members had no way to follow places or to find where people are now. Notion rows "Cruising: seeding the directory", "Cruising: favourites and alerts", "Cruising: search and active now".

- **Seeding the directory (decision 84).** The team adds spots without the review queue:
  - `POST /admin/cruising/spots` creates one spot with the same fields and checks as a proposal, already **LIVE** (or PENDING when asked), `createdById` null (a team spot has no proposer to email). Recorded in the activity log.
  - `POST /admin/cruising/spots/import` takes a file's text, `format` `csv` or `geojson` (≤ 2 MB, ≤ 500 rows), and `dryRun`. CSV has a header row with the columns `name, type, lat, lng, countryCode, city, description, bestTimes` (any order; `bestTimes` optional; comma or semicolon separated, quoted fields allowed, UTF-8 with or without BOM). GeoJSON is a `FeatureCollection` of `Point` features whose `properties` carry the same fields (coordinates from the geometry). `type` accepts the enum or its English or Spanish label, any case; `countryCode` the two letters, any case.
  - Every row is checked like a proposal; a row is **skipped** with its line (CSV, header = 1) or index (GeoJSON) and a reason when it is invalid, or when a spot that isn't REJECTED or REMOVED, or an earlier row of the same file, of the same `type` lies within **100 m** (a duplicate). The dry run creates nothing and answers what would be created and skipped; the real run creates the valid rows, each with its own slug, LIVE (or PENDING with `publish: false`), in one transaction, and records one activity entry with the count.
  - Admin → Cruising gets an "Add spots" tab: the one-spot form (with "publish now") and the import (choose a file, see the dry run's table, then import).
- **Favourites and alerts (decision 85).**
  - An entered member saves up to **100** LIVE spots as favourites (`CruisingFavorite`, `userId` + `spotId` unique). The spot page has a star; `/favorites` lists them with "N here now". A spot leaving LIVE drops out of the list (the row stays, so it comes back if the spot does); deleting the account deletes the favourites.
  - **Check-in alert:** when someone checks in at a spot, each member who saved it is alerted that there is someone at one of their favourite spots, **never who** (an anonymous check-in alerts too). Not the person checking in, not anyone blocked by or blocking them, not suspended members. At most one alert per member and spot every **3 hours** and **10 a day** per member (`CruisingAlert` rows, kept 2 days).
  - **Web Push**, the first channel: the member turns on notifications in `/me` (the browser's permission, a `CruisingPushSubscription` per device, up to 5, deleted when the push service answers 404 or 410). The Cruising host gets its own service worker (`/cruising-sw.js`, scope the app). The notification's text names neither the spot nor a person ("Someone is at one of your favourite spots", "New message"); opening it goes to `/favorites` or `/messages`. New messages also push at once (the unread email after 15 minutes stays). Off without the VAPID keys, as the video chat's.
  - **Email**, when the member has no push subscription: at most **one a day** ("There's activity at your favourite spots", nothing more), and only with `favoriteEmails` on (default **off**; `emailAlerts` off turns it off too).
  - Settings in `/me`: notifications on this device, `favoriteAlerts` (default on: push for favourites), `favoriteEmails`.
  - **Built as:** `CruisingFavorite` (`userId` + `spotId` primary key), `CruisingAlert` (`userId`, `spotId`, `channel` PUSH | EMAIL, `createdAt`; deleted after 2 days by the hourly cleanup) and `CruisingPushSubscription` (`endpoint` unique, `p256dh`, `auth`); the profile gains `favoriteAlerts` and `favoriteEmails`. The alerts go out after the check-in has answered (`CruisingSocialService.alertFavorites`; errors are logged), and only to members entered with the current rules. `CruisingPushService` sends with `web-push` and the video chat's VAPID keys; the notification's language is the account's. Favourites and the last 2 days of alerts are in the account export. A member with a device subscribed and `favoriteAlerts` off gets no favourite alerts (no email either: the email is only for members without push).
- **Search and active now (decision 86).**
  - `GET /cruising/search?q=` (public, 2–60 characters, accents and case ignored): up to 8 **places** (cities with LIVE spots: `{ countryCode, city, citySlug, count, bbox }`, the box around their spots) and up to 8 **spots** (`CruisingSpotSummary`, by name). The map gets a search box: choosing a city fits the map to its box, a spot opens it.
  - The map's filters: type (as now) and minimum rating (`minRating` 1–5 on `GET /cruising/spots`, spots with no rating are left out when set).
  - **Active now** is only for entered members: `GET /cruising/active?bbox=` returns `{ items: [{ spotId, here }] }` for LIVE spots in the box with active check-ins (`here` counts anonymous ones, never the blocked, never the suspended), on the same 0.25° grid as the map. The map marks them (a pulsing ring and the count) with an "Active now" toggle that shows only them, and `/places/<country>/<city>` shows "N here now" next to each spot and an "Active now" section first. Signed out, nothing about presence is shown or asked for.
  - Nothing about the searcher's position is sent: search is by text, and active now uses the grid like the map.

#### Phase 4: profile photos and videos

Owner's request, 2026-11-16: members upload photos and videos of themselves to their profile. The owner chose: **explicit content allowed**, seen by every entered member; **published at once and reviewed afterwards** (post-moderation); up to **12 photos and 4 videos of up to 60 seconds**. Decision 87. Notion row "Cruising: profile photos and videos". Built on Swipe's photo pipeline (4.10).

- **Verification is optional (owner's decision, 2026-11-16).** Any member with a nickname uploads; verification only gives the badge below. Once, a member with a nickname may send a photo of themselves holding a paper with their nickname, "Cruising" and the date (`POST /cruising/verification`, like Swipe's); the team approves or rejects it in `/admin/cruising` and only the team sees it (deleted once reviewed). Moderators reject anyone who doesn't clearly look adult. With no check before uploading, the safeguards are the consent at every upload, the immediate hiding on serious reports, the team's queue of new media and the blocked hashes.
- **Verified badge (owner's request, 2026-11-16):** a member whose verification photo was approved carries a **"Verified" badge** (a fuchsia tick with the word, `verified: true` in every payload that names a member) next to their nickname everywhere: their profile, presence lists and check-ins, reviews, conversations and the messages list. Its tooltip and the help text say what it means without overstating it: "The team checked a photo of this person holding their nickname: the profile is theirs and they are an adult" (no identity document is asked for, as in Swipe, so the badge never claims a checked ID). Any member with a nickname can verify, with or without media; the badge is the incentive. The team can withdraw it (on a report or a suspension), and it goes when the nickname changes and the member hasn't sent a new photo with the new one. `/me` shows the state (not sent, pending, verified, rejected with the note) and the button to send the photo.
- **Uploading:** JPEG/PNG/WebP photos up to 10 MB and MP4/MOV/WebM videos up to 100 MB and **60 s**; up to **12 photos and 4 videos** active per member, 20 uploads a day. Every upload asks for explicit consent: "only I appear, or everyone who appears is 18+ and agreed to be here; I took it or let it be taken; I want it here". Photos are re-encoded with sharp as Swipe's (rotation applied, ≤ 1600 px, WebP, metadata stripped, dHash checked against the blocked hashes). Videos are transcoded by a queue in the API, one at a time and at low priority (`ffmpeg`, `nice`): H.264/AAC MP4, ≤ 720p, ≤ 60 s (longer is refused), `-map_metadata -1` (no camera data or location), `faststart`, plus a WebP poster frame whose dHash is checked like a photo; the item is PROCESSING until done and FAILED (with the reason, file deleted) when it can't be read. Files live in `CRUISING_UPLOADS_DIR` (mode 0600, its own Docker volume, out of the backups like Swipe's), named `<uuid>.webp` / `<uuid>.mp4`; a cleanup every 6 hours deletes files no row points to.
- **Disk:** uploads are refused (`507`) while the server's free disk is under the Cloud disk guard's low mark (decision 49), so media can't fill the single server.
- **Order and cover:** the member orders their items and picks the cover photo (the first photo by default), shown next to their nickname in presence lists, reviews and conversations.
- **Seeing media:** only entered members, never signed-out visitors and never search engines. `GET /cruising/media/:id/file` (and `/poster`) streams to its owner, an admin, or an entered member who can see the profile (LIVE item, not blocked either way, owner not suspended), else `404`; the web proxies it with `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and supports HTTP range requests for video. A watermark overlay (nickname · cruising.codeqlick.com), no dragging or long-press saving. The profile page shows a gallery (photos and videos with poster, played on tap, muted by default).
- **Post-moderation:** an item is **LIVE as soon as it is processed**. Every new item also enters the team's **"New media" queue** in `/admin/cruising` until a moderator marks it reviewed or removes it; the team digest counts it. Reports gain the target `media` with the reasons MINOR · NON_CONSENSUAL · STOLEN · ILLEGAL · HARASSMENT · SPAM · OTHER: **MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL, STOLEN and ILLEGAL hide the item at once** until reviewed, and MINOR emails the team at once. The team removes an item (its image is blocked by hash), and may suspend the member and withdraw their verification. The DSA removal form accepts a media reference (each item shows a short ref) and hides it at once for those same reasons.
- **Legal:** `CRUISING_TERMS_VERSION` moves on (everyone accepts again): the terms allow sexual content of oneself between adults, forbid anyone under 18, anyone who didn't consent and anything illegal, and say uploads are published before review; the privacy policy adds photos and videos (special category data, explicit consent at upload) and their retention. The rules page gets the same lines.
- **Privacy:** the account export lists the media (refs, kinds, statuses, dates); deleting a media item or the account deletes the files within 6 hours; the verification photo is deleted once reviewed (approved or rejected), only the decision is kept.
- **Deploy:** the API image gains `ffmpeg`; the Nginx sites of the Cruising web host and the API allow bodies up to 100 MB on the upload route only (everything else keeps 1 MB).

#### Phase 5: reasons to come back, trust and safety

Owner's request, 2026-11-16 (after phases 3 and 4). No paid features: Stripe doesn't allow adult businesses, so nothing here charges. Everything about presence, people or places' activity is **only for entered members**; signed-out visitors see what phase 1 shows plus the spots' structured information and the testing centres. Texts members write go through the contact filter of 4.10 and can be reported. Notion rows "Cruising: …" for each part. Decisions 88–96.

- **Meetups (decision 88).** An entered member with a nickname plans a meetup at a LIVE spot: `startsAt` between 30 minutes and 30 days ahead, 1–4 hours long, `title` ≤ 80, `description` ≤ 500, optional `capacity` (2–50). Others mark "I'm going" (`CruisingMeetupAttendee`, with `anonymous` like a check-in: counted, not named); the host can cancel (attendees are pushed/emailed that a meetup they joined was cancelled, never its name). The spot page and `/meetups` (upcoming in the member's favourite spots and countries) list them; the city page shows the city's upcoming meetups. Members who saved the spot are alerted like a check-in (decision 85's channels and caps, "A meetup was planned at one of your favourite spots"). 3 new meetups a week per member; reports gain the target `meetup`; blocked people don't see each other's meetups or attendance; a meetup is deleted 7 days after it ends.
- **Busiest hours (decision 89).** Each check-in adds one to every hour of the week it covers, in the spot's **local time** (the spot's IANA time zone is computed from its coordinates when it is created or moved, with a small offline lookup library, exact version), in `CruisingSpotHour` (`spotId`, `day` 0–6, `hour` 0–23, `weight`). A nightly job multiplies every weight by 0.98, so the last few months count most. The spot page shows a week chart (bars per hour, the busiest marked) only once at least 10 check-ins were ever counted there, and only to entered members. No person, check-in or timestamp is kept for it: only the counters.
- **Traveller mode (decision 90).** A member with a nickname announces a trip: a city (one with LIVE spots, chosen from the search) and dates (`from` ≤ 60 days ahead, at most 14 days long), up to 3 upcoming trips. The city page shows entered members "Visiting soon" (nicknames, dates, verified badge; not blocked, not suspended), from which they can open a conversation; the traveller sees the city's busiest spots and upcoming meetups on their trip card. A trip is deleted the day after it ends. Hidden option: a trip only for the member (to plan), not listed.
- **Safety alerts (decision 91).** On a LIVE spot, an entered member posts an alert: POLICE · THEFT · AGGRESSION · SURVEILLANCE · CLOSED · OTHER, with a note ≤ 200. It lasts 6 hours (CLOSED: 7 days). Other members **confirm** it (each confirmation extends it by 3 hours, up to 24 hours from the first) or mark it **gone** (three "gone" end it). The spot page and the map (a warning marker) show active alerts with their age and confirmations to entered members; members who saved the spot are alerted ("Safety alert at one of your favourite spots", decision 85's caps). 5 alerts a day per member; reports gain the target `alert`; the poster is never shown.
- **Meetups, trips and safety alerts are built as** (decisions 88, 90, 91): `CruisingMeetup` (`status` LIVE · HIDDEN · CANCELLED; `capacity` counts attendees, not the host) and `CruisingMeetupAttendee` (`meetupId` + `userId`, `anonymous`); `CruisingTrip` (`startsOn`/`endsOn` calendar days in UTC, both included, `listed`); `CruisingSafetyAlert` (`posterId` set null when the account goes, so the warning stays; `hidden` by a serious report) and `CruisingSafetyVote` (one CONFIRM or GONE per member and alert, never on one's own). `CruisingCommunityService` (with `CruisingCommunityRepository` and `CruisingCommunityController`) checks entry, nickname (meetups and trips) and suspension; titles, descriptions and notes go through the contact filter (`400`). A meetup's or alert's savers are told through `CruisingSocialService.alertFavorites(spot, actor, now, kind)` (the same `CruisingAlert` caps and channels as a check-in; the email is the same daily "activity at your favourite spots"); push texts: "A meetup was planned at one of your favourite spots" (opens `/meetups`), "Safety alert at one of your favourite spots" (opens `/favorites`). Cancelling pushes "A meetup you joined was cancelled" to attendees with a device, or emails those without one (unless `emailAlerts` is off); no title, spot or person. Blocks: a hidden host's meetups are left out and `404` to join; hidden attendees are neither counted nor named; a joined meetup counts its real attendees for `full`. "Visiting soon" lists listed trips of members with a nickname, entered with the current rules, not suspended, not blocked either way, never mine. A trip card shows the city's 5 spots with the most people now (then best rated) and the city's meetups during the trip. Reports: `meetupId` / `alertId` on `POST /cruising/reports`; MINOR and ILLEGAL hide a meetup or alert at once, a dismissal restores it when nothing else is open, the action's `remove` deletes it; the team sees the host or the poster. An hourly cleanup deletes meetups 7 days after they end, trips the day after their last day and alerts a day after they expire. The account export lists meetups planned and joined, trips and the alerts posted.
- **Structured spot information (decision 92).** Spots gain `tags` (any of NUDIST · DAY · NIGHT · PARKING · PUBLIC_TRANSPORT · HARD_ACCESS · LIT · SECLUDED · INDOOR · ACCESSIBLE · GLORY_HOLES · DARKROOM), `openingHours` (≤ 200, optional) and `tips` (≤ 500, optional). The proposal form, the team's correction, the one-spot form and the import (a `tags` column, separated by `|`, enum or label) set them. The map filters by tags (all of the chosen); the public spot page lists them (and `amenityFeature` in its JSON-LD) for search engines.
- **Helpful reviews and contributor badges (decision 93).** Entered members mark a review **helpful** (`CruisingReviewVote`, one per member per review, not their own); reviews are sorted by helpful votes, then newest. Badges, computed from counters kept on the profile: **Explorer** (3 spots of theirs LIVE), **Guide** (10), **Helpful** (10 helpful votes received), **Local** (25 reviews). Shown on the profile and, small, next to the nickname on reviews.
- **Sexual health (decision 94, opt-in).** Off unless the member turns it on, with its own explicit consent (GDPR art. 9.2.a, separate from entry's): `prepStatus` (ON_PREP · NOT_ON_PREP · null), `lastTestedMonth` (`YYYY-MM`), and `healthVisible` (default **off**: when off only the member sees them; on: entered members see them on the profile). Reminders: every 3 or 6 months after `lastTestedMonth`, a push or email "Time for your check-up" (nothing more). **Testing centres**: `CruisingHealthCenter` curated by the team (name, address, `lat`/`lng`, country, city, `url?`, `phone?`, `free`, `anonymous`, `rapid`), added one by one or imported like spots (decision 84's import, `kind: health`); public `/health` page with a map and a list per city, indexed. Turning the feature off deletes the fields.
- **"Check on me" timer (decision 95).** A member names up to 2 **trusted contacts** by email; each gets an email asking to accept (double opt-in, in the member's language, naming the member as they chose: a display name ≤ 40, not the nickname) and can refuse or stop at any time. Then, when checking in or on its own, the member starts a timer (30 minutes to 6 hours) with an optional message ≤ 200 and a choice to share the spot (its name and an OpenStreetMap link) or not. 10 minutes before the end they get a push/email to extend or end it. If it runs out without being ended, the contacts get an email: the display name, the member's message, the time, and the spot only if they chose to share it; the email names Codeqlick's safety alert, not the app. One active timer; the timer rows are deleted 7 days after they end. SMS later (it needs OVH SMS credits): not in this phase.
- **Installable app (decision 96).** The Cruising host gets a web app manifest (`/manifest.webmanifest` on that host: name, short name, icons 192/512 and maskable, `start_url` the map, `display: standalone`, theme colours), Apple touch icon and meta tags, and the service worker of decision 85 also caches an offline page ("You're offline") and the app shell. An "Install the app" hint in `/me` when the browser offers it.

#### Phase 6: community, notifications, onboarding, languages

Owner's request, 2026-11-16 (after phase 5). No paid features (Stripe, decision 88's note). Everything that shows people is only for entered members, respects blocks both ways and hides suspended members; texts go through the contact filter and can be reported. Notion rows "Cruising: …". Decisions 97–103.

- **Winks and profile visits (decision 97).** A member with a nickname sends a **wink** to another (one per pair a day, 30 a day; `CruisingWink`); the recipient is told (notification centre and a push "Someone winked at you", nothing more) and can wink back or open a conversation. **Profile visits** are reciprocal and **off by default** (`showVisits`, GDPR art. 25): only members who turned it on are recorded when they open a profile and see who opened theirs (`CruisingProfileView`, one row per viewer and profile a day, kept 30 days; the last 100 visitors, newest first). Turning it off deletes their recorded visits both ways.
- **Notification centre (decision 98).** `CruisingNotification` (`userId`, `kind`, `data` with ids only, `readAt`, `createdAt`), kept 30 days and at most 200 per member: new messages (one per conversation until read), winks, visits (a daily summary count), favourite-spot activity, meetups planned/cancelled at favourites or joined, safety alerts at favourites, a private album shared with me, my spot published/rejected/removed, media removed, verification decided. A bell with the unread count in the app's header (refreshed every minute and on navigation), `/notifications` lists them with links and "mark all as read". Push and email stay as they are; the centre is written whenever one of those would be, and also when the member has neither.
- **Private album (decision 99).** Media (phase 4) gain `album`: PUBLIC (the profile, as now) or PRIVATE (up to another 12 photos and 4 videos, same rules, same moderation and reports). From a conversation, a member **shares their private album** with that person (`CruisingAlbumGrant`, owner + grantee) and can **revoke** it any time from the conversation or `/me`; a block or leaving the conversation revokes it. The grantee sees the private items on the profile and in the conversation; file access checks grow the grant. The grantee is notified (centre, push "Someone shared their private album with you").
- **City chat (decision 100).** One room per city with LIVE spots (`countryCode` + `citySlug`; no row until the first message). Entered members with a nickname whose account is over 24 hours old post text messages ≤ 500 (`CruisingRoomMessage`), 10 a minute and 200 a day; **no contact details ever** (the contact filter always applies in rooms) and no links. The web polls every 5 s; messages are kept 7 days. Blocked people's messages are hidden from each other. Reports gain the target `room_message` (MINOR, ILLEGAL and HARASSMENT hide it at once); the team deletes messages and can **mute** a member from rooms (24 h, 7 d, 30 d, permanent; `roomMutedUntil`). The city page links its room; `/rooms` lists the rooms of my favourites' cities, my trips' cities and my country's busiest.
- **Private album and city chat are built as** (decisions 99, 100): `CruisingMedia.album` (PUBLIC · PRIVATE, existing rows PUBLIC); `POST /cruising/media` takes a multipart `album` field and `PATCH /cruising/media/:id` `{ album }` moves an item (`409` when that album is full; a private photo is never the cover). `CruisingAlbumGrant` (`ownerId` + `granteeId`); sharing needs an open conversation and pushes "Someone shared their private album with you" only on a new grant (`CruisingAlbumService`, the hook point for the notification centre); deleting the conversation or a block deletes the grants both ways in the same transaction. `CruisingRoomMessage` (no room row); `CruisingProfile.roomMutedUntil` (a permanent mute is stored as 9999-12-31). `GET …/rooms/:countryCode/:citySlug/messages` also answers `posting: { allowed, reason: nickname · new-account · muted · suspended \| null, until }` so the composer can explain itself. Room messages refuse anything the Swipe contact filter catches plus any link (a scheme, `www.`, or a domain followed by a path). Reports keep the message text in `transcript`; the report's `roomMessageId` is set null when the message is purged (the 7-day purge runs in the hourly phase 5 cleanup). The admin report list gives `target: { type: 'ROOM_MESSAGE', roomMessage: { id, countryCode, citySlug, hidden } \| null }` and `reported.roomMutedUntil`. The account export lists the album grants given and the room messages.
- **Admin statistics (decision 101).** `/admin/cruising/stats`: for the last 12 weeks, per week, new members (entries), active members (any action), new spots by status, check-ins, meetups, messages and room messages, media uploaded, reports opened and decided with the median time to a decision, removal requests; plus the top 10 countries and cities by LIVE spots and by check-ins in the last 30 days. Aggregates only, computed on request in SQL and cached 10 minutes; no person is listed.
- **Onboarding (decision 102).** After entering, a skippable tour of 4 steps (`onboardingDoneAt`): choose a nickname; save favourites from suggestions (`GET /cruising/suggestions`: the best-rated and most active LIVE spots in the member's country, or near the map's last view on the grid); turn on notifications on this device; add a photo or verify (both optional). It can be reopened from `/me`. The tour shows above the map (`/map`) while `onboardingDone` is false; each step reuses what `/me` does (its profile form with the country, the favourite star, the push switch, links to the photos and the verification). "Near the map's last view" is the map's `?lat=&lng=` when the page was opened on a place: a box of ±0.5° around it grown to the grid; otherwise the member's country.
- **More languages (decision 103).** The Cruising host serves **French, German, Italian and Portuguese** besides English and Spanish (`/fr`, `/de`, `/it`, `/pt`): every Cruising page, its legal texts, metadata, sitemap and hreflang, and Cruising's emails and push texts. `Locale` gains `FR`, `DE`, `IT`, `PT` (the account's language); emails outside Cruising fall back to English for them, and the main site stays in English and Spanish (its hosts redirect the new prefixes to English). Translations are made in the code from the English texts; the owner is advised to have them reviewed by native speakers, especially the legal texts. Done last, after the rest of phase 6, so it covers their texts too.

### 4.16 Adult apps: shared safety, linked profiles, calls and inbox (`modules/adult`)

Owner's request, 2026-11-17: the three 18+ apps (`ADULT_WEBAPPS`: Cruising 4.15, Videochat 4.7, Swipe 4.10) stop being three islands. The owner chose all four parts: **shared safety, always on**; an **optional linked profile**; a **video call from a conversation**; **one inbox and one notifications bell**; and, where it overlaps, Swipe and Videochat brought to Cruising's level (installable app, verified badge, six languages). Notion roadmap rows "18+ apps: …", one per phase. Decisions 112–120.

A new module `modules/adult` holds what the three share (`AdultSafetyService`, `AdultVerificationService`, `AdultInboxService`, `AdultNotificationService`, `AdultCallService`, `AdultLinkService`, each with its repository and controller). The app modules call it; it reads and writes the apps' tables only through their repositories (the layering rule stays). `AdultApp` is the enum `CRUISING · SWIPE · VIDEOCHAT`. Nothing in this section is ever shown on the main site, on the account pages or the account site (decision 108), or in the apps for everyone (Meetings, Citas, Cloud): it lives only on the three adult hosts and in the admin panel.

**What exists and stays.** Videochat and Swipe already show the same public name, the account's `@username` (section 6, "Username"); Cruising has its own nickname (`CruisingProfile.nickname`, `[a-z0-9_]`, with decision 83's 14-day hold). Each app keeps its own entry (date of birth, rules, terms version), block table (`VideochatBlock`, `SwipeBlock`, `CruisingBlock`), reports, suspension fields on its profile and settings. This section adds the shared rules on top of them.

#### Phase 1: shared safety (always on)

- **Blocks are shared (decision 112).** Blocking someone in any of the three apps blocks both people in all three, both ways, at once. Built as a **fan-out** so every app's existing checks keep reading its own table: `AdultSafetyService.block(blockerId, blockedId, sourceApp)` writes the row into the three tables (they reference the account, so the blocked person needs no profile in an app) and runs each app's block side effects, in one transaction: Cruising deletes the conversation and the album grants both ways (check-ins, reviews, meetups and rooms are filtered as now); Swipe ends the match between them (deleting its chat); Videochat deletes both likes (no longer contacts). After the commit an in-process event `adult.blocked { a, b }` tells the video chat gateway, which ends a call, ringing or group invitation between them and drops them from each other's matching. Videochat's report (which blocks) and blocks made from reports in Swipe and Cruising go through the same service. Each table gains `sourceApp AdultApp?` (null on older rows). The blocks list of each app (`/cruising/blocks`, Swipe's and Videochat's settings) shows every block with the app where it was made, naming the person by their name in the current app, or "A member of another 18+ app" when they have none there (never another app's name). **Lifting a block from any app lifts it in all three** (one transaction).
- **One verification (decision 113).** The three verification flows become one, kept on the account: `AdultVerification` (`userId` primary key, `status` NONE · PENDING · APPROVED · REJECTED, `sourceApp`, `verifiedName` (the name on the paper), `photoKey?`, `note?`, `sentAt?`, `decidedAt?`, `decidedById?`, `tries`/`triesSince` (5 photos a day), `withdrawnAt?`, `withdrawnReason?`). The photo shows the person holding a paper with **their name in the app they send it from, "Codeqlick" and the date** (one photo serves the three). It is sent from Cruising's `/me`, Swipe's `/photos` or Videochat's `/settings` (`POST /adult/verification`, multipart `file`, re-encoded to WebP without metadata as now), stored privately in `SWIPE_UPLOADS_DIR/verification/` (mode 0600, out of the backups). **One queue:** the same rows appear in the verification tab of `/admin/cruising`, `/admin/swipe` and `/admin/videochat`; deciding in one decides for all. **APPROVED gives the "Verified" badge in all three apps** (decision 87's wording: "The team checked a photo of this person holding their name: the profile is theirs and they are an adult") and meets Swipe's "verified before uploading" rule. It goes (back to NONE, photo deleted) when the team withdraws it from any admin (on a report or a suspension), when a shared suspension starts (below), or when the name on the paper is no longer any of the account's adult names (its `@username` or its Cruising nickname): decision 83's rule, now for the three. The person is emailed the decision and any withdrawal; the email names "Codeqlick's 18+ apps", never one app. **Retention changes (owner's decision, 2026-11-17):** in all three apps the approved photo is **kept while the verification stands** (a private file only the team sees; Swipe's moderators compare new uploads with it, and Cruising's and Videochat's can on STOLEN or impersonation reports). It is deleted when the verification is withdrawn or lost (team withdrawal, shared suspension, name change), when the account is deleted, or when a new approved photo replaces it; a rejected photo is deleted at once, as now. This changes Cruising (decision 87) and Videochat (4.7), which deleted the photo once reviewed; their privacy policies say so. From this phase the per-app columns (`verificationStatus`, `verificationKey`, `verificationNote`, `verificationSentAt`, `verifiedAt`, Cruising's `verificationTries*`) are no longer read and are dropped one release later; `POST /cruising/verification`, `/swipe/verification` and `/videochat/verification` answer like `POST /adult/verification` for that release. Videochat's `ageVerifiedAt` (a real age check) stays separate: the badge is not age verification.
- **Serious suspensions are shared (decision 114).** Every suspension the team makes now carries a **category**: the report's reason when it acts on a report, or chosen by the moderator when suspending from a person's page (a required select in the three admins). **MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL and ILLEGAL are serious**: the suspension is stored as an `AdultSuspension` (`id`, `userId`, `category`, `sourceApp`, `reportId?`, `reason`, `until?` (null: permanent), `byId`, `createdAt`, `liftedAt?`, `liftedById?`, `liftReason?`) and applies **in the three apps with the same end date**: each app's "is suspended" check becomes its own profile's suspension **or** an active `AdultSuspension`, so it also stops the person entering an app they hadn't used. Starting one ends what the person is doing live (Videochat socket closed with `suspended`, Cruising check-in ended, ringing or live calls of phase 3 ended) and withdraws their verification. Lifting it from any admin lifts it in all three (with a reason, logged). Every other category (HARASSMENT, HATE, SPAM, STOLEN, DANGER, WRONG_INFO, OTHER) stays per app as now. **Automatic suspensions stay per app:** Videochat's at-once suspension after one MINOR report, and after 3 reports in 24 hours, apply only to Videochat until a moderator reviews the report; acting on it with a serious category is what shares it, so one unreviewed (possibly malicious) report can't shut someone out of three apps. Videochat's appeals work on a shared suspension: decided once, applied to the three. The email says "your access to Codeqlick's 18+ apps is suspended until …" with the reason and never names the app where it started.
- **No coming back with a new account (decision 120, owner's decision 2026-11-17).** When an account with an active shared suspension (MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL or ILLEGAL) is deleted, `AdultBannedEmail` keeps only `emailHash` (HMAC-SHA256 of the normalised email: trimmed, lower-cased, and for Gmail addresses without dots and `+tags`; key `ADULT_BAN_HASH_KEY`, a server secret), `category`, `createdAt` and `expiresAt`: the suspension's end, or **2 years** for a permanent one. No name, no account id, no reports. Entering any adult app (the entry of each of the three) with an account whose email matches an unexpired hash is refused with a neutral message ("This account can't use Codeqlick's 18+ apps; contact hello@codeqlick.com"); an email change to a matching address doesn't lift that (it is checked at every entry). The hourly cleanup deletes expired rows. A lifted suspension deletes nothing that isn't there (the account still exists); the team can delete a row from the admin on an accepted appeal by email (`DELETE /admin/adult/banned-emails`, `{ email }`, logged). Legal basis: legitimate interest in members' safety (art. 6.1.f); the privacy policies say so, with the retention.
- **Cross-app history for the team.** The person's page in each admin gains an **"Across the 18+ apps"** panel (`GET /admin/adult/users/:userId`): for each app whether and when the person entered and their name there; reports received by app, status and reason; reports made; suspensions per app and shared (category, source app, dates, by whom); how many people blocked them; the verification and its history; and (phase 4) the linked profile. Report lists in the three admins show "also reported in Swipe · Cruising · Videochat (N open)" next to the reported person. Only admins see it. The team's shared actions go to the activity log with target `adult` (`adult.suspension`, `adult.suspension_lifted`, `adult.verification_decided`, `adult.verification_withdrawn`); members' blocks are not logged, as now.
- **One team digest:** the three apps' digests become one email ("18+ apps: N things waiting") with a section per app and the shared verification queue counted once, same rhythm (every 30 minutes when something is new, at most once an hour). MINOR reports and removal requests still email at once.
- **Migration** (one Prisma migration with SQL, then an idempotent one-off task in the API's background work):
  1. *Blocks:* every row of each block table is copied into the other two (`INSERT … SELECT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `sourceApp` = the table it came from). Then, for every pair now blocked, the side effects run once, in batches of 500: Swipe matches and their chats between them deleted, Cruising conversations and album grants deleted, Videochat likes deleted. Reports and their saved transcripts are kept.
  2. *Verification:* per account, `AdultVerification` is built from the three profiles: any APPROVED wins (the earliest `verifiedAt`, its app as `sourceApp`, its nickname as `verifiedName`; Swipe's kept photo becomes `photoKey`); otherwise the newest PENDING (its photo moved to the shared directory, any other pending photo deleted, nobody emailed); otherwise the newest REJECTED with its note; otherwise NONE. Someone approved in one app and pending in another is approved and their pending photo deleted.
  3. *Suspensions:* existing ones stay per app (no category was recorded). Active ones whose report was MINOR, NON_CONSENSUAL or ILLEGAL are listed once in the admins ("Review for all 18+ apps") so the team can share them or leave them; nothing is shared automatically.
- **Legal texts (decision 119).** `VIDEOCHAT_TERMS_VERSION`, `SWIPE_TERMS_VERSION` and `CRUISING_TERMS_VERSION` move on, so everyone accepts again on their next visit. Each app's terms and privacy policy gain a section **"Safety across Codeqlick's 18+ apps"**: Codeqlick runs three 18+ apps on the same account; a block in one applies in all three; a suspension for minors, non-consent or illegal content applies in all three; one verification gives the badge in all three and its photo is kept privately while it stands (deleted when it is withdrawn or lost, replaced, or the account deleted); after a serious suspension, deleting the account keeps a keyed hash of its email for the suspension's length (2 years if permanent) so the person can't enter again with it; the team sees a person's reports and suspensions in the three when moderating. **Legal basis:** legitimate interest (GDPR art. 6.1.f) in protecting members and preventing abuse; the balance holds because a member reasonably expects someone removed for sexual content involving minors not to stay in a sister app on the same account, only safety measures are shared and only moderators see the history. Because using these apps reveals sex life or sexual orientation (art. 9), that processing also rests on the **explicit consent given at entry** (art. 9.2.a), whose text gains "and that safety measures (blocks, serious suspensions, verification) apply across Codeqlick's 18+ apps"; Videochat's and Swipe's entries, which have no separate art. 9 checkbox today, get one with this phase, like Cruising's. A lawyer should confirm that tying cross-app safety to entry is acceptable for art. 9 data, and review the texts.
- **Privacy:** the account export gains `adult`: the verification (status, dates, app, never the photo), the person's shared suspensions, and each block's `sourceApp`. Deleting the account deletes `AdultVerification` (and its photo), `AdultSuspension` rows and the blocks (every adult table cascades with the user, as the apps' do); only the email hash of decision 120 stays, when it applies.

#### Phase 2: one inbox and one notifications bell

- **One inbox (decision 115).** The messages view of each adult app (Cruising's `/messages`, Swipe's `/matches` and a new `/messages` on Videochat) lists in one list, newest activity first: the member's **Cruising conversations**, **Swipe match chats** and (phase 3) **video calls**, each tagged with its app ("Cruising", "Swipe", "Call"), with the partner's name **in that app**, their verified badge, their picture where that app shows one (Cruising cover, Swipe photo, blurred under `blurPhotos`), the last message's first 80 characters (text only), the unread count and the time. `GET /adult/inbox?cursor=` builds it from the apps' tables through their repositories (no copy of any message). Only apps the member **entered with the current terms** are included, so a member of one app sees what they see now. Opening an item goes to that app's chat on its own host (`https://cruising.codeqlick.com/{locale}/messages/<id>`, `https://swipe.codeqlick.com/{locale}/matches/<id>`): the chats don't move. With single sign-on (decision 108) the jump is seamless; until it ships, the person signs in on that host once (cookies are per host, 6.3). A switch in each app's settings, **"Show my other 18+ apps' messages here"** (`AdultPreferences.unifiedInbox`, default on), turns the merged list off for whoever shares a device. Blocks and suspensions filter as in each app.
- **One notification centre (decision 115).** Cruising's centre (decision 98) becomes the three apps': `CruisingNotification` is renamed `AdultNotification` and gains `app AdultApp?` (existing rows `CRUISING`; null for shared ones). New kinds: Swipe `SWIPE_MATCH`, `SWIPE_LOVE` («me encanta»), `SWIPE_LIKES` (a daily count, grouped like `VISITS`), `SWIPE_MESSAGE` (one per match until read), `SWIPE_PHOTO_DECIDED`; Videochat `VIDEOCHAT_CONTACT` (a new mutual contact), `VIDEOCHAT_MISSED_CALL` (a contact's call nobody answered); shared `CALL_MISSED` (phase 3), `VERIFICATION_DECIDED` (now shared) and `SUSPENSION`. Decision 98's rules stay: `data` holds ids and counts only, never a text or a person; kept 30 days and at most 200 per member across the three; an unread one with the same `groupKey` is updated; written whenever a push or email would be, and also when the member has neither. **The bell** in the `AppHeader` of the three adult apps (decision 107; Swipe and Videochat gain it, for entered members) shows the unread count across the apps the member entered, refreshed every minute and on navigation. `/notifications` on each adult host lists them with the app's tag and absolute links to the right host, a filter per app and "mark all as read". `GET /adult/notifications`, `GET /adult/notifications/unread`, `POST /adult/notifications/read`; `/cruising/notifications` answers the same for one release, then goes. The bell and the list exist **only on the three adult hosts**.
- **Push: one service worker per host stays.** `/cruising-sw.js`, `/swipe-sw.js` and `/videochat-sw.js` keep their hosts and scopes (a worker can't serve another origin). The three subscription tables become `AdultPushSubscription` (`id`, `userId`, `app`, `endpoint` unique, `p256dh`, `auth`, `locale`, `crossApp`, `createdAt`; up to 5 per app and account, deleted on 404/410), filled from the three by the migration (`crossApp` false). **Routing:** an event of app X goes to the member's subscriptions of X; when they have none for X, to their subscriptions on the other adult hosts that turned on **"Also notify me here about my other 18+ apps"** (`crossApp`, per device, default **off**). The payload is `{ title, body, url }` with `url` absolute on X's host; each worker opens it with `clients.openWindow(url)` only when its origin is one of the three adult hosts (an allowlist in the worker; anything else opens the worker's own home). Texts stay as now and reveal nothing: "New message", "New match", "Someone loved one of your photos", "Incoming video call"; the title is the app's short name. Videochat's "someone is waiting" and peak-time pushes stay on Videochat's own subscriptions.
- **Privacy:** the inbox and the centre show members their own data (performance of the contract, art. 6.1.b, within the entry's explicit consent): no new purpose. The export gains `notifications` (30 days, all apps) and the push devices (app and date, never the endpoint). Account deletion cascades.

#### Phase 3: video call from a conversation

- **Where and who (decision 116).** From a **Cruising conversation** or a **Swipe match chat** either person proposes a private 1:1 video call ("Video call" in the chat's menu). The call runs **in Videochat**, at `https://videochat.codeqlick.com/{locale}/call/<callId>`: its host is the only adult host allowed the camera and microphone (6.3), it has the WebRTC room, coturn credentials, report and block, and its rules (no recording, consent on camera) are the ones that fit live video. So **both people must have entered Videochat** (date of birth and its rules, current `VIDEOCHAT_TERMS_VERSION`), and neither may be suspended in Videochat, in the source app or by a shared suspension, nor blocked either way. The button shows for everyone; whoever hasn't entered Videochat is taken to its entry first and then to the call. In Cruising, proposing needs that **both have sent at least one message** in the conversation (no cold calls); a Swipe match is already mutual.
- **Flow.** `POST /adult/calls { app, conversationId }` creates an `AdultCall` (`id`, `app` CRUISING · SWIPE, `conversationId` (the Cruising conversation or the Swipe match; null once deleted), `callerId`, `calleeId`, `status` RINGING · ACCEPTED · DECLINED · MISSED · CANCELLED · ENDED, `createdAt`, `answeredAt?`, `endedAt?`, `durationSec?`, `endReason?`). The callee is told at once: in the chat (its 4 s poll returns `call: { id, from: 'me' | 'them', status }`, showing "Incoming video call" with Accept and Decline), by push "Incoming video call" (nothing else; it opens the chat) and in the notification centre. It **rings 60 s** (then MISSED: `CALL_MISSED` in the callee's centre); the caller can cancel. Accepting (`POST /adult/calls/:id/answer { accept }`) sends both to `/call/<callId>` (the caller's chat follows on its next poll). Someone who must first enter Videochat has 5 more minutes before the call is CANCELLED. The call page opens the video chat socket (ticket as usual) and sends `direct-join { callId }`; the server checks the call (ACCEPTED, the account is a party, under 10 minutes since the answer, both still allowed) and, once both are there, sends `direct-matched { callId, partner, initiator }` (`partner` as in `matched`: name, verified, interests, plus phase 4's `linked`); then `signal`, `chat`, `typing`, `react`, `report` and `block` work as in a 1:1 call. **No next, no queue:** it **ends when either leaves**, closes the tab or stays disconnected for 30 s (`direct-ended { reason: left | lost | blocked | suspended }`), and the page shows "Call ended" with a link back to the chat. One live socket per account still holds: joining a direct call ends whatever the person was doing in the video chat.
- **Safety.** Only the two people (signals relayed only between them; TURN credentials as usual). **Nothing is recorded:** media goes browser to browser or through coturn, the in-call chat isn't stored (except what a report saves), and the contact filter of 4.10 applies to it while it applies to the conversation. No phone number is ever asked for or shown. **Report and block** work during the call and up to 5 minutes after it: a report goes to Videochat's moderation (`VideochatReport` gains `callId?` and `sourceApp`, and keeps the call's last 30 messages), and a block is phase 1's shared block, which ends the call and deletes the conversation. A report or block from the chat itself also ends a ringing or live call. Videochat's automatic suspensions count these reports like any other.
- **Limits:** one RINGING call per conversation; 3 proposals per conversation an hour and 10 per member a day; after a decline the same caller waits 10 minutes before proposing again in that conversation; one live direct call per account. Over a limit: `429` with the wait.
- **History:** calls appear in the inbox ("Video call · 12 min", "Missed call") and in the chat's timeline; `AdultCall` rows are kept **30 days** (an hourly cleanup), deleted with the conversation and with either account; they hold no content. Direct calls stay out of Videochat's anonymous statistics (`VideochatCallLog` measures the random chat); `/admin/videochat/stats` gains a separate daily count of direct calls and their median length.
- **Ads:** never on the call page (Videochat's rule: never on screen with a call).
- **Legal and privacy:** Videochat's terms gain "calls from a Cruising conversation or a Swipe match follow these rules"; Cruising's and Swipe's terms say such calls run in Videochat under its rules. The export lists the member's calls (app, the other person's name in that app at export time, status, times, length).

#### Phase 4: linked profile (optional)

- **Off by default, with explicit consent (decision 117).** A member who entered at least two adult apps can link them from any app's settings ("Link my profiles"): they choose which of their apps to link (two or three) and tick a separate consent: "I want members who also use these apps to see that these profiles are mine: my name and photo will be the same in them and my profile will link to the others. I can unlink at any time." (GDPR art. 6.1.a and, since it reveals sex life or orientation across apps, art. 9.2.a: a new purpose, never bundled with entry.) Stored as `AdultLink` (`userId` primary key, `apps AdultApp[]` (≥ 2), `consentAt`, `consentVersion`, `photoApp?`, `photoId?`, `updatedAt`). **Unlinking** (one app or all) takes effect at once: the links disappear from every payload on the next request (nothing linked is cached: `no-store`).
- **One name, the `@username` as source of truth.** Videochat and Swipe already use the account's username. Linking **Cruising** makes its nickname that same name: the member picks one valid under both rules (3–20 of `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`; no `.`, which Cruising doesn't allow), free as a username (or already theirs) and free as a Cruising nickname (or theirs, or held for them by decision 83). `PUT /adult/link/name` saves `User.username` and `CruisingProfile.nickname` in one transaction (the unique indexes settle a race: `409`). While Cruising is linked, changing the name in any adult app or in My account changes both, under decision 83's rule for both: no limit, no wait, the old name held for its owner 14 days, now also as a username (`CruisingNicknameChange` gains `alsoUsername`; the username check then answers "taken", never why). Unlinking Cruising leaves both names as they are, no longer tied. Linking only Swipe with Videochat changes no name. A name change follows phase 1's verification rule. The consent screen says the Cruising name becomes the Codeqlick username too (shown in the account menu and the admin, never on the public pages of the apps for everyone).
- **One photo.** The member picks one **profile photo** for the link among their LIVE public photos in a linked app (Cruising public photo or Swipe public LIVE photo; never a private-album item or a video): `PUT /adult/link/photo { app, mediaId }`. It shows where a linked app shows a person's picture: Cruising's cover slot (only when the member has no Cruising cover of their own), Swipe's chat header and matches list (cards keep the photo being voted), and Videochat's contacts list, `incoming`/`calling` and the direct call's waiting screen (Videochat shows no photos today). The file is served through its source app's route, whose check grows: the viewer is entered in the app where it is shown and could see the member there (no block, not suspended), the member is still linked and the photo still LIVE. Removing or hiding the photo in its app drops it from the link at once. The account avatar (section 6, "One profile") is never used.
- **Links between profiles.** A viewer looking at a linked member (Cruising profile page; Swipe card, chat and matches list; Videochat `matched`, contacts and direct calls) sees "Also on" links **only when the viewer has linked their own profiles too (reciprocal, owner's decision 2026-11-17), and only to apps that both of them linked and the viewer has entered** (current terms, not suspended there), never when either blocked the other. Why: linking is an exchange, so only people who show their own links see others'; and someone who doesn't use Swipe gains nothing from learning that a person does (data minimisation). A viewer who hasn't linked sees no links and no hint that the person linked. Each link opens: **Cruising** → `/members/<name>`; **Swipe** → `/deck?person=<name>`, the target's LIVE public photos the viewer hasn't voted on (the deck's usual rules except `seeking` and age; never the album); **Videochat** → only when the two are already contacts, `/contacts` with Call; otherwise no Videochat link (Videochat has no profile pages and by design nobody can reach a stranger there). Payloads that name a person gain `linked: { apps: AdultApp[], photo: boolean }` filtered by these rules (empty when nothing applies).
- **Safety:** blocks and suspensions always apply regardless of linking (phase 1). A report about a linked photo, from any app where it shows, goes to its source app's queue with the media reference; the admin panel shows the link state and the photo, and a moderator can remove the photo from the link.
- **Privacy:** the export lists the link (apps, consent date and version, photo reference). Unlinking or deleting the account deletes `AdultLink` at once. The privacy policies describe linked profiles, the consent and how to withdraw it.

#### Phase 5: Swipe and Videochat at Cruising's level

A separate phase (decision 118), done last so its translations cover phases 1–4's texts.

- **Installable app:** Swipe and Videochat already have manifests; they reach decision 96's level: icons 192/512 and maskable, Apple touch icon and meta tags, `start_url` the deck and the room, `display: standalone`, and their workers (`/swipe-sw.js`, `/videochat-sw.js`) also cache an offline page ("You're offline") and the app shell, never personal data (`no-store` responses, images, chats). "Install the app" in their settings when the browser offers it.
- **Verified badge everywhere:** with phase 1 the badge is the account's: Swipe shows it on cards, matches and chats, Videochat in `matched`, contacts, calls and the inbox, Cruising where decision 87 says, always with the same tooltip.
- **Six languages:** Swipe and Videochat serve **French, German, Italian and Portuguese** besides English and Spanish, as Cruising does (decision 103): every page, legal texts, metadata, sitemap and hreflang on their hosts, their emails and push texts; the menu footer lists six languages. `Locale` already has them. Translations are made in the code from the English texts, to be reviewed by native speakers (above all the legal texts). Videochat's `language` filter is unrelated and unchanged.

#### Phases and order

1. **Shared safety**: shared blocks, one verification, shared serious suspensions, cross-app history, one digest, new terms, migration. First: it protects people now, and every later phase relies on it.
2. **One inbox and one bell**: `AdultNotification`, `AdultPushSubscription` and routing, the inbox. Before calls, so a call has somewhere to ring and to be missed.
3. **Video call from a conversation**: `AdultCall`, the `direct-*` socket messages, the call page on Videochat.
4. **Linked profiles**: consent, one name and photo, links. Last of the four: optional, the most sensitive, and built on phases 1 and 2.
5. **PWA and six languages for Swipe and Videochat.**

Each phase is its own pull request (phase 1 may split into API and web) with its Notion row.

### 4.17 Admin overview (`modules/stats`)

Owner's request (2026-11-17): one page in Admin that shows how the whole platform is doing (people, money, activity and health) without opening each app's admin. Decision 122.

- **Where:** `/[locale]/admin/overview`, the first entry of the admin menu ("Overview" / "Resumen"); the dashboard (`/admin`) stays as it is. ADMIN only (the two-step verification rule for admins applies as to every admin route).
- **Weeks:** the last 12 weeks, oldest first, the current one last, Monday to Sunday in Europe/Madrid (`start` is the Monday, `yyyy-mm-dd`), every week present (zeros included); the same bucketing as Cruising's statistics (decision 101), shared code. Totals are counts now.
- **How:** aggregates only, no person, email or name is ever returned; each figure is counted in PostgreSQL with tagged `$queryRaw` templates (values are bound parameters, nothing is built as a string) in one transaction; the answer is kept 10 minutes in the API's memory (`generatedAt` says when it was computed).
- **People:**
  - **Accounts:** people's accounts (role `CLIENT`; the team's `ADMIN` accounts are not counted), all of them and new per week (`users.created_at`).
  - **Active per week:** distinct accounts with a session row created that week (a sign-in, or a refresh of the session, which makes a new row: section 4.3). Session rows are deleted 30 days after they expire (`TokenCleanupService`), so weeks more than about 8 weeks back undercount; the page says so.
  - **Per app:** Cruising, Swipe and Videochat (a profile: the person entered the app), Cloud (member of at least one team), Citas (owns at least one booking page), Meetings (owns at least one room) and the portal's clients (`CLIENT_WHERE` of decision 29, the same rule as the dashboard's "clients", written again in SQL; an e2e test keeps both in step). The total per app now, and new per week: the profile's creation, or the person's first membership, page or room; for clients, the account's creation.
- **Revenue** (from the database only; no Stripe call, so the page works with Stripe down or off; all prices are in euros):
  - **Cloud PRO:** teams whose `subscriptionStatus` is `active`, `trialing` or `past_due` (they are PRO, decision 46).
  - **Maintenance plans** (decision 110): projects whose `maintenanceStatus` is `ACTIVE` or `PAST_DUE` and not marked included, and apart those marked included (they pay nothing).
  - **MRR:** what those subscriptions bill a month before VAT, summed: Cloud PRO's, the maintenance plans' and the total. Stripe's price was only read live (cached an hour), so the amount is now **stored** when the webhook syncs a subscription: `CloudTeam.subscriptionAmountCents` and `ClientProject.maintenanceAmountCents`, `null` until then. It is each item's `price.unit_amount × quantity`, brought to a month (`interval` `month` divided by `interval_count`; `year` ÷ 12; `week` × 52 ÷ 12; `day` × 365 ÷ 12; rounded to the cent). The migration fills the maintenance plans already paid with decision 110's price (1 900 cents); Cloud PRO's price was never written down, so a PRO team synced before this change has no amount until its next subscription event (Stripe sends `customer.subscription.updated` at every renewal, so within a month). Those are counted in `unpriced` and the page says the MRR leaves them out.
  - **Website purchases** (decision 20): `PAID` purchases per week by `paidAt`, their number and the total charged (`amountCents`, VAT included, as Stripe charged it).
- **Activity per week:** leads that came in (`leads.created_at`, every source), support requests opened (`created_at`) and resolved or closed (`resolved_at`), Cloud deployments made (`cloud_deployments.created_at`; rows deleted by Cloud's cleanup are not counted), website checks run (`site_audits.created_at`, section 4.12) and newsletter sign-ups confirmed (`confirmed_at`). Totals now: support requests still open and newsletter subscribers `ACTIVE`.
- **Health** (now):
  - **Disk:** the disk guard's reading (decision 49: `state`, `totalBytes`, `freeBytes`) of the disk holding `CLOUD_ROOT`, or of `UPLOADS_DIR` when Cloud's hosting isn't set up (the same disk on the single server); `null` when it can't be read.
  - **Errors** (section 4.11): open groups (`resolvedAt` null) of the API and the website, and apart the open CSP report groups (source `CSP`, decision 109).
  - **Uptime:** the server monitor (`deploy/monitor/monitor.sh`) keeps no history in the database (only its alert state, on the host), so codeqlick.com's uptime isn't shown; what is stored is the client sites' monitors (`SiteMonitor`): how many are watched and how many are `DOWN` now, linked to Admin → Monitoring.
  - **Moderation waiting:** Cruising (spots `PENDING`, reports `OPEN`, media not reviewed yet, verification photos `PENDING`, removal requests `OPEN`), Swipe (photos `PENDING`, reports `OPEN`, verification photos `PENDING`, removal requests `OPEN`) and Videochat (reports `OPEN`, appeals `OPEN`, verification photos `PENDING`), each linked to its queue (the tab of `/admin/cruising?tab=…` and `/admin/swipe?tab=…`; `/admin/videochat`, whose page holds reports, appeals and verifications).
- **Page:** cards with the totals (accounts, active this week, total MRR, leads this week, open support requests, disk), then sections People, Revenue, Activity and Health. Charts are the server-drawn `BarChart` (SVG `aria-hidden`, the numbers in a visually hidden table), one per weekly series: new and active accounts, new per app, purchases, leads, support opened and closed, deployments, website checks and newsletter sign-ups. The per-app totals, the revenue and the moderation counts are tables. Money is shown with `Intl.NumberFormat` in the page's language.

## 5. API contract (v1)

Base URL: `https://api.codeqlick.com/v1`. JSON in and out. Dates are ISO 8601 strings. Lists are paginated:

```json
{ "items": [ … ], "page": 1, "pageSize": 20, "total": 57, "totalPages": 3 }
```

Query params for lists: `page` (default 1), `pageSize` (default 20, max 100).

### Public

| Method | Path | Body / query | Response |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/health` | | Terminus health result |
| POST | `/leads` | `{ name, email, company?, projectType?, message, locale, website? }` (`projectType`: web, custom, mobile, design, support or not-sure; `budget?` is still accepted from older pages) (`website` is a honeypot and must be empty) | `201 { id }` — limited to 5/min/IP |
| GET | `/indexnow/key` | — | `{ key }` (the IndexNow key, public by design) or 404 while `INDEXNOW_ENABLED` is off |
| GET | `/posts` | `?locale=en&page&pageSize&tag&q` | Paginated `PostSummary` (published only). `q` (max 100 characters) keeps posts whose title, excerpt or content contains it in the requested locale or English, ignoring case and accents (Postgres `unaccent`); `%` and `_` are literal |
| GET | `/posts/tags` | `?locale=en` → `[{ slug, name, count }]`: tags used by live posts, named in `locale`, by name |
| GET | `/posts/redirects/:slug` | (decision 71) an old slug of a post → `{ slug }`, the post's current slug, only while that post is live (published, `publishedAt` ≤ now); `404` otherwise. Declared before `:slug/related`. Default rate limit, like the other public reads |
| GET | `/posts/:slug/related` | `?locale=en&limit=3` (1–6) → `PostSummary[]`: live posts sharing the most tags, newest first among equals, topped up with the latest posts |
| GET | `/posts/:slug` | `?locale=en` | `PostDetail` or 404 |
| GET | `/portfolio` | `?locale=en&page&pageSize&testimonials=true&technology&service` | Paginated `PortfolioSummary` (published only, by `sortOrder`); `testimonials=true` keeps those with a testimonial in `locale`; `technology` (exact name) and `service` (key) filter and combine |
| GET | `/portfolio/filters` | — | `{ technologies: { name, count }[], services: { key, count }[] }` over published case studies, for the list's filter chips |
| GET | `/portfolio/redirects/:slug` | (decision 71) an old slug of a case study → `{ slug }`, its current slug, only while it is live; `404` otherwise. Default rate limit |
| GET | `/portfolio/:slug` | `?locale=en` | `PortfolioDetail` or 404 |
| GET | `/brief` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>` → the key's project's brief (`{ project: { id, name }, status, answers, ... }`) | |
| GET / POST | `/tasks` | `Authorization: Bearer <release key>`. GET `?status=TODO|IN_PROGRESS|DONE|open` → the key's project's `ProjectTask[]` (open first: in progress, to do; then done). POST `{ title, description? }` → `201` task | |
| PATCH | `/tasks/:taskId` | Release key + `{ title?, description?, status? }` → task; `DONE` may resolve its request. Limited to 60/min/IP | |
| GET | `/bookings/slots` | `{ durationMinutes, timeZone: 'Europe/Madrid', slots: string[] }` (ISO instants); `404` until published | |
| GET | `/reviews` | `?locale=en&limit=6` (1–12) → published reviews written in that language (`{ quote, author, role, rating }`), newest first | |
