The main buttons carry `data-umami-event` (`cta-header`, `cta-home-hero`, `cta-home-bottom`, `cta-basic-website-top`, `cta-basic-website-bottom`). Events hold no personal data.
- **Local service pages (owner's request 2026-11-17, decision 121):** pages that answer "service + city" searches ("diseño web Valencia", "software a medida Bilbao") without case studies or reviews, which the owner chose not to show yet.
  - **What and where:** four services, the ones a local business looks for: web design (`web-design`, the basic website offer), custom software (`custom-software`), mobile and PWA (`mobile-and-pwa`) and maintenance (`maintenance-and-support`); the last three reuse the slugs and keys of the service pages. Ten cities, Spain's main markets: `madrid`, `barcelona`, `valencia`, `sevilla`, `malaga`, `bilbao`, `zaragoza`, `alicante`, `murcia`, `palma` (English names Madrid, Barcelona, Valencia, Seville, Málaga, Bilbao, Zaragoza, Alicante, Murcia, Palma). Both lists, with all the per-city copy, live in `src/content/local-seo.ts`; the page's fixed labels in the `LocalSeo` messages (EN/ES). Routes follow the site's single path scheme (English segments, city slugs as the city is written locally, same in both languages): `/{locale}/services/<service>/<city>`, 4 × 10 × 2 = 80 pages, listed by `generateStaticParams` with `dynamicParams = false` (anything else is a 404).
  - **Content, not a template:** each city has its own 2–3 sentences on its business context (what drives its economy and who searches for these services there) and, for each of the four services, one sentence on what that service means for businesses in that city; the intro is both. Then what is included (the service page's list, or the basic website's), the price from (web design: the basic website's "From €200"; the others: the smallest size of the published cost calculator, `estimate(config, { type, size: 'small', extras: [] }).min`, or "quoted per project" while it isn't published or the type isn't priced), the process (four steps per service), FAQ (three per service plus one per city on working remotely), the call to action (web design: the basic website request, and the contact form; the others: contact) and two lists of links: the same service in the other nine cities and the other three services in the same city. Honest by rule: Codeqlick works remotely across Spain, so no office, address, phone or invented clients appear, and the copy says so. A unit test fails when two pages share an intro or a title, or when a city or service is missing a language.
  - **Hubs:** `/services/<service>` lists the ten cities ("Where we work") at the end of the service page; `/services/web-design`, which had no page, is the hub of web design (the offer in short, its price, the cities and a link to `/basic-website`). The services page links the four hubs in a "Near you" section; the header doesn't change.
  - **SEO:** unique title (`<Service> in <City>` / `<Servicio> en <Ciudad>`, plus the price when known) and description per page; canonical and hreflang EN/ES/x-default (`pageMetadata`); `og:image` drawn by `services/[slug]/[city]/og` with `renderOgImage` (eyebrow "Codeqlick · <City>", the title). JSON-LD: `Service` with `areaServed` the city (`City`, in Spain) and `provider` a `ProfessionalService` (Codeqlick, its URL and email, `areaServed` Spain, no address), `offers` with the price from when known; `BreadcrumbList` (Services › service › city); `FAQPage` from the FAQ. The sitemap lists the hub and the 80 pages. IndexNow is not involved: the API announces only posts and case studies, and these pages change only with a release.
- **Updated date:** a post's page shows "Updated on <date>" ("Actualizado el <fecha>") after the publication date when `contentUpdatedAt` is set; its `BlogPosting` JSON-LD carries `dateModified` (`contentUpdatedAt`, or `publishedAt` without one). The sitemap's `lastmod` is `contentUpdatedAt ?? publishedAt` for posts and `contentUpdatedAt` (when set) for case studies, which show no date on their page.
- **llms.txt (for AI assistants):** `/llms.txt` (route handler `src/app/llms.txt/route.ts`, outside `[locale]`; `text/plain; charset=utf-8`; rendered per request because it depends on the host, with its API reads cached for `PUBLIC_REVALIDATE` and `Cache-Control: public, max-age` of the same) is a Markdown map of the website for ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity and similar tools, in the format of llmstxt.org, in English: `# Codeqlick`, a blockquote with the tagline and one sentence on what the studio does, a line saying each page also exists in Spanish under `/es/` and the contact email, then `##` sections of absolute links (`- [Title](url): note`) built from the English messages and routes: **Services** (the services page and each service page), **Basic website** (its page and the request form), **Codeqlick Cloud** (its landing on the cloud host), **Free website check**, **Case studies** (published, the 20 first in the list's order), **Blog** (the 20 latest published posts with their excerpt), **About and contact** (about, contact, security, and legal/privacy only when `LEGAL_OWNER_NAME` is set). Content comes from the same public API calls as the sitemap; when the API fails, the content sections are left out rather than failing the file. Prices and anything behind sign-in are never listed. Only the website's own host answers it: a web app's host gets 404 (its 18+ apps must not be described to assistants).
- **Social preview images:** a post or case study is shared with its cover. Without one, `og:image` points to `/[locale]/blog/[slug]/og` (or `/work/[slug]/og`), a 1200×630 PNG drawn on request with `next/og` from the title, a label (date or client) and the brand mark, in the brand fonts read from `public/og-fonts/` (WOFF). Pages with neither use `/og-image.png`.
- **Linkable headings and table of contents:** Markdown rendered on blog posts, case studies and legal pages (`Markdown` with `anchors`) gives every `h2` and `h3` a stable `id`: the heading's text in lower case, accents removed (NFD, combining marks dropped), anything other than `a-z`, `0-9` collapsed to one `-`, trimmed, `section` when nothing is left; a repeated slug, or one the page layout already uses (`main`, `app-main`, `mobile-nav-panel`, `footer-studio`, `footer-clients`), gets `-2`, `-3`… in order of appearance. Each such heading ends with a small "#" link to its own anchor (`aria-label` "Link to this section" / "Enlace a esta sección"), shown on hover and on keyboard focus, so readers can copy or share the address with no script. Posts and case studies with three or more `h2` show a "Contents" / "Contenido" list before the text: a `nav` with `aria-label`, one item per `h2` with its `h3` nested below, built on the server from the same outline (`lib/markdown-outline.ts`, so ids and links always match); shorter texts and legal pages show none. Headings in the client portal, admin and project updates keep no ids. Targets use `scroll-margin-top` so the sticky header doesn't cover them; smooth scrolling only without `prefers-reduced-motion`. Frontend only: the API and stored Markdown don't change.
- **Copy button on code blocks:** code blocks (fenced or indented) rendered by `Markdown` with `copyCode` (blog posts, case studies and the Cloud docs at `/cloud/docs`) get a small "Copy" / "Copiar" button in their top-right corner. A tiny client component wraps only the `pre` (the Markdown itself stays server-rendered); pressing it copies the block's text (`textContent` of its `code`, as written, trailing newline dropped) with the Clipboard API, and the button reads "Copied" / "Copiado" for 2 seconds, announced through an `output` live region; a refused copy says "Couldn't copy" / "No se pudo copiar". Without the Clipboard API (old browsers, a page not served over HTTPS) the button isn't shown. It is a real `button` (keyboard reachable, visible focus), always visible on touch screens and shown on hover or focus elsewhere; the block gets padding on the right so the button doesn't cover the first line (the longer failure message may, for its 2 seconds). Inline code, the portal, the admin and project updates don't change. Frontend only.
- **Old addresses after a slug change (decision 71):** when `/[locale]/blog/[slug]` gets a 404 from `GET /posts/:slug`, it asks `GET /posts/redirects/:slug`; with an answer it calls `permanentRedirect` (308) to `/[locale]/blog/<current slug>`, keeping the locale, and otherwise shows the usual not-found page. `/[locale]/work/[slug]` does the same with `/portfolio/redirects/:slug`, and so do both `og` route handlers (to the current item's `og`). The sitemap and RSS are unchanged: they list only current slugs. In the admin panel the post and case study edit pages list the item's `oldSlugs` under "Old addresses that redirect here" / "Direcciones antiguas que redirigen aquí", read-only; nothing is shown when there are none.
- **Images:** uploads go through a Server Action to `POST /v1/admin/media`; the stored URL is `SITE_URL/media/<filename>`, which a route handler serves by fetching the file from the API, so the BFF rule also holds for images. Covers that are our own uploads go through `next/image` (`images.localPatterns` allows only `/media/**` and the basic-website demo screenshots in `/demos/**`), so the browser gets a WebP resized to the width it shows; covers at any other URL are shown as they are.
- **No secrets in the client bundle.** Only `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is public. `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` (Turnstile's public site key, section 4.3) is read at request time by the sign-in and sign-up pages and given to the widget.
- **Security headers:** section 6.4.
- **Client portal, what waits on the client:** the portal home starts with "Waiting on you" (from `GET /portal/todo`): each item with a link to where it is done (an update's approval opens the project at `#update-<id>`); nothing is shown when nothing waits. Each project card shows its next milestone and how many items wait on that project. The project page shows the same items for that project at the top, puts the updates before the shared access, and leaves out start and delivery dates that aren't set. Its optional data (tasks, brief, status, access, review, plan, site, hours) is fetched in parallel.
- **Website editor, guidance:** each folded part of the editor's form is flagged "Check" when a field inside has an error (and opens), or "Incomplete" (legal data without tax ID or address); the invalid-draft banner has a button to the first field to fix. The status says whether the saved draft still has to be published or the site is up to date. The client's site page warns about unpublished changes and missing legal data, linking to the editor (`#legal` opens that part).
- **Emails, readability:** web addresses in an email's text are links that may break anywhere, and on phones the card's padding shrinks, so no email is wider than a 375 px screen. A website request's summary (the lead's message and the requester's acknowledgement) is written in the requester's language. A support request's status email says what the new status means for the client ("We need your reply" when it waits on them).
- **Browser tests, wider:** the E2E job also starts the site builder and gives the API `MAIL_OUTBOX_DIR` (honoured only with `NODE_ENV=test`), where it writes every email it sends as JSON; the tests read links from there. They cover signing up with the email confirmation, an admin turning on two-step verification and signing in with a code, a basic-website request and its acknowledgement, and the client's website editor (draft, preview, errors, publishing). Paying with Stripe stays covered by the API's e2e tests, which fake Checkout and sign the webhooks.
- **Accessibility:** semantic HTML, labels on every input, visible focus, 4.5:1 contrast (small fuchsia text on white uses `--color-fuchsia-dark`: the brand fuchsia is 4.3:1), works without JavaScript for reading content.
- **Public UI patterns:** the header marks the visitor's section with `aria-current="page"` (a fuchsia underline; a left bar in the mobile menu); below 480px the header's call to action moves to the end of the mobile menu. Cards that lead somewhere (services, recent work) are clickable as a whole: the link's `::before` covers the card, which lifts on hover and draws the focus ring. Post and case-study cards lift and zoom their cover. Field errors carry a "!" badge so they don't rely on colour alone. Pages of the public site sit in `.site-shell`, so the footer stays at the bottom of short pages. Password fields (`FormInput type="password"`) have a show-password toggle (`aria-pressed`). Blog posts show a reading-progress bar (CSS scroll-driven animation, none without support or with reduced motion). The website request's style cards mark the chosen one with a badge on the thumbnail's corner. On phones the portal and admin bar keep logo and account controls on one row, the tabs below. In the portal, the proposals and support lists (`listTable`) make the whole row the link and, on phones, stack each row with every value under its label; panels space their text from the buttons or form that follow; short forms (account, accepting a proposal) are capped at a readable width (`formNarrow`); "write a comment"-style toggles (`commentForm`) carry a turning chevron, as do the editor's sections. The admin's lists (contacts, posts, portfolio, projects, websites, previews, users, support, errors, proposals, broken links) use the same `listTable`; other links in a row (a site's address, a member's account) stay clickable above the row's link. Admin tabs (Swipe, Videochat and its statistics) are small pill buttons like Errors', the active one filled. Dashboard counters fill six to a row on a laptop and two on phones; a filter's search box gets room for its placeholder. The lead board is the containing block of its cards' visually hidden labels, so on phones it scrolls inside instead of widening the page. Fieldsets that hold a scrolling row (the booking days) set `min-width: 0` so they don't widen the page. Text fields are never under 16px on touch screens (the `mono` variant included), or iOS zooms the page in on focus and the zoom outlives the navigation that follows (the 2FA code used to leave the admin panel zoomed in). Motion uses `--transition-fast` / `--transition-base` and is switched off under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### 6.3 Web apps on subdomains (`lib/webapps.ts`)

Besides client work, Codeqlick runs its own web apps, each on `<app>.codeqlick.com` and served by this same app under `/[locale]/<app>` with the same accounts. `WEBAPPS` lists them (now `videochat`, `meetings`, `citas`, `swipe`, `cruising` and `cloud`). On its host an app's addresses don't repeat its name: `videochat.codeqlick.com/es/room` is the route `/es/videochat/room` (owner's request, 2026-11-08). The proxy, before anything else: on an app's host, `/{locale}/…` is rewritten to the route `/{locale}/<app>/…` (the browser keeps the short address), the sign-in pages (`login`, `register`, `forgot-password`, `reset-password`) are served as they are, the main site's sections (`SITE_SECTIONS` in `lib/webapps.ts`, kept in sync with the folders by a test) and the other apps go to the main host, and the old addresses with the app's name (`/{locale}/<app>/…`) are redirected to the short ones on navigations (images and form posts to them are just served); on the main host, `/{locale}/<app>/…` goes to the app's host without the app's name. In the code links keep the route (`/videochat/room`): each app's layout sets `WebappProvider` on its own host, and the `Link` and `useRouter` of `i18n/navigation` then write the short address. Server-side redirects and sign-in `next` values with the route still work through the redirect. Emails and notifications link to the short addresses. Cookies are per host, so a person signs in on the app's host too. Locally the app opens at `http://videochat.localhost:3000` (browsers resolve `*.localhost`). Requests by IP address (Next.js calling itself) are never redirected. A new app needs its name in `WEBAPPS` (here and in the API's `common/webapps.ts`), its pages and its card on the main site's `/apps` page (links go to `/{locale}/<app>`, which the proxy sends to the app's host). Its subdomain is published by the release itself: after `deploy/deploy.sh` restarts the stack, it runs the root helper `codeqlick-webapp-site` (installed once by `deploy/setup-webapps.sh`, the deploy user may run it with sudo and nothing else) for every app in the API's `WEBAPPS` whose Nginx site has no certificate: DNS `A` record through OVH's API, an Nginx site from the helper's template (pages to the web, `/ws` to the API's `/v1/<app>/ws`) and the certificate (decision 25).

**Apps menu** (owner's choice 2026-11-09): every app's menu drawer (below) ends its sections with "Other Codeqlick apps", listing the other Codeqlick apps with a one-line description, plus "See all apps" (`/{locale}/apps` on the main host); links are absolute (`webappUrl`), each tagged for Umami (`app-switcher`, `from`, `to`). `switcherApps` in `lib/webapps.ts` decides the list: inside an app for everyone (Meetings, Citas) only the apps for everyone; inside an 18+ app (`ADULT_WEBAPPS`: videochat, swipe, cruising) every other app, the 18+ ones last with a "+18" tag. So a booking page or a meeting never points at explicit content. (Until decision 107 it was an "Apps" `<details>` button in the header, `components/layout/AppSwitcher`, now removed.)

**App header and menu** (owner's request 2026-11-17, decision 107): every web app (Cruising, Swipe, Videochat, Citas, Meetings and Cloud, whose console pages share Cloud's layout) has the same header and a menu drawer instead of a row of links.
- **Header** (`components/layout/AppHeader`, a Server Component): the Codeqlick logo (linking to the app's home, as before) with the app's wordmark and, on the 18+ apps, the "18+" badge; on the right only the app's call to action when it has one (Cruising: "Propose a spot", a small button hidden below 40rem), the notification bell where the app has one (Cruising, for entered members, polling as decision 98 says) and the ☰ button, which, signed in, also holds the person's account avatar (32 px; their initials on fuchsia without a photo). Same on desktop and phones.
- **Menu** (`components/layout/AppMenu` with the client part `AppMenuDrawer`): the ☰ button (44 px, accessible name "Menu"/"Menú", or signed in "Menu — <name>"/"Menú — <name>" with the avatar inside the same button, so there is one trigger and one dialog; `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) opens a native `<dialog>` with `showModal()`: it slides in from the right (no motion under `prefers-reduced-motion`), full height, at most 360 px wide (the whole width on narrower phones), with a title and a close button. The browser makes the page behind inert and closes on Escape; a click on the backdrop and the close button close it too; on opening the focus goes to the close button, on closing back to the ☰ button; the page behind doesn't scroll while it is open (the root's `overflow` is hidden); following any link in it (or a navigation for any other reason) closes it. Links are 44 px tall, with the brand's focus ring, and the current page's link carries `aria-current="page"` (a fuchsia bar), worked out in the browser from the path, in either form (`/cruising/map` on the main host, `/map` on the app's host), the app's home only when it is the page itself, any other link also for the pages under it (`/cruising/messages/<id>` marks Messages).
- **User card** (owner's feedback 2026-11-17: "opening Cruising or Swipe, I don't see my profile in the menu"): the drawer opens with a card. Signed in it shows the account's avatar, name, `@username` when set and, on Cruising, Swipe and Videochat, a "Verified" badge when that app's verification was approved (read from the app's status, which the layout already asks for or asks once more); then two 44 px buttons: **"My profile"**, the app's own profile, and **"My account"**, the account's settings. On the 18+ apps a person who hasn't entered yet (or has no profile there) sees **"Complete your profile"** instead, leading to the app's entry screen. Meetings and Citas have no profile of their own: only "My account". "My account" comes from one helper (`accountSettingsHref` in `lib/app-menus.ts`): the account site (`https://account.<site>/<locale>`, decision 108) once it is switched on with `ACCOUNT_SSO=true`, until then the website's `/<locale>/portal/account` (`/admin/account` for the team; English for Cruising's other languages). Signed out the card holds "Sign in" (`/login?next=` the page the app's own sign-in button already uses: Cruising the map, Swipe the deck, Videochat the room, Meetings and Citas their home, Cloud the console) and "Create account" (`/register`).

  | App | "My profile" | Before entering ("Complete your profile") |
  |---|---|---|
  | Cruising | `/cruising/me` | `/cruising/me` (shows the entry step) |
  | Swipe | `/swipe/photos` (profile, verification, photos) | `/swipe/deck` (entry step and profile) |
  | Videochat | `/videochat/settings` | `/videochat/room` (entry step and profile) |
  | Cloud | "Console", `/cloud/console` (my teams) | — |
  | Meetings, Citas | — (only "My account") | — |
- **Contents**: the user card, grouped sections under small uppercase headings, then "Other Codeqlick apps" (above), then a footer with the app's rules (where it has them), the language switcher (six languages on Cruising as a wrapped list of names, EN | ES elsewhere) and, signed in, "Sign out" (the same form as before); signing in is only on the card. Each app's configuration is a pure builder in `lib/app-menus.ts` (unit-tested), fed by the layout with the same state as before (signed in; on Cruising, Swipe and Videochat also entered, which only changes the card's profile button on the last two), so who sees which link doesn't change. Labels are message keys resolved on the server; section headings live in the `AppMenu` namespace (translated in Cruising's six languages).

| App | Sections (members-only items in brackets) | Footer |
|---|---|---|
| Cruising | Discover: Map, Explore places, [Meetups], Health; Community [entered only]: Messages, City chats, Favourites, Trips, Notifications; Me: [My spots, signed in], Propose a spot (the profile is on the card) | Rules, languages, sign out |
| Swipe | Swipe [signed in]: Swipe, Matches, My photos | Rules, EN/ES, sign out |
| Videochat | Videochat: Home, [Contacts], [Your profile] | Rules, EN/ES, sign out |
| Meetings | Meetings: Home | EN/ES, sign out |
| Citas | Citas: Home | EN/ES, sign out |
| Cloud | Cloud: Home, Docs (external when `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` is set), [Console] | EN/ES, sign out |

A section with no visible item is left out.

**The 18+ apps together** (section 4.16, decisions 112–120): on the three hosts of `ADULT_WEBAPPS` only, the header's bell (Swipe and Videochat gain it in phase 2) counts the shared notification centre, the messages view lists the inbox of the three apps with an app tag and absolute links to each app's host, and the menu of Swipe and Videochat gains Messages and Notifications; a direct video call opens `/call/<id>` on Videochat's host (`/[locale]/videochat/call/[id]`, `PROTECTED_PATH`, entry first when needed). Settings pages of the three gain the shared sections: verification (one for the three), blocks (with their app), "Show my other 18+ apps' messages here", "Also notify me here about my other 18+ apps" and, in phase 4, "Link my profiles". None of this is rendered on any other host, and `switcherApps` keeps never pointing the apps for everyone at an adult one.

**Video chat** (`/[locale]/videochat`, section 4.7): its own layout in the website's light colours (no site header or footer) and `noindex`. `/` is the landing with the adult-content warning and the way in; `/rules` the rules people accept; `/room` (protected like the portal) shows the suspension notice, the 18+/rules form, or the room. The room (`components/videochat/VideochatRoom`, a Client Component) turns on the camera, gets a ticket through a Server Action, opens the WebSocket (`VIDEOCHAT_WS_URL`, which the build also adds to the CSP's `connect-src`, else `wss://<host>/ws` in production and the local API in development) and runs WebRTC with the partner: next, stop, mute, camera off, text chat, report (reason and details, in a `<dialog>`) and block. Every page on the video chat's host allows `camera=(self), microphone=(self)` (moving between pages keeps the first page's policy, so the room alone would not be enough); every other host denies them. Admin → Videochat lists who is online and the reports (open, acted on, dismissed) with their messages, to dismiss or suspend, and the suspended accounts, to lift. Full product (section 4.7): `/settings` edits the public profile and filters and turns notifications on or off for the device (`components/videochat/PushToggle`); the landing offers to install the app (`components/videochat/InstallApp`), and `app/manifest.ts` returns the video chat's manifest on its host; the suspension notice shows the reason, the end date and an appeal form (then its status and answer); Admin → Videochat adds the open appeals (accept or reject with an answer) and a page per person (`/admin/videochat/users/[id]`: profile, status, reports received and made, sanctions, appeals; suspend for a chosen time, lift, age verified).

**Meetings** (`/[locale]/meetings`, section 4.8): its own layout in the light colours, indexable landing. `/` explains it, takes a code or a pasted link ("Join a meeting") and, for a signed-in person, lists their rooms (create, copy link, waiting room on/off, delete). `/<code>` (`noindex`) shows "this room doesn't exist" or the room (`components/meetings/MeetingRoom`, a Client Component): a camera preview with mute and camera toggles and the name (guests) or "you'll join as …"; then the waiting room or the meeting: a grid of tiles (1, 2, then 3 columns; on phones 1 for two people, else 2), mute, camera, share screen (computers), chat and people panels (the owner's knocks, remove, waiting room switch, end for everyone), copy link and leave; and a closing card for each way out (left, declined, removed, ended, full, gone, connection lost). The ticket comes from a Server Action (signed in: the account's ticket; otherwise, or when the session ran out, a guest's). The socket is `MEETINGS_WS_URL` (added to the CSP like the video chat's), else `wss://<host>/ws` in production and the local API in development. The proxy refreshes an expiring session on `/meetings` but never sends anyone to sign in (`OPTIONAL_SESSION_PATH`). The hosts of both calling apps allow `camera`, `microphone` and `display-capture` for themselves.

**Citas** (`/[locale]/citas`, section 4.9): the Meetings layout and colours. `/` explains it and, for a signed-in person, lists their pages (copy link, view, edit, pause, delete) and their upcoming or past (`?scope=past`) appointments with cancel. `/editar/<id|nueva>` (signed in, `PROTECTED_PATH`) is the page editor; hours are typed per weekday as `09:00-13:00, 16:00-19:00`. `/<slug>` (`noindex`) is the public page: days with free times, then the times in the page's zone (and the visitor's own when it differs), then name, email, phone and note. `/cancelar?token=` shows the appointment and cancels it. The proxy refreshes sessions on `/citas` without requiring one (`OPTIONAL_SESSION_PATH`).

**Swipe** (`/[locale]/swipe`, section 4.10): its own light layout with the Codeqlick logo and the `swipe` wordmark, public pages indexed (section 4.10), listed on `/apps` with an 18+ warning. `/` is the 18+ landing (warning, how it works, safety), `/rules` the rules, `/takedown` the public removal form. `/deck`, `/photos`, `/matches` and `/matches/<id>` need a session (`PROTECTED_PATH`) and show the onboarding (date of birth + rules) or the suspension first. The deck drags cards with the pointer (100 px votes) or the arrow keys, with like/skip buttons, report and the match banner; `/photos` holds the profile, the verification photo and the photo grid (uploads are shrunk in the browser); the chat polls every 4 s. Images go through `/{locale}/swipe/img/<id|verification>`, a route handler that streams them from the API with the session's token. Moderation is `/admin/swipe` (tabs: to review, reports, removals, live, suspended, hidden); its images go through `/{locale}/admin/swipe/img/<kind>/<id>` on the main host.

**Cruising** (`/[locale]/cruising`, section 4.15): its own light layout with the Codeqlick logo and the `cruising` wordmark, listed on `/apps` with an 18+ warning. Indexed (canonical and hreflang on its host, `rating: adult`, its own sitemap from `GET /cruising/sitemap`): `/` (the 18+ landing: warning, how it works, safety, the way in), `/map`, `/spots/<slug>`, `/places`, `/places/<country>` and `/places/<country>/<city>` (lists of LIVE spots), `/rules`, `/takedown` (the public removal form), `/terms-of-use` and `/privacy-policy`. `/new` (propose a spot; `PROTECTED_PATH`, shows the onboarding (date of birth, rules, consent) or the suspension first; `?lat=&lng=` prefills the coordinates, which can also be typed) is not indexed. Phase 2: `/me` (profile), `/members/<nickname>`, `/messages` and `/messages/<id>` (the chat polls every 4 s), all `PROTECTED_PATH`. `robots.txt` keeps out `/new`, `/me`, `/members`, `/messages` and the sign-in pages (which also send `noindex`). `/map` (`components/cruising/CruisingMap`, a Client Component) holds the Leaflet map, loaded by dynamic import with `ssr: false`, and Leaflet is loaded only on `/map` and on spot pages (their small location map). The map asks a route handler (`/{locale}/cruising/api/spots?bbox=`, a BFF: the browser never calls the API) for the spots in view when it stops moving; "locate me" centres the map in the browser and sends nothing. Moderation is `/admin/cruising` on the main host (tabs: spots to review, reports, removals, live spots (with a LIVE / HIDDEN / REJECTED filter), suspended; phase 4 adds "New media" (new / hidden / all) and "Verifications"). Phase 5: `/meetups` (upcoming meetups at my favourites, in my country and my trips' countries, and the ones I host or go to) and `/trips` (my trip cards), both `PROTECTED_PATH` and kept out by `robots.txt`; the spot page adds, for entered members, its safety alerts (post, "still there", "gone", report), presence and meetups (plan, "I'm going" with the anonymous choice, "not going", the host's cancel, report); the city page adds the city's meetups, "Visiting soon" (a "Message" button opens the conversation) and "I'm visiting <city>" (the trip form); the map adds a warning badge on spots with active alerts through the route handler `/{locale}/cruising/api/alerts` (whole grid cells only, like `/api/active`). Times of meetups are shown in the reader's time zone (the browser rewrites the server's UTC). Phase 4: the profile page shows the member's gallery (`components/cruising/MemberGallery`) and `/me` the verification and the media manager; files go through the route handlers `/{locale}/cruising/media/<id>` and `/<id>/poster` (BFF, `Range` forwarded, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`; on the Cruising host `/{locale}/media/<id>`, kept out by `robots.txt`) and, for the team, `/{locale}/admin/cruising/media/<file|poster|verification>/<id>`. Uploads go to `POST /{locale}/cruising/api/media`, a route handler that streams the multipart body to the API without holding it; it is left out of `src/proxy.ts`'s matcher (the proxy buffers a body up to `proxyClientMaxBodySize` and cuts the rest), so it checks the `Origin` itself, and Nginx allows it 100 MB.

**Cloud** (`/[locale]/cloud`, section 4.13): the console at cloud.codeqlick.com, in the website's light colours with the `cloud` wordmark; listed on `/apps` under "For developers". `/` (indexed) explains Codeqlick Cloud and the free plan; `/docs` (indexed) is the documentation (when `CLOUD_DOCS_URL` is set it redirects with `307` to the public documentation site, section 4.13): getting started, the CLI, deployments, previews and downloading one, single-page apps (`200.html`), custom domains, limits, CI (GitHub Actions with `CODEQLICK_TOKEN`) and the HTTP API; `/terms-of-use` the Cloud terms (`/terms` is the main site's); `/report` the public abuse form; `/status/<team>` a team's status page, with the email sign-up when the team has it on, and `/status-updates/confirm` and `/status-updates/unsubscribe` (not indexed) for its subscribers (decision 105). Signed in (every console page first asks for the current Cloud terms when the person hasn't accepted them, 4.13 Terms; `PROTECTED_PATH`): `/console` (my teams, or creating the first one with the terms), `/console/<team>` (projects and usage), `/console/<team>/<project>` (deployments with their addresses, the branch address on the row of a branch's newest preview, a "Kept" badge, keep / stop keeping, promote, download as `tar.gz` and delete; domains with the DNS records to add and "Check now"; settings: rename, "Move to another team" (owners), delete), `/console/<team>/members` (members, roles, invitations), `/console/<team>/events`, `/console/tokens` (my tokens, read-only ones with a "Read only" badge; the "New token" form has an "Access" choice: Full, "deploys and changes projects, deployments and domains", or Read only, "can only list and read; anything else is refused"; a new one is shown once, with a copy button), `/cli?code=` (approve a CLI sign-in) and `/invite?token=` (join a team). Phase 1b: the project's "Forms" tab (forms with their HTML snippet and a "Spam filter" switch (ADMIN); submissions in two views, "Inbox" and "Spam (n)", with search, CSV and delete, a "Spam" button on inbox rows and "Not spam" on spam rows, which show the reason, and a note that spam is kept 30 days and isn't emailed or counted), `/console/<team>/monitors` (list with state and 24-hour sparkline and a "Slow" badge while an episode lasts; the HTTP form's optional "Alert when slower than (ms)" field (3 checks in a row, about 15 minutes) on creation, and an admin-only card on the monitor page to change or clear it; a monitor's page with uptime bars, checks and incidents, the threshold (a dashed line on the response-time chart if there is one, text otherwise) and since when it is slow; heartbeats show their URL and a curl example), `/console/<team>/status-page` (settings and preview, and a "Notices" section for incidents and scheduled maintenance), `/console/<team>/audits` (run a website check, recent ones, a report page), and the public `/status/<team>` (indexed when enabled, with open notices on top). The public `/analytics/<token>` is a project's shared analytics report (read-only, `noindex`, no team or console links; 4.13). Phase 2: the project's **Git** and **Builds** tabs, a build's page `/console/<team>/<project>/builds/<number>` (live log; the preview check's score linking to `/console/<team>/audits/<id>`), and `/github/setup` (GitHub's return after installing the app). Usage alerts: every team page shows a warning banner when a limit is at 80 % or more of its plan (error-styled at 100 %) with a link to `/console/<team>`, from `meters` of the usage endpoint. Admin → Cloud on the main host: teams, projects (suspend, lift) and abuse reports.

### 6.4 Security headers and Content Security Policy (decision 109)

Owner's request (2026-11-17): the same protective headers on every host the website serves (codeqlick.com, every host of `WEBAPPS` (6.3), staging), built in one place, `src/lib/security-headers.ts` (pure functions, unit-tested per host). The host is recognised by its first label (`webappForHost`), so a new entry of `WEBAPPS` gets the defaults without touching this module. Managed client sites (8.2) and Cloud's hosting addresses (4.13) keep their own rules. Nginx adds none of these headers: the website and the API send them, so no installed (certbot-edited) site needs a change.

**Content-Security-Policy.** Production only (`next dev` needs `eval` and its own socket). For every request it handles, `src/proxy.ts` makes a nonce (16 random bytes, base64), puts the strict policy with it in the *request's* `Content-Security-Policy` header and the nonce in `x-nonce` (Next.js reads the first and adds the nonce to its own scripts and inline bootstrap; `components/layout/Analytics` reads the second for Umami's tag), and answers it. The strict policy, by host:

| Directive | Every host | By host |
|---|---|---|
| `default-src` | `'self'` | |
| `script-src` | `'self' 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` (no `'unsafe-inline'`, no `'unsafe-eval'`; browsers with CSP 3 ignore `'self'` and hosts next to `'strict-dynamic'`, and run what a trusted script adds: Next.js chunks, Leaflet, Turnstile's script, ExoClick's ad tag) | video chat and Swipe: ExoClick's hosts (4.7) |
| `script-src-attr` | (falls back to `script-src`: no inline event handlers) | video chat and Swipe: `'unsafe-inline'`, because ExoClick's tag puts its click handlers in attributes (`onclick`), which the previous policy allowed; `<script>` elements still need the nonce there. Its `eval` and its video ads' CDN stay blocked, as before |
| `style-src` | `'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (the inlined CSS and the `style` attributes of React and Leaflet; styles can't run code) | |
| `img-src` | `'self' data: blob:` | main host (posts and case studies may show remote images) and the hosts with ads: `https:` too; Cruising: `https://tile.openstreetmap.org` too |
| `font-src` | `'self'` | |
| `media-src` | `'self' blob:` (Cruising reads a chosen video's length from a `blob:`) | |
| `connect-src` | `'self' https://challenges.cloudflare.com`, plus `VIDEOCHAT_WS_URL` and `MEETINGS_WS_URL` when they are on another origin | video chat and Swipe: ExoClick's hosts |
| `frame-src` | `'self' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` | video chat and Swipe: ExoClick's hosts |
| `worker-src` / `manifest-src` | `'self'` (service workers would otherwise fall back to `script-src`, where `'strict-dynamic'` ignores `'self'`) | |
| `object-src` / `base-uri` | `'none'` / `'self'` | |
| `frame-ancestors` | `'none'`: no host frames its own pages (the website editor frames `/site-draft/*`, which keeps its own `sandbox` policy and gets none of these headers) | |
| `form-action` | `'self'`, the main host and every app host (signing in and redirects between hosts), `https://checkout.stripe.com https://billing.stripe.com` (Checkout and the customer portal, where Server Actions redirect) and `https://github.com` (installing the GitHub App): a form posted before the page hydrates follows these redirects | |
| `upgrade-insecure-requests` | over HTTPS only (a local production build on `http://localhost` would break) | |
| Reports | `report-uri /api/csp-report; report-to csp`, with `Reporting-Endpoints: csp="https://<host>/api/csp-report"` | |

**Rollout, `CSP_ENFORCE`** (the website's environment, read at request time, default `false`): off, the previous policy (scripts from the site with `'unsafe-inline'`, the same hosts as above, `frame-ancestors 'none'`) stays enforced as `Content-Security-Policy` and the strict one goes as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, so nothing can break and every would-be violation is reported (4.11). On, the strict one is `Content-Security-Policy` and the previous one is gone. Staging runs with it on by default (`docker-compose.staging.yml`), production with it off. CI's browser flows run with `CSP_ENFORCE=true` and fail on any violation (`e2e/csp.spec.ts` visits the main pages of every host and listens for `securitypolicyviolation`). Production switches it on once the reports in Admin → Errors after a week of traffic show only noise (extensions, ad networks), after checking by hand on every host: the map's tiles (Cruising), Turnstile on sign-in and sign-up, a video call and a meeting (WebRTC, screen sharing), Stripe Checkout from a preview, a passkey sign-in, an upload (Swipe, Cruising) and the service workers (video chat, Cruising).

**Cost:** a nonce must be new on every response, so every page renders per request (the `[locale]` layout reads `x-nonce`); nothing is prerendered as static HTML any more. Public data is still read through Next.js's data cache (`revalidate`), so the API gets no more requests; only the web server renders more. Paths the proxy doesn't handle (files with an extension, `/api/*`, `/_next/*`, `/site-draft/*`) get no CSP: they aren't pages.

**The other headers** (`next.config.ts`, every path but `/site-draft/*`; the values come from the same module):

| Header | Value |
|---|---|
| `Strict-Transport-Security` | `max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains` (production). Every name under codeqlick.com that a browser opens is HTTPS; `mail`, `imap`, `smtp`, `pop3`, `autoconfig` and `autodiscover` are OVH's mail names, which mail apps reach without HSTS. No `preload`: it is hard to undo, so it waits for the owner's decision |
| `X-Content-Type-Options` | `nosniff` |
| `Referrer-Policy` | `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` |
| `X-Frame-Options` | `DENY` (the same as `frame-ancestors 'none'`, for older browsers) |
| `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` | `same-origin`: no page needs a window it opened or that opened it (Stripe is a redirect, Turnstile a frame, passkeys a browser dialog) |
| `Permissions-Policy` | `accelerometer=(), camera=(), display-capture=(), geolocation=(), gyroscope=(), magnetometer=(), microphone=(), payment=(), usb=(), publickey-credentials-create=(self), publickey-credentials-get=(self)`; the video chat's and Meetings' hosts allow `camera=(self), microphone=(self), display-capture=(self)` on every page (moving between pages keeps the first page's policy), Cruising's `geolocation=(self)` |

**Reports:** `/api/csp-report` (a route handler, outside the proxy, so a report never refreshes a session) reads at most 64 KB of what the browser sends (`application/csp-report` or `application/reports+json`), forwards it as JSON to the API's `POST /csp-reports` with the visitor's IP (for the rate limit) and user agent, and always answers `204`. What the API keeps: section 4.11.

**API** (`app.setup.ts`, helmet): `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` in production (the result pages of the form endpoints style themselves inline; routes that serve a fuller page set their own: Cloud's password page, the drafts' `sandbox`; outside production none, for Swagger UI), `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` (the routes other sites load set `cross-origin`: Cloud's analytics script, badges, key-value reads) and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.

**security.txt and the security page:** section 6.2.

| Token | Value |
|---|---|
| `--color-fuchsia` | `#D9177E` (primary; white text on it passes WCAG AA at 4.8:1) |
| `--color-fuchsia-dark` | `#A80F5E` (hover, eyebrows on light backgrounds) |
| `--color-fuchsia-light` | `#FF7DBE` (accents on dark backgrounds) |
| `--color-ink` | `#14101F` (text, dark sections) |
